# OpenZCAD master roadmap

**Single source of truth for priorities, dependencies and delivery status.**
Consolidated 2026-09-12 against OpenZCAD `main` at `28d1551f` (PR #315) and
reconciled 2026-09-17 against `main` at `3d7fdc33` (#316–#356). Canonical
documents use schema v33 (I01 linked and unlinked sketch projections, I05 reference images, D02 drawing dimensions and notes, D01 drawing sheets, F01 extrude to-face extents and draft, R01 datum axes with revolve, circular-pattern and Move consumers, I01 sketch ellipses and splines, S04 sketch composites, R01 three-point and parallel-at-point datum planes, F01/M11 extrude intersect and keep-originals, M10 transform copies and pivots, F05 typed parameters, F01 through-all cuts, R01 datum planes, S07 symmetric/point-on-curve/lock relations, reference-form mesh payloads and versioned canonical sketch bases, retaining v15
on/off parameters with body visibility bindings).
Kernel snapshot refreshed 2026-10-21 against OpenZCAD `main` at `afedeb86`:
the paired `remus-wasm` and `remus-wasm-io` packages are version `2026.1.71`,
pinned to immutable Remus commit `b464097112a6a25878666740442a46eb54b0ed7a`
in [the adapter manifest](packages/kernel-adapter/package.json) and
[pnpm-lock.yaml](pnpm-lock.yaml). This source snapshot is not a fresh
qualification of upstream Remus `main` or a production-site acceptance report.

Revised 2026-09-26 against a feature inventory of a mainstream direct-modeling
CAD ([comparison record](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md)); that pass added
U05–U07, S07, S08, F05, M10–M13, I04, I05, L08 and L09 and named slices inside
existing rows. It changed no delivery status.

Expanded 2026-09-29 with a file-interoperability scope: I06–I20 add format
qualification and native-CAD translation; D01/D04, I02 and I04 retain their
existing PDF, DXF, STEP-fidelity and export-option ownership. The
[format specification](docs/plans/file-format-interoperability.md) defines
directions, priorities and acceptance; these are planned capabilities.

Reviewed 2026-09-30 through the installed FreeCAD 1.1.3 interface
([hands-on comparison](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md)).
This pass sharpens existing sketch, expression, reference, drawing and assembly
acceptance; it adds no delivery claim or new specialist-workbench lane.

Reviewed 2026-10-14 in a full project review
([record](docs/reviews/project-review-2026-10-14.md)): 39 confirmed bugs and 16
improvements across persistence, sharing, import, the kernel boundary, the
viewport and tooling. Its fix PRs record their evidence in the rows they touch;
the remaining findings are assigned to the master rows below; this evidence update changes no delivery status.

Start here to choose work. The documents in [Supporting plans](#supporting-plans)
retain specifications, acceptance details and historical evidence; their old
phase numbers, priorities and status tables no longer define independent queues.
ADRs retain architecture decisions. Update delivery status here, and update a
supporting specification only when its contract changes.

## Where we are

OpenZCAD has a working browser CAD foundation: exact solid modeling, editable
feature history, expressions, direct manipulation, local persistence, optional
cloud sync and collaboration. The largest remaining product gaps are complete
sketch constraint workflows, reference geometry, drawings and assemblies.
Recent work improved existing modeling and the bounded imported-holder workflow,
and the 14 September kernel-adoption wave (#330–#342) moved booleans, patterns,
blends, mesh import, sketch modify tools and sections onto typed kernel entry
points; it did not complete those larger milestones.
The 26 September comparison with a mainstream direct-modeling CAD confirmed
those four gaps and added a fifth that needs no kernel work: the workspace
conventions every mainstream tool shares (a full hotkey set, saved views, item
groups, history step actions, notable-point measurement picks, X-ray display). It also
recorded that the reference CAD ships drawings, visualization and share links
with no assemblies at all, which supports the milestone order below.
The FreeCAD walkthrough reinforces the same sequence: actionable sketch feedback,
coherent expression/feature editing and a dimensioned drawing handoff, then
component instances and grounding before joint solving. Keep the quiet-stage
workspace; breadth of a reference CAD is not a reason to copy its toolbar density.

**Status vocabulary:** `Open` means a defined remainder; `Partial` means a
bounded subset is delivered; `Revalidate` means the older finding lacks current
proof and must be reproduced before implementation; `Deferred` means deliberately
outside the next delivery sequence. No feature row below is claimed actively
owned without a linked open implementation PR. “Delivered” means merged source
with referenced evidence; production acceptance is recorded separately.

## Delivery order

Finish useful workflows before adding a second way to do the same action.
Use the ordered product milestones below; kernel/reliability work runs alongside
only where dependencies and touched files are disjoint. Each row is an outcome,
not necessarily one PR. Split a large row into named, bounded slices under the
same ID before coding; never treat a partial slice as the whole row's completion.

| Order | Milestone                            | Exit signal                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ----- | ------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 0     | Close the shipped workflow gaps      | H01 has an explicit acceptance matrix; H02 has measured cold/warm behavior; U01/U02 resolve reproduced command/value/feedback disagreements; L01 records outstanding live checks.                                                                        |
| 1     | Complete the modeling foundation     | S01–S08, R01, F01–F05 and U05–U07 meet their row contracts: durable dimensions, understandable constraints, editable sketch geometry, useful reference/parameter surfaces, and the keyboard, history/items and viewing conventions of a mainstream tool. |
| 2     | Deepen part modeling and inspection  | Selected M01–M13, I01, I04–I08 slices work through creation, revision and export with their kernel prerequisites proved.                                                                                                                                 |
| 3     | Drawings MVP                         | D01 then a bounded D02 slice: aligned scaled views plus a useful dimension, explicit revision update/invalidation, offline reopen and PDF handoff. D03/D04 add sections and DXF; the milestone is a dimensioned shop handoff, not outlines alone.        |
| 4     | Assemblies MVP                       | AS01 then AS02/AS03; AS04/AS05 follow only when their query, exchange and reference dependencies qualify.                                                                                                                                                |
| Later | Reach, automation and new body types | Select L02–L09, D05, K09, M12 or I09–I20 from a measured use case; do not expand every lane at once.                                                                                                                                                     |

**Next bounded picks, in order (reviewed 2026-10-21):** [H02](#h02)
remaining sustained publication-equivalence, branch lifetime, quiet target-device
timing and whole-worker retention evidence; [H01](#h01) remaining live acceptance from its matrix; then
[S02](#s02) remaining reference-distance variants and qualified composite/datum
targets, and [S04](#s04) shared composite target identity and explicit
legacy conversion (follow-ups #800/#801 are in review). [S03](#s03) actionable constraint inspection and first-picked
anchoring are delivered; per-entity freedom still requires qualified solver
evidence. [R01](#r01) offset/point planes and axes and their AI operations are
delivered; qualify the vertex-source recovery slice (#802) before adding the
remaining reference constructors. [U05](#u05)
keyboard/command coherence and the [F05](#f05) expression-field regression audit
can run independently; use their current row evidence to select a remainder. [S01](#s01) saved driving-label placement is complete. Current
open implementations already own U05/U06 search and folders (#780), I01
projection (#781) and I05 reference images (#782); refresh their heads and
ownership before assigning overlapping work. U01/U02 and L01 can
proceed when ownership permits. Live acceptance needing credentials/source data
stays pending without blocking independent foundation work. This sequence
replaces the conflicting next lists in older plans.

These milestones are not a global gate: independent foundation work can run
while live acceptance is pending, and drawing/assembly design can start before
every part-modeling row is complete. No calendar estimates are carried forward
from audits of earlier interfaces.

All five local gates and the unchanged Linux private 80-edit session pass at `3e9814ae`; fresh quiet M5, whole-worker retention and detached Text acceptance remain open. 21 October [unavailable-probe follow-up](docs/qa/2026-10-21/h02-lineage-runtime-candidate.md): finalized mode-independent lineage proofs preserve eager-to-idle checkpoint reuse under the existing byte ceiling. Four new public regressions, all five local gates and the private 80-edit lifecycle probe pass at `08393cb4`; its own quiet M5 timing remains required. 21 October [fresh M5 follow-up at `64f07065`](docs/qa/2026-10-21/h02-64f07065-m5-qualification.md): all six alternating arms pass fixed Text restore; recorded-load exact-completion p95 is 3,442.9 → 2,487.1 ms (27.8% lower, unchanged ≥20% gate met). The private 80-edit session passes all seven raw/publication and four eviction comparisons, three replay-budget cycles, retirement, refusal/recovery and actual worker restart; all five local gates pass at that clean head. Quiet-host and whole-worker retention acceptance remain open; the proposed detached Text cache is unimplemented and unaccepted. 21 October [runtime follow-up](docs/qa/2026-10-21/h02-lineage-runtime-candidate.md): the Linux private 80-edit session passes all seven raw/publication comparisons, four eviction comparisons, three replay-budget cycles, retirement, refusal/recovery and worker restart. An enabled arm restores fixed Text under unchanged budgets; fresh M5 matched timing, whole-worker retention and detached Text acceptance remain open. 21 October [PR #805 M5 qualification](docs/qa/2026-10-21/h02-pr805-m5-pro-qualification.md): private raw/publication oracles match through 80 edits, recovery and restart, but candidate p95 improves only 8.3% (target ≥20%) and all enabled arms fail fixed Text restore. Fresh-arena fallback leaves private checkpoint retirement/replay-budget coverage unmet; whole-worker retention, quiet timing and detached Text acceptance remain open. 21 October [M5 Pro qualification](docs/qa/2026-10-21/h02-m5-pro-qualification.md): all six alternating matched arms pass the unchanged budgets; existing-cache exact-completion p95 is 4,784 → 2,938 ms (38.6% lower) under recorded background load. The 80-edit private session completes eviction, recycling, refusal/recovery, durable worker restart and repeated post-GC probes. Raw B-rep matches throughout, but five sustained publication comparisons fail in face/edge references and lineage diagnostics; restart restores publication equality on this trace. A public two-box strict probe isolates eager-to-idle lineage checkpoint reuse with identical raw B-rep; the frozen baseline publication gate still fails. A separate [lineage runtime candidate](docs/qa/2026-10-21/h02-lineage-runtime-candidate.md) qualifies mode-specific checkpoint metadata and conservative fresh replay; new target-device acceptance remains required. Quiet-host timing, whole-worker retention and the detached Text contract remain unaccepted. Implementation plan: [revision-safe edit-to-frame performance](docs/plans/edit-pipeline-performance.md) covers staged geometry, optional analysis, cancellation, body deltas, owned outputs, kernel query costs and memory controls. [Interactive edit sessions](docs/interactive-edit-sessions.md) add retained parameter-preview preparation, verified lettering placement, immutable fixed-branch reuse and lighter face-drag mesh/metadata updates, with exact acceptance gates intact. Three quiet Node runs on the public 160-face STEP part publish accepted first-edit geometry at a 719 ms median versus the baseline's 3,111 ms complete publication; final analysis is 3,011 ms. Volumes and warnings match. Browser coverage also verifies pending accepted geometry and analysis-only mesh reuse; five box edits reach a rendered frame at a 350 ms median in headless Chromium with SwiftShader. This is local adapter/browser evidence, with target-device limits recorded in the plan. Characterize the reported 18–31 s first edit after reload; measure cold and warm runs on named hardware, identify the bottleneck and pin a justified regression budget. Keep preview timing separate from exact completion. Measured 2026-09-17 on an M5 Pro, production build, both redistributable holders ([report](docs/qa/2026-09-17/first-edit-after-reload.md), probe `test/e2e/perf-holder-reload.spec.ts`): once a reload settles, the first edit is a warm edit (1.2 s drilled / 0.33 s lettered; the history prefix restores from the reload's own rebuild); an edit typed before the reload settles pays that reload once (1.9 s production, 4–8 s dev server). Bottleneck per edit is measurement of the edited body, chiefly planar-distance edit proofs (0.6–0.7 s), not replay. Budget pinned as within-run ratios, asserted by the probe under `OZ_PERF_BUDGET=1`. 2026-09-29 private hammer, M5 Pro/macOS 27, production preview, three serial runs per build ([report](docs/qa/2026-09-29/h02-private-first-edit.md)): placing the fixed Text import in the newly compiled recipe's existing history prefix removes 2.16 s median Text replay. First settled edit exact median 10.90 → 9.27 s (−15%); sample p95 12.31 → 14.03 s (worse), so the design's 20% p95 target is not met. Nine bodies still measure and eight reuse; exact validation/recognition remain intact. The probe now requires fresh live readiness and successful committed edits, durable reload values, and asserts no Text replay under `OZ_PERF_BUDGET=1`. Preview timing stays separate (metric unavailable in these private runs). Existing saved histories are unchanged. Remaining: tail/reload variability, private B-rep/lineage equivalence, retained-heap evidence, and W02 before accepting the proposed new caches. 2026-09-29 [cache-safety qualification](docs/qa/2026-09-29/h02-cache-safety.md): owned measurement payloads, full live handle/validation witnesses, provenance keys and arena-wide fail-closed recovery added without a Remus bump. Three private runs per build: first-edit median 8.670 → 8.697 s; sample p95 8.985 → 8.732 s (−2.81%, below 20% target). Fixed Text replay stays zero; nine bodies measure/eight reuse; peak accounted measurement retention 15.60 MiB, actual private heap unavailable. Private fresh-build publication oracle fails on main and patch in blend-region keys/lineage diagnostic messages; assertion preserved. Detached Text interval remains disabled: pinned restore cannot preserve an independently edited holder branch. Public 2,000-edit checkpoint/retention and 270-edit dependent recycling regressions pass; local lint/typecheck/test/parity/build and six focused browser tests pass. 2026-09-30 publication determinism ([report](docs/qa/2026-09-30/h02-publication-determinism.md)): both field families the oracle caught are fixed at the source — `blendRegionKey` derives from its members' ADR-011 face hashes instead of kernel arena handles, and no published `lineageDiagnostics` message quotes a handle (regressions: `test/h02-publication-determinism.test.ts`, `packages/kernel-adapter/src/lineage-message-determinism.test.ts`); the stepped-bore replay comparison and the cad-operations warm/fresh oracle no longer strip `blendRegionKey`. H02 remains Partial pending the branch lifetime contract, a private fresh-build-oracle rerun and raw serialized B-rep equivalence, and worker-heap proof. 2026-10-04 edited-import measurement ([record](docs/performance-baseline.md#edited-imported-body-measurement-2026-10-04)): an imported body with history beyond fixed moves or rotations publishes an unsupported opening (the growing-holder recipe refuses it) instead of measuring one, and each face is read once per body measurement; Node offset-face sync on the 160-face hammer fixture 15.34 → 11.91 s median of three, cold import 12.62 → 11.97 s. Imported-feature recognition still runs fresh after edits because the edit changes it; its 5.7 s is two whole-solid kernel queries (`test/edited-import-opening.test.ts`). Geometry-only face-drag candidate: offset/radius previews use a separate disposable display/validation API and cancel obsolete frames; release still completes authoritative quantities and recognition. Adapter, protocol and coalescer regressions cover owned buffers, current-revision completion and retained refusals (`staged-geometry.test.ts`, `geometryWorker.test.ts`, `useGeometryWorker.test.tsx`, `livePreview.test.ts`); existing exact preview browser probes now assert no full sync while dragging and a full sync on Apply. See [contract](docs/plans/edit-pipeline-performance.md#geometry-only-face-drag-previews). Local lint/typecheck, the unchanged full `pnpm test` on an idle cloud host (4,040 root tests plus 2,364 web tests; eight root skips), 182 parity tests (one skip) and unchanged build budgets pass. Browser execution is pending hosted CI (local Chromium download HTTP 403), and target-device latency qualification remains separate. 2026-10-11 follow-up: continuous exact face previews coalesce without aborting every input, reuse one current gesture frame on reversal, and retain full completion on Apply. See the [matched-fixture qualification record](docs/qa/2026-10-11/face-drag-responsiveness.md) and [plan](docs/plans/edit-pipeline-performance.md#face-preview-follow-up-2026-10-11); Same-`2026.1.45` public-fixture qualification (three runs) records first browser frame 2230 → 630 ms median, with Apply 23 → 2100 ms because the baseline already completed analysis in its preview. Ambient host load and the smaller proxy fixture limit interpretation; this is earlier feedback, not a full-regeneration speedup. Separate kernel and combined-package qualification remain distinct. Final paired `2026.1.52` qualification records a 577 ms median first rendered preview and 1455 ms Apply-to-frame on the public proxy in SwiftShader (three runs); full analysis remains required on Apply. 2026-10-11 tracing follow-up to PR #624: geometry-only face-drag and typed candidates omit authoritative revision installation/frame measures; accepted live pending geometry and full commits keep their traces. The focused `visual-selection-live-preview.spec.ts` regression fails on unchanged main and passes locally in headless Microsoft Edge, including newer-revision ready traces after Apply; see the supporting plan. Hosted gates and target-device latency remain separate evidence. 2026-10-13 fast exact-drag scheduling on refreshed `5689adba`, unchanged Remus `2026.1.53`: three serial runs per build/operation on M5 Pro/macOS 27.0.1, production preview, headless Chrome 155/ANGLE Metal, show the 100 ms start floor dominates a small chamfered-cylinder workload. Reducing only that floor to 32 ms lowers subsequent input-to-frame medians from 98.9 → 32.2 ms (offset) and 99.3 → 32.6 ms (radius); worker medians remain 7–9 ms and first frames remain about 23–25 ms. See [method, samples and limits](docs/plans/edit-pipeline-performance.md#fast-exact-drag-scheduling-2026-10-13) and opt-in actual-drag probes in `visual-selection-live-preview.spec.ts`. Single-in-flight/coalescing, exact geometry/tolerances and full authoritative analysis before commit remain intact. H02 stays Partial: small-fixture local evidence does not establish private/large-part, p95, low-power/Windows/touch, physical-display, sustained-load, worker-heap or WKWebView acceptance; post-release ready-frame tracing is also unqualified by this slice. 15 October [matched-cache benchmark preparation](docs/qa/2026-10-15/h02-matched-cache-benchmark.md): production harness now supports observed disabled/enabled adapter-cache arms, matched input/pair checks, per-stage median/p95, overlap-aware measurement timing and optional post-GC worker JS heap telemetry without changing runtime defaults. Current-pin local holder oracle matches all normalized publication fields across 17 bodies/720 faces; unchanged-source byte/structure hit also passes. Browser campaign stopped under unrelated host load before any complete matched pair; timings, browser retained heap and 20% p95 target remain unmeasured. Quiet target-machine runs, branch-lifetime/mutation/long-session qualification and raw B-rep equivalence still block accepting a detached Text cache. Face-drag slice ([2026-10-18 record](docs/qa/face-drag-latency-2026-10-18.md)): a pointer-to-presented-frame stage probe (`perf-face-drag-trace.spec.ts`) found main-thread viewer installation at 1.1–1.3 s per update on the 160-face hammer STEP; typed-array smoothing with B-rep boundary outlines, layout-free frames and reused unchanged-face measurements cut it to about 0.1 s and median lag from 2.6–3.2 s to about 0.7 s, and simple-body lag by 10–20%. Proposed targets (60 fps, first geometry within 100 ms, p95 under 50 ms) remain unmet; kernel tessellation/area time and preview presentation remain. 2026-10-19: offset-face drag previews move the face through Remus's prepared face move (`prepareMoveFaces`, esaueng/remus#1181) from the second frame on the same face; it is feature-detected, so the pinned kernel keeps the unprepared move until the Remus update lands. Against a local Node build of that Remus head on the 160-face hammer STEP (40 preview frames, −6…−7.2, two interleaved runs), the preview median is 164–177 → 162–171 ms and p95 216–226 → 195–200 ms versus the same kernel unprepared; main's kernel measured 218–231 ms, most of that gap being Remus's exact face areas. Each prepared preview matches a cold unprepared one (`test/prepared-face-move-preview.test.ts`). The [2026-10-19 drag baseline](docs/qa/face-drag-kernel-calls-2026-10-19.md) records about 10,000 kernel calls per holder tick, 3.3× slower ticks with 200 unrelated bodies (all re-meshed, prepared path not engaged), and arena slot growth of about 2,600 per tick. Drag previews now checkpoint the feature before the dragged edit, so long histories keep the prepared move; with 200 unrelated bodies the accepted tick wall p50 drops 405 → 260 ms, and to 195 ms once previews keep their own meshes of bodies the drag rebuilt; each complete sync now also checkpoints its last feature, so the commit after a drag re-measures only the edited body (commit wall 1.80–1.88 → 1.52–1.70 s, geometry ready 0.70–0.81 → 0.48–0.58 s; same record). 2026-10-21 preview-cache identity slice (this PR): a refused analytic, domain, normal or boundary read now forgoes reuse and measures the face cold; an unreadable input can no longer become a shared `unavailable` key. Six failure/recovery regressions cover those reads and prove distinct faces keep their own measurements; complete reads resume safe reuse (`preview-face-geometry-cache.test.ts`). This closes a bounded cache-correctness gap; detached Text-branch lifetime, target-device timing, private B-rep equivalence and worker-heap acceptance remain open.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Existing holder instrumentation; [performance record](docs/performance-baseline.md). #296 improved warm rebuilds; it did not close cold latency. 21 October [existing-cache qualification](docs/qa/2026-10-21/h02-existing-cache-qualification.md): the current-pin private oracle matches every per-body serialized B-rep byte across 17 bodies/720 faces; real measurement-cache budget eviction, refusal/recovery and retired-checkpoint regressions pass. All six matched Linux browser arms pass; observed exact-completion p95 is 12,386.6 ms disabled / 9,348.2 ms enabled (24.5% improvement) under shared-host load, with post-GC worker JS heap recorded. Detached Text reuse still lacks a branch-preserving kernel operation; the M5 Pro local-chat handoff preserves target-device and sustained retained-memory acceptance as separate gates. |

Delete key on a viewport pick: a picked face or edge now deletes the feature that defines it (the feature its readout names and Edit opens), so selecting a fillet band and pressing Delete or Backspace removes that fillet, through the same dependents confirm and Undo toast as a History row. A union, and an add or cut extrude, keeps its base body's name. Face lists name a face after the feature that made it, derived at display time from its ADR-011 lineage through boolean operand slots, add/cut targets and fillet/chamfer copies — "Box 1 · top", "Box 2 · top", "Hole · through hole Ø6", "Fillet · blend face R3" instead of "Top face (1)/(2)" — with faces the lineage cannot place keeping the viewport's name (`faceProducerName.test.ts`, `test/body-and-face-names.test.ts`); the namer loads in its own chunk. The form now sends none, and the document validator (entry, edit, build) and the kernel guard apply one rule, `extrudeSidesConflict`, under which a zero back distance is the one-sided default. Slice 5, search and ask: the ⌘K bar ends in an Ask button, which is the closed assistant; search lists every feature and parameter by name (picking one opens the drawer on it) and ends with an "Ask the assistant" row that sends the typed words to the conversation, or leaves them in its composer when it cannot take them; the conversation floats over the stage standing on the bar instead of docking a column, so opening it costs the model no width, and its collapse control moves into its header (this PR; `CommandPalette.test.tsx`, `AssistantPanel.test.tsx`, `WorkspaceReadout.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/quiet-stage-search.spec.ts`; the stored assistant width is no longer read, and removing it is a follow-up) (#415). | <a id="u08"></a>U08 | Complete | Dockable model panels, decided 2026-10-20 on a design canvas: Parameters, Objects, History, Revisions and Diagnostics leave U04's single drawer and become independent panels that open unpinned from the rail, pin into stacked columns docked at either screen edge (rails on the inner edge, up to three columns right and two left), or float as movable, resizable, stackable windows; dragging a header or a rail icon previews the landing slot; a View options menu holds presets (Essentials default), Hide all, Lock, Compact and Width; layouts persist per device. Six slices, one PR each: layout model and persistence, panel extraction, right dock replacing the drawer (with the v1 migration, the no-dock rule at 720 px and below and the e2e fixture and count-readout migration), drag/left dock/floating (with the combined width cap), view options, documentation. Slice 1, layout model: pure `lib/panelLayout.ts` with placement, drag preview, stacking weights, floating z-order, recency, presets, view options, the 60% dock cap with parking, the 720 px no-dock rule, geometry and hit-testing, and the `openzcad-panel-layout:v1` load/save with v1 migration; nothing renders it yet (#766; `test/panel-layout.test.ts`). Slice 2, extraction: `components/modelPanels/` holds Parameters, Objects, History, Revisions and Diagnostics as standalone panels with their counts and hooks, and `Sidebar` composes them with an unchanged drawer DOM (#767; `modelPanels/*.test.tsx`). Slice 3, right dock: `PanelDock` replaces the drawer and `Sidebar.tsx`; a rail press opens a panel unpinned beside the rail, Pin docks it into one stacked right column with gap resizing and an even split, Close remembers its home, Revisions and Diagnostics join the rail, sketch and model drags suspend every open panel, ⌘K presents History or Parameters, nothing docks at 720 px and below (one panel in the right lane), and the layout key migrates from `openzcad-panel-state:v1` on first load (#771; `test/e2e/panel-dock.spec.ts`, which replaces `quiet-stage-drawer.spec.ts` and `drawer-disclosure-targets.spec.ts`). Slice 4, drag, left dock and floating: a header or rail drag-out lifts a panel to a chip beside the cursor and previews its slot in a column, a new column past either rail (the rail sliding over while hovered) or a window on the model, the emptied column holding until the drop; floating windows stack, move by header or frame grip, resize from the corner and keep a stored z-order a press, focus, rail press or ⌘K raises; the grip's Move menu, Alt+↑/↓, the gaps' arrow keys and the windows' move and resize keys do the same by keyboard, and Pop out joins the header above 720 px; the left dock publishes `--left-dock-w`, which the verb rail and the bottom lanes step past; the 60% cap spans both docks; new devices start in the two-sided Essentials; drags commit once and the viewer does not re-render during one (#788; `test/e2e/panel-drag.spec.ts`, `test/e2e/panel-float.spec.ts`). Slice 5, view options: the panel rail's View options menu (there was no Layout button to replace) picks Essentials, Full workspace, Modeling, Review or Model only, keeping the settings and turning Hide off, and marks the preset the layout still matches; Hide all keeps the layout behind a "Panels hidden · Show" pill and toggles on `Ctrl+\` (`⌘\`), a global, remappable `lib/keymap.ts` command; Lock layout turns off header and rail drags, gap and window resizing, the Move menu's placements and the move and resize keys, and disables the presets and Panel width with "Unlock the layout to change it" while Pin, Pop out and Close still act; Compact rows tightens every panel's rows; Panel width S/M/L (256/296/360 px) re-runs the cap, so Large parks Essentials' left column at 1280 px; all of it persists in `openzcad-panel-layout:v1` with no format change, and the menu's radio and checkbox items take the arrow keys (#789; `test/e2e/panel-view-options.spec.ts`). Slice 6, documentation: the quiet-stage plan marks its slice 4 drawer superseded and U04's row points here; `README.md` and `docs/design/README.md` describe the panels as built — a rail press opens one unpinned, Pin docks it, Close remembers its home, a header or rail drag stacks it in either dock, a new column or a floating window, the Move menu and keys do the same, View options holds the presets (Essentials by default), Hide all (`Ctrl+\`, `⌘\` on macOS), Lock, Compact and Width S/M/L, a stage 720 px wide or less shows one panel at a time in the phone lane, and `openzcad-panel-layout:v1` keeps the layout per device; the README's Build capture is regenerated in place and two new captures show a stacked floating window and the open View options menu, all three 1600 × 1000 from the production bundle with anonymous API fixtures, written by the opt-in `test/e2e/docs-captures.spec.ts` (skipped unless `OZ_CAPTURE=1`, weighted 0 in `shard-weights.json`) (#790; local captures, documentation evidence only; `test/deploy-safety.test.ts`, `test/e2e-shards.test.ts`). All six slices are recorded; production acceptance is separate. Docked columns fill the dock by default again (Fit panels to content is opt-in, stored as `hug` so the old default-on `fit` key no longer applies); a handle under each docked column sets its height (drag, arrow keys, End or a double-click to fill), remembered per set of panels, so a second panel in a shortened column fills the dock and taking it out restores the height (`panel-layout.test.ts`, `panel-dock.spec.ts`). Full-height docks and denser panels: the docks run from under the top bar to the window's foot, the cube moves to the model's bottom-right inside the instrument rail, and the scale bar stands upright against the model's left edge; floating windows may go anywhere on the stage, over the top bar and rails too; docked panels hug their content (View options › Fit panels to content, on by default) with a 4px dock gap between panels; the selection filter chip is gone (Q cycles and hands back to the tool, the status line names each choice); shorter panel headers, one-row Objects find/filter, and parameter descriptions shown only while written or being edited (`panel-layout.test.ts`, `sidebarContracts.test.ts`, `column-panel-sizes.test.ts`, `ViewportScaleIndicator.test.tsx`, panel and viewport e2e specs; PR #845). Issue #951: new in-place creation commands suspend model panels and More tools using the command's stable session identity. Explicit panel/tool requests last through picks, values and previews, and reset on the next command, including a relaunch of the same tool or Extrude on the same sketch. Cancel and Done restore the stored docks, floating windows, unpinned panels and fold without reframing the camera or changing preferences. Build-only guards, Move and direct drags retain their existing behavior. Browser coverage is in `test/e2e/panel-suspension.spec.ts`; existing reference-picking and History journeys explicitly request the panels they read. Panel suspension receives the effective workspace mode after seat and project permissions resolve the stored preference. Stale feature/sketch editing state cannot suspend panels in View, Tweak or Draw. Three hook regressions fail against the preceding implementation and pass with this guard; Build-mode suspension and explicit-release controls remain covered. The browser viewer journey verifies that effective View mode keeps modeling unavailable. History details suspend panels only while a primitive form or the selected feature's actual in-place editor is open. Read-only imported/direct-edit rows and failed editor arming keep panels visible. The imported-mesh browser regression reproduces hidden History before the fix; active primitive/Hole editing remains covered by the same production-renderer suite. Sketch plane selection and the entered sketch share one suspension identity. A browser regression reopens History at the plane prompt, keeps it visible after choosing the plane, then verifies a new sketch session suspends it again. Delete accepts a selected feature from the current complete validated commit while the background worker catches up. Canonical document-version checks still apply, and other selection edits retain worker-readiness checks. The timing-controlled browser regression holds worker analysis after Fillet commit: deletion fails before the fix, then removes the selected Fillet and completes its rebuild after the gate is released. Existing staged-geometry refusal checks remain covered. Production design review (PR #890): feature forms suspend model panels and the extra-tools fold while preserving the saved layout and camera. Explicit panel requests override suspension for their editing session; direct switches between sketch or feature sessions reset the release. Header controls own their full 24px hit rectangles in both themes (`usePanelSuspension.test.tsx`, `design-review-fixes.spec.ts`, `panel-dock.spec.ts`). | [Plan](docs/plans/dockable-panels-plan-2026-10-20.md). Supersedes U04 slice 4's drawer when slice 3 merges; keeps the quiet-stage constraints, the "Model panels" toolbar and "<Name> panel" names, and the entry-chunk budget. U06 Objects work lands in the extracted Objects panel. No document-format change; new per-device key `openzcad-panel-layout:v1`. |
| <a id="u09"></a>U09 | Done | In-viewport commands, decided 2026-10-24 ([plan](docs/plans/in-viewport-tools-plan-2026-10-24.md)): every Build tool runs as picks in the model, an evaluated preview and handles with keypad chips, finished by a Done/Cancel pill; the feature form goes away (rare options behind an Options popover; docked model panels unchanged), the selection card offers every applicable tool while the rail stays fixed, primitives place by click-drag and Scale pivots on the bounding-box centre. Phase 1 proves the contract on Revolve (profile, then an edge or axis, angle rig); each converted tool needs an e2e spec driven only by picks, drags and the keypad, and the same command factory as the AI patch path. Axes and directions are picked in the model, as the plan's §5 table has them. Revolve, Rotate about axis and Circular pattern take a straight edge or a cylinder's (or cone's) axis besides the listed lines, and Rotate about axis now lists X, Y and Z too (Revolve a world axis in its sketch plane that is not its own); the stored features only turn about datum axes, so Done adds the picked line as a datum axis in the same undoable step, just before the feature (an edit moves it before the edited one), and the turn follows its source. A revolve refuses a line off its profile's plane before the build would. A linear pattern reads a straight edge, a flat face's normal or a datum axis as its repeat direction (stored as its numbers, a world axis when it is one). Circular pattern adds Equal spacing / Fixed step under Options, a fixed step storing the total its copies span. Not expressible without schema work, so left out: a two-sided revolve (its angle runs one way from the profile, (0°, 360°]) and a sweep along model edges (its path is sketch curves only) (`tools.test.ts`, `datumPicks.test.ts`, `useInPlaceCommand.test.tsx`, `in-place-axis-picks.spec.ts`, `datum-axes.spec.ts`). Extrude runs in place: a region pick (or E) arms the region's arrow as before, and the extrude card's form is gone. A second arrow out of the back face sets an independent back distance, a rig hinged on the foot of the profile's longest side sets the draft angle, and each chip opens the keypad. The card's settings moved under the bar's Options with the same refusals (`lib/extrudeDraft.ts`, shared with the History editor): operation (Automatic, New body, Add, Cut, Intersect) and its target body, extent (Distance, To face with its face pick, Through all on Cut with its direction), One side or Symmetric, Distance, Back distance and Draft angle typed as expressions, and Reverse direction; Done or Enter creates it. History edits of an extrude still open its editor card (`extrudeDraft.test.ts`, `inPlace/extrude.test.ts`, `ExtrudeCommand.test.tsx`, `extrude-in-place.spec.ts`, `extrude-intent.spec.ts`, `extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts`, `extrude-through-all.spec.ts`, `multi-region-extrude.spec.ts`). Phase 7: Fillet and Chamfer run in place and their card is gone. Edges are clicks (in pick order, one body; the selection, a context menu or Options' Select all fill them), the size is a linear handle on the first edge with its chip, and the card's other inputs moved under the bar's Options: Radius change (constant, linear, eased) with an End R chip, a chamfer's shape (equal, distance and angle, two distances) with its chip and Swap the two faces, and the edges listed by name with remove buttons. Done commits through the card's own `edgeModifierCommand`, and a History row re-arms the same command, edges resolved by their stored names (`tools.test.ts`, `edit.test.ts`, `useInPlaceCommand.test.tsx`, `InPlaceCommandBar.test.tsx`, `edge-form-preview.spec.ts`, `edit-card-lifecycle.spec.ts`, `build-reach.spec.ts` F30, `hook-all-edge-fillet.spec.ts`). Phase 7: a History row re-arms the in-place command that made its feature (revolve, loft, sweep, helical sweep, hole, shell, thicken, solid offset, draft, mirror, split, scale, rotate about axis, the booleans and the patterns) with its picks in the slots, its values on the chips and its choices in Options, instead of opening a form. What the cards typed is typed on chips, expressions kept: a mirror or split plane, a helix axis, a draft's pull and hinge, a hole's U and V, a scale's pivot, a loft apex, a pattern's vector, so an untouched Done writes the feature back unchanged. Values and options the user has not changed follow the feature when someone else changes it. Done commits the same `updateFeature` the cards did, validated the same way. A Move still edits in its card (`edit.test.ts`, `useInPlaceCommand.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/in-place-history-edit.spec.ts`, `edit-card-lifecycle.spec.ts`). Phase 7: an in-place Hole's counterbore or countersink is sized on chips (its diameter and depth, or diameter and included angle) beside the bore. Until a chip is set the step follows the bore with the card's old ratios; a typed value or expression is stored as typed (`tools.test.ts`, `useInPlaceCommand.test.tsx`, two `in-place-face-tools.spec.ts` specs that read the exported hole). Phases 2–5: Loft, Sweep, Helical sweep, Hole, Shell, Thicken, Solid offset, Draft, Mirror, Split, Scale, Rotate about axis, Union, Subtract, Intersect and the linear, circular and grid patterns run in place through one command table (`apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.ts`) and one hook (`apps/web/src/hooks/useInPlaceCommand.ts`). Picks are region, curve, face, body and plane clicks (a box sweep fills Shell and Draft); numbers are linear, angle and chip handles with keypad entry; rare choices sit behind the bar's Options menu; Done waits for an exact preview of exactly what it commits (`tools.test.ts`, `useInPlaceCommand.test.tsx`, `linearRig.test.ts`, `curvePickerRig.test.ts`, `test/e2e/in-place-sketch-tools.spec.ts`, `test/e2e/in-place-face-tools.spec.ts`). Primitives, Plane, Axis, Image and Fillet/Chamfer still open their cards. Phase 6 (P, R): Plane, Axis and Image run in place. A Plane's sources are picked in the model — one plane (a ghost principal plane, a datum or a flat face) is an offset plane with an offset handle, two a midplane, a plane and an edge end a parallel plane through it, three edge ends a plane through them — each kept as the associative source the card stored; Flip normal is in Options. An Axis is inferred the same way from a straight edge, a cylinder, two edge ends or two planes, with Reverse in Options. The datum is previewed from the exact build before Done, which commits through the same resolved-datum check the card ran. An Image asks for its picture, then a plane or face; a click places it on the sketch snap, a corner handle scales it, a ring turns it, an offset chip slides it along a principal plane's normal, two clicks on it and a distance chip scale it to size, and opacity is in Options; Done adds it as one step and Cancel releases its bytes (`datums.test.ts`, `image.test.ts`, `useInPlaceCommand.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/datum-planes.spec.ts`, `test/e2e/datum-point-planes.spec.ts`, `test/e2e/datum-axes.spec.ts`, `test/e2e/reference-images.spec.ts`, `test/e2e/datum-plane-viewport.spec.ts`). Typed point positions and 5% opacity steps stay on the History and image cards. Phase 6, primitives in place: Box, Cylinder, Cone, Sphere and Torus open no card. The command previews the card's default at the origin at once, so Enter alone still makes it; a press-drag on the ground (or the upright principal plane facing an edge-on view) or on a flat face draws the footprint, a box's rectangle corner to corner and a round primitive's circle from its centre; the sizes are linear handles with chips, the height an arrow off the plane; the exact position (corner, base center or center) is typed from the bar's Options. On a face square to an axis a box stays unturned; a round primitive on a side face, or anything on a tilted face, turns in the same placement Move. Commits go through the card's own `createPrimitiveCommand` (`primitives.test.ts`, `tools.test.ts`, `useInPlaceCommand.test.tsx`, `InPlaceCommandBar.test.tsx`, `primitivePlacement.test.ts`, `test/e2e/in-place-primitives.spec.ts`; the e2e suite places primitives through `createPrimitiveInPlace`). Editing a primitive from History still opens its card. Phase 1, Revolve in place: no card; the profile is a region click, the axis a click on one of the drawn sketch or datum axis lines, the angle a drag of the new angle rig (`packages/viewport/src/gizmo/angleRig.ts`) or its chip's keypad, with an exact preview through the commit's own command and a Cancel / Done bar in the guidance row. Schema v38 adds the revolve's region `profile`, so a rectangle drawn as four edges revolves (`test/region-revolve.test.ts`, `useInPlaceRevolve.test.tsx`, `angleRig.test.ts`, `axisPickerRig.test.ts`, `InPlaceCommandBar.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/in-place-revolve.spec.ts`). Gap closure (options and rigs): Move finishes on the in-place Cancel / Done bar in the command row instead of a card in the right lane, and its six exact numbers, name, body picker and Copy sit behind the bar's Options; the gizmo is unchanged. Draft's angle is an angle rig hinged where the first face meets the neutral plane (base, top, clicked point, origin or typed), its arm up the face so a positive drag leans the face out of the body as a positive draft does; a curved face or one square to the pull keeps the typed chip. Not expressible on the pinned kernel and schema, so not offered: a left-handed helix (the kernel refuses a negative pitch, "pitch must be positive", and reversing the axis keeps a right-handed coil) and an outward shell ("Shell thickness must be finite and positive at the solid's scale"; the stored thickness is inward only) (`tools.test.ts`, `DirectModelingOverlays.test.tsx`, `TransformForm.test.tsx`, `in-place-face-tools.spec.ts`, `panel-dock.spec.ts`, the Move specs). Phase 7: the form cards the in-place commands replaced are gone. Every modeling tool except Move, Extrude, the primitives, sketches, datums and reference images starts and edits in place, so the Revolve, Boolean, Pattern, Fillet/Chamfer and modeling-operation (Mirror, Split, Hole, Shell, Thicken, Solid offset, Draft) cards, the Inspector branches and App state that fed them, and the Hole card's bore ghost were unreachable and are deleted (about 7,500 lines). The commits they made still go through the same `updateFeature` and feature commands the in-place Done uses; the Move card's Transform form stays for the Move edits it serves. Phase 7: the Selection card carries the verbs the plan's §5 table gives a pick. A flat face adds Shell, Draft, Thicken and Plane after its own edits, Sketch and Hole; a round face Axis and Shell; edges Axis; a body Rotate, Scale, Split, Pattern and Solid offset beside Move and Mirror; two bodies Subtract and Intersect beside Union and Move. The card shows the first four as buttons, so a pick's usual verbs keep their places, and the rest behind a More button in its header; a lit verb is always a button. A picked region's Extrude card offers Revolve, Sweep and Loft (the region as its first section), each starting its in-place command with the region already in its slot (`selectionCallout.test.ts`, `SelectionCard.test.tsx`, `ToolCard.test.tsx`, `in-place-face-tools.spec.ts`, `in-place-sketch-tools.spec.ts`). Done 2026-10-26: every Build tool runs in place, merged in #866, #867, #873, #883, #885, #887, #889, #893, #895, #896, #917, #919, #920 and #934. What §5 asks for and the pinned kernel or schema cannot express is left out by name, not approximated: a two-sided revolve, a sweep along model edges, a left-handed helix and an outward shell. Extrude edits run in place too, and the extrude card is gone: a History row (or the Inspector's Edit extrude) re-arms the in-place Extrude seeded from the stored feature, expressions as written, with its own distance arrow, the back arrow and the draft rig on the model and its extent (to-face target and through-all side included), Sides and stored operation and target under the bar's Options; an edit never infers an operation. Done commits through the same `updateFeature` the card's Apply used, validated and refused the same way (its refusals said in the bar), and an untouched Done writes the feature back exactly as stored (`extrudeEditData`). `ExtrudeForm`, `ExtrudeExtentControls`, `ExtrudeControls` and the Inspector's extrude branch are deleted (`extrudeEditing.test.ts`, `ExtrudeCommand.test.tsx`, `extrude-edit-in-place.spec.ts`, `extrude-intent.spec.ts`, `extrude-through-all.spec.ts`, `extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts`, `feature-history.spec.ts`, `solid-result-options.spec.ts`). Browser CI retains representative in-place creation, exact editing, refusal, Cancel/Done, undo, persistence and exchange journeys in a tagged smoke suite; the extended geometry and responsive regression matrix remains available through `pnpm test:e2e:full`. Suite selection and separate shard weights are covered by `test/e2e-shards.test.ts`. This reduces routine browser coverage and does not claim new product behavior or additional U09 acceptance. Routine unit CI selects representative model, exact-kernel, command and web editing/persistence cases while retaining workflow, privacy and package guards. `pnpm test:full` retains the extended root/web regression suites. Selection and fail-closed pre-worker Git preparation are covered by `test/unit-suites.test.ts` and `test/unit-global-setup.test.ts`. Shared STEP links now fetch referenced import artifacts through the anonymous capability route, verify source bytes before opening, and block link creation while a source remains device-only (`test/project-share-links.test.ts`, `exactSourceResolver.test.ts`). Collaboration rooms use hibernatable sockets, restore identity, presence and edit leases after eviction, load document history on edit, and skip unchanged lease writes on wake (`test/collaboration-room-socket.test.ts`, `test/project-edit-lease.test.ts`); live duration savings and cross-device behavior still need verification. The project-card Properties dialog inspects dates, model counts and document/source sizes on demand without opening or changing the model (`StartScreen.test.tsx`, `projectProperties.test.ts`, `test/e2e/project-properties.spec.ts`); missing creation history and older offline device copies are labelled explicitly. 1 October review F6: a tab open across a deploy no longer fails silently — every `import()` that loses its chunk (reported by the build's `vite:preloadError`) raises the stale-build notice, error text names the update instead of quoting the hashed URL, and the start screen, which has no toast lane, keeps a "new version — Reload" offer in its footer (`staleChunk.test.ts`, `errors.test.ts`, `StartScreen.test.tsx`; checked against `vite preview` with the demo chunk deleted mid-session). Serving the previous asset generation for a while after a deploy is not done. Review regression fixes: manual checkpoints serialize with autosave and retain later edits; locked tabs cannot save; freshness pulls retain the old baseline when local edits race storage, and measurement pulls preserve newer device annotations (`test/cloud-project-autosave.test.ts`, `test/account-project-refresh.test.ts`, `test/measurement-cloud-sync.test.ts`, `test/e2e/cloud-sync.spec.ts`). Parameter rename visits authored expression fields only (`test/parameter-rename.test.ts`); wheel pan respects disabled controls and held drags (`CameraController.test.ts`). 5 October recording fixes: account copies that only extend compatible saved history reconcile without a conflict, live acknowledgements preserve same-version checkpoints, and completed exact results survive bookkeeping-only save/reopen adoptions without restarting geometry or resetting the active undo manager. Recovery deduplication includes named saves and undo history instead of treating equal geometry as all preserved work (`test/project-open-choice.test.ts`, `useGeometryWorker.test.tsx`, `useCollaboration.test.tsx`, `conflictRecovery.test.ts`, `test/e2e/cloud-sync.spec.ts`). These are source/local-browser regression results; named-build and live cross-device acceptance remain open. 11 October Edge QA follow-up (EDGE-03): accepted canonical edits start serialized device writes immediately; native departure protection remains armed through queued, active and failed writes, and a failed durability barrier keeps the current workspace open. Eleven controller tests and four local headed Edge regressions cover online/offline edits, immediate real reloads, blocked IndexedDB, quota failure and retry (`localProjectAutosave.test.ts`, `test/e2e/local-save-departure.spec.ts`); cloud-sync coverage checks canonical persistence with timers paused. These are local results, with offline edits reconnected for app-shell reload; named deployed-build verification remains open. Production [Edge QA, 11 October](docs/qa/2026-10-11/production-qa-edge.md) records EDGE-03: immediate reload during Saving lost the accepted Sphere in 2/2 online and 2/2 offline attempts on `7d66dc71`. The [12 October production pass](docs/qa/2026-10-12/production-qa-edge.md) confirms the #647 pending-save fix on `51c2a0d2`; broader live acceptance remains open. Download failures now offer an honest connection/reload toast on both the start screen and workspace without claiming a new build. 14 October review (this PR): anonymous share links serve only the current model — no undo history, command log, save-state names, branch provenance or owner id — and `/sources` serves only imports the current model uses; trash purge stays within D1's 100-parameter limit and can no longer fail the project list; the sharing-off room notice fires on the on→off transition only (`test/project-share-links.test.ts`, `test/cloudflare-adapters.test.ts`, `test/api-routes.test.ts`, D1 sharing SQL in `test/project-sharing.test.ts`). 14 October review follow-ups: SEC-3 sign-in disruption (fix PR #677), SEC-5 collaboration reconnect disruption and SEC-6 anonymous-route abuse remain assigned here; security mechanisms stay withheld until their fixes ship. Supporting evidence: [project review](docs/reviews/project-review-2026-10-14.md). 2026-10-14 collaboration QA fixes (this PR): room owners/editors establish a merge base from matching content or proven revision descent, adopt editor broadcasts while lease-denied, and preserve divergent offline work. Shared members use room acknowledgements and publish explicit checkpoints through the room instead of the refused account-write route; terminal room refusals and required updates report a durable local copy without an indefinite save spinner. Same-tab invitation/public-link navigation is handled with superseded invitation requests fenced through acceptance and document loading, identical pending invitations preserved, settled attempts retryable, navigation releases only its own busy state even after session changes, waits for unrelated foreground work, dismissal restores the prior share URL, and direct navigation/copy paths guarded, public-to-invitation transitions exit the anonymous workspace, pending account saves drain before public or invitation navigation, share-fragment removal cancels pending loads and exits anonymous sessions without stale departures overriding newer navigation, malformed invitations cancel cleanly, all shelf workspace actions cancel delayed shares and clear failed share fragments, failed replacements restore the active share URL, and revoked-link guidance waits for startup and remains visible. Regression evidence: `useCollaboration.test.tsx`, `collaboration.spec.ts` and local built-browser verification with two authenticated QA accounts against the real beta backend. Deployment verification remains separate. Source-copy and cancellation regressions now use migrated SQLite, R2 streams and real collaboration rooms to cover independent ownership, stale source aliases, same-version archival, delayed finalization, rollback/publication races and oversized cloud-only copies (`test/imported-source-duplication.test.ts`, `test/mesh-artifact-discard.test.ts`). Migrations 0026/0027 and readiness guards are source candidates; deployment and live recovery remain unverified. 2026-10-15 L01 checkpoint durability follow-up (#681): explicit member saves persist a restorable immutable revision before room acknowledgement, preserve buffered edits and already-adopted echoes, advance the acknowledged base even if device storage fails, and reject older-server false success. Revision retention starts only after successful room publication; refused or failed publication keeps every previously acknowledged save point. A lost or late HTTP acknowledgement reuses a verified checkpoint already adopted from the room broadcast, including after buffered edits, rather than creating an unrestorable local duplicate. Oversized HTTP edit failures release waiting checkpoint drains. A failed or stalled first-use checkpoint module retains the clicked named state on this device; the existing 15-second deadline and room cancellation cover the import, and late loading cannot publish after fallback. Regression evidence: `test/collaboration-checkpoint.test.ts`, `useCollaboration.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/member-checkpoint-roundtrip.spec.ts`, `test/e2e/member-checkpoint-offline.spec.ts` and the collaboration/navigation browser suites. These are source and local-browser checks; deployed cross-device acceptance remains open. Device-only member checkpoints now travel in project backups with their source bytes and restore independently of the room timeline (`projectBackupDeviceCheckpoints.test.ts`); live backup recovery remains open. Committed checkpoints return their HTTP acknowledgement even if socket broadcast delivery fails. Definitively refused checkpoint publications, including lease/membership loss after insertion but before publication starts, now remove their inserted revision and unreferenced R2 snapshot while retaining prior revisions and uncertain-outcome recovery data; new STEP/mesh sources remain inside the checkpoint snapshot until a canonical save, so refusal restores storage usage without deleting shared sources (`test/collaboration-checkpoint.test.ts`, `project-object-storage.test.ts`). Member restores reject room changes detected while loading a snapshot before writing private history, and keep their before/after save points in device storage while ordinary room writes carry the restored model and acknowledged timeline only; reload and Undo/Redo remain available (`localSaveStateRestore.test.ts`, `test/e2e/member-checkpoint-roundtrip.spec.ts`). Device-only branches bypass the account request when offline, and history metadata uses a key-only IndexedDB index with existing-state upgrade coverage (`localProjectStore.test.ts`, `test/e2e/member-checkpoint-roundtrip.spec.ts`). Retention orders equal-version saves by creation time, protects the just-written point across timestamp ties, and verifies fallback retention before claiming device storage; schema 13 updates legacy age indexes without loading snapshot bodies (`collaborationCheckpointFallback.test.ts`, `localProjectStore.test.ts`). SEC-3 ([14 October review](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/675)): someone who knows only an email can no longer void its pending sign-in code or spend its code allowance — requests are throttled per email and IP under a 10-per-15-minute email ceiling, throttled repeats send nothing and return the requester's own usable pending challenge under the same attempt cap, a successful sign-in atomically creates its session and voids the email's other codes, and after 20 wrong codes in 24 hours each code allows one try (`test/auth.test.ts`). Failure counts conservatively remain active until 24 hours after code expiry (at most ten extra minutes), with late-guess verification, throttled reuse, owner sign-in and eventual recovery regressions in the same real-SQLite suite. Profile/account erasure also clears every requester bucket for that email so immediate re-registration succeeds without clearing other emails or the shared IP budget (`test/auth.test.ts`); live verification follows deployment. Shelf background reconciliation (PR #814) bounds admission across tabs, keeps discovery independent of network backlog and preserves pending edits through cancellation and retention. The source design and regression record is in `docs/reviews/shelf-reconciliation-background-2026-10-21.md`. Local retention is owned by the current discovery generation, including signed-out work, separately from permission to send account writes. Superseded discovery is checked before purging and after awaited metadata reads inside each delete transaction. Current metadata eligibility, alias fences, pending tombstones and explicit permanent deletion remain intact. Regression coverage includes account changes before purge, during metadata reads and between rows, cross-tab markers, restored/newer metadata and legitimate signed-out expiry. Copy-name normalization preserves suffix counters, whitespace handling and the project-name length limit with linear processing. Compatibility coverage is in `test/project-shelf.test.ts`; assistant request normalization retains its existing grammar with coverage in `test/all-edge-fillets.test.ts`. [Account data download](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/blob/main/docs/data-download.md) adds Privacy & data ZIP export for device/account data, portable project copies, retained revisions, files and authorized shared-project measurements (PR #974). Browser download coverage exercises desktop and mobile layouts; native artifact downloads stream through the authenticated bridge. Production verification remains separate. Data downloads skip unavailable or oversized project copies and continue with the remaining projects. The completion UI and the ZIP's `NOT-INCLUDED.txt` and manifest list every omitted backup by name, ID, copy and reason. Project/history files commit atomically, failed projects spend no archive budget, report space is reserved, and portable backups take priority over the optional raw snapshot. Archive and UI regressions cover failed reads, missing history, capacity, later-project success, raw snapshot omission and account-change cancellation. Explicit conflict choices (PR #982): compact clickable rows show each version's latest canonical edit date, local time and timezone, with a Newer label. Replacing either side keeps the original project and creates no recovery project. Only Keep both saves an ordinary local copy owned by the current user before remote adoption, with retries reusing only a copy still present outside Trash. Imported STEP/mesh sources and reference pictures, including retained undo/redo, are integrity-checked and cached before their original artifact identities are detached. Existing recovery projects remain readable. Evidence: `ProjectConflictDialog.test.tsx`, `conflictRecovery.test.ts`, `accountConflictRecovery.test.ts`, `conflictLocalCopy.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/cloud-sync.spec.ts` cover timestamps, ownership, preserved undo/redo, permissions, failures/retries and all three choices across two browsers, including 390px and keyboard selection. Live deployment acceptance remains separate. Library shelf cross-device sync: the Projects shelf re-lists the account on focus, visibility and every 60 s (#1036, `libraryRefresh.test.ts`), and library folders, pins and order sync through the account (#1037, #1040, #1041, #1043): a per-field mergeable folder set (`libraryFolderSync.test.ts`, including a seeded three-device convergence run), D1 migration 0045 with compare-and-set `/api/library-folders` (`library-folder-sets.test.ts`), the client sync loop (`libraryFolderCloudSync.test.ts`) and, for encrypting accounts, records sealed under the account key per the ADR-024 amendment of 2026-10-30 (`library-folders.test.ts` fixed vector, `sealedLibraryFolders.test.ts`). Enabled for beta by `LIBRARY_FOLDER_SYNC_ENABLED` once migration 0045 is applied. Portable backups retain one canonical source payload across the model and retained history instead of duplicating STEP originals in `files` and `sources`. Legacy embedded data is externalized in the backup copy without altering the live document. Original interchange files are opt-in; required rebuild sources always travel. Bulk downloads offer an explicit choice to skip extra device recovery records, reuse each project's file catalog across backups, and collect save coverage without reparsing completed backup JSON. The existing size/memory bounds, local/cloud separation, verified encrypted readers and atomic omission reporting remain in force. Tests cover independent restore, optional originals, duplicate STEP bytes, catalog request counts, snapshot selection and responsive download controls. Production timing and affected-account completion remain separate from local/hosted validation.  Reload restoration now publishes local projects with their verified account folder scope, reserves actual grouped tiles for every sort, primes up to 16 device previews with a 120 ms ceiling, waits for a drawn workspace frame, and flushes the camera on pagehide. Local-copy/account wording and room-refusal details are distinct. Regression coverage: `library-reload-stability.spec.ts`, `workspace-reload.spec.ts`, `PartThumbnail.test.tsx`, `useScrollMemory.test.tsx`, `useProjectView.test.tsx`; local mocked browser evidence only, authenticated production confirmation remains open. | [Cloud-sync record](docs/plans/project-cloud-sync-plan.md), E6/E7 and former TODO release gates. Deployment/resource changes require separate authorization. |

The design-only contract is recorded in the [S02 reference-dimensions plan](docs/plans/sketch-reference-dimensions-plan.md); slice S02-A is delivered: an additive v15 `SketchNode.referenceDimensions` field with stable `annotationId` identity and a pure distance/angle/radius resolver over primitive targets; reference records carry no value field and are excluded from GCS input by construction, unresolvable targets return a named `unresolved` status, and unit conversion is display-only (this PR; `packages/shared/src/sketch-reference-dimensions.test.ts`, `test/sketch-reference-dimensions.test.ts`). Add/delete/placement replay stable annotation identities and preserve them through undo/redo, native backup import and local reopen. References never enter GCS or exact-history digests; annotation-only changes preserve solve evidence, and referenced geometry deletion refuses until its annotations are explicitly removed. Invalid saved targets remain visibly unresolved and removable; normalization checks current and undo/redo-only annotation payloads. Regression evidence: `test/sketch-reference-commands.test.ts`, `test/sketch-reference-exact.test.ts`, `SketchReferenceDimensions.test.tsx`, `sketch/solveEvidence.test.ts`, dimension graphic/layout tests and `test/e2e/sketch-reference-dimensions.spec.ts`. The browser contract covers mm/cm/inch, read-only activation, label drag/keyboard placement, geometry edits, deletion recovery, undo/redo, saved reopen and phone controls. Composite/datum targets (S02-D), horizontal/vertical distance variants and a separate display-unit selector remain open; no schema bump is introduced beyond the merged optional-field envelope. Comparison slice: a distance-type badge (absolute/horizontal/vertical) on length dimensions, decided with the driven/driving semantics. S02-E projected reference distances (PR #797): Horizontal and Vertical modes measure sketch-local separation, including valid zero projections, while omitted modes retain legacy absolute distance. Stored identities and label positions survive edits, undo/redo, replay and reopen. Current, readable-history and replay carriers must satisfy the field's own introduction version. Acceptance evidence and remaining composite/datum/display-unit scope are recorded in the supporting sketch-reference-dimensions plan. Combined integration coverage uses actual linked projections and folder edits with retained drawing/image/PNG history: absolute and horizontal distances follow the source width while vertical stays fixed, with exact geometry, undo, native backup and replay unchanged (`test/schema-distance-folders-retention.test.ts`). Collaboration HTTP/WebSocket boundaries refuse backdated distance carriers before storage (`test/schema-distance-mode-compatibility.test.ts`). | Explicit dimension schema/semantics; product S-2.
