| <a id="s03"></a>S03   | Partial  | Truthful DOF/under/over-constrained status, entity highlighting and removable-constraint diagnostics; conflicting edits leave the prior solved sketch intact. Delivered slices: conflict refusal names top residual constraints and preserves the solved sketch; the solve pill reports kernel classification/DOF; residual-backed constraint rows are marked removable and only entities named by non-zero residuals are highlighted in the sketch viewport (#350, this PR, `sketch/constraints.test.ts`, `control-reference.test.ts`). Rank redundancy remains intentionally unattributed when the kernel supplies no residual culprit. Sketch-wide defined colouring is delivered: the pinned GCS reports one DOF scalar and a classification with no per-entity freedom, so `sketchDefinedState` paints all non-construction geometry and its snap-point dots in the success colour only on a converged, zero-DOF, solved sketch, and the pill, constraint rows and viewport read one derivation (this PR; `sketch/constraints.test.ts`, `viewer/sketchModeController.test.ts`, `SketchToolRail.test.tsx`). Per-entity under-defined colouring stays open until the solver supplies per-entity evidence. Actionable inspection and recovery now expose a Review constraints route from solve warnings, focus the existing constraint list, and let every stored relation reveal its current referenced geometry for selection in the normal entity editor. Navigation writes no history; measured residual rows remain the only attributed conflicts, while unqualified solver refusals explicitly say that no conflicting relation was identified. Regression evidence: `sketch/constraintInspection.test.ts`, `SketchToolRail.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/sketch-constraint-inspection.spec.ts` cover stale references, keyboard inspection, live geometry selection, compact-window action reachability, refusal preservation, deletion, undo/redo and saved reopen. The anchored-entity setting remains open. Comparison slices: fully-defined/under-defined colour states on sketch geometry and points, and an anchored-entity rule (first or last selected stays put when a relation moves things) exposed as a setting. Add a navigable remaining-DOF inspection path only where qualified solver evidence identifies relevant entities; numeric status, geometry highlighting and constraint-list state must agree. Keep attribution explicitly unknown when the solver cannot identify a culprit ([FreeCAD E03](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md#hands-on-evidence)). [Production QA remediation](docs/plans/production-qa-fixes-2026-10-05.md) proposes the confirmed 5 October fixes; local regression and build evidence belong to its implementation PR, with production retest pending deployment. 14 October review follow-up UX-2 is delivered: rectangles and polygons drawn since S04 are lines whose corners carry point references, and every point-taking tool (Distance, Coincident, Midpoint, Symmetric, Point on curve, Lock, Disconnect) now refuses a snap without one by name instead of repeating its hint — a parametric rectangle's corners, centre or side midpoints and a parametric polygon's centre (redraw with the Rectangle or Polygon tool), a text origin, a midpoint, quadrant, intersection or the sketch origin; grid and axis-alignment snaps keep the hint (this PR; `sketch/constraints.test.ts` against the real snap generator, `test/e2e/sketch-constraint-set.spec.ts`). Explicit conversion of legacy parametric shapes remains S04. Viewport relation glyphs (ZC-039) are delivered: every non-dimensional relation draws a glyph on the curve or point it holds (curve relations at a line's middle, a circle's 45° point or an arc's mid-sweep; point relations clustered per coincident point), relations the last solve named as conflicting are marked. A presentation-only `sketching.relationGlyphs` preference (Always · Selected · Hidden, default Selected, so a sketch shows the relations of the entity being worked on) sits in the sketch palette and Settings → Sketching; older clients and stored settings default it (this PR; `sketch/relationGlyphs.test.ts`, `viewer/sketchRelations.test.ts`, `test/app-settings.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-relation-glyphs.spec.ts`). Research-audit Stage 2e completes the glyphs: each chip now stands off its geometry in screen space — a curve's chip on the side away from the sketch's centre (so a closed profile's chips sit outside it), a circle's outward, a point's chip in the widest gap between the curves meeting there — and chips claim space in a fixed anchor order (point chips first, then curve chips, by position), sliding along their curve or swinging round their point, then stepping out a ring, so none covers its own anchor, any other anchor or another chip, and none leaves the viewport; a chip with no room within that bounded search (a dense sketch zoomed far out) is hidden until zooming makes room. Screen directions are the projection's exact clip-space derivative, so they hold at any coordinate magnitude. The layout runs on every drawn frame from typed arrays and a screen grid, so it costs about linear time, allocates nothing and writes no style on a still frame; chips stay one CSS2D label per cluster. Because glyphs no longer sit on geometry they take clicks: a click selects that relation (shown on every chip it draws on; the entity selection stays, an armed pick tool gives way to Select), Delete removes it in one undoable edit before it would remove the entity, and Escape clears both in one press. A per-kind show/hide filter (`sketching.hiddenRelationGlyphs`, presentation only, no document schema change) sits in the sketch palette and Settings → Sketching; unknown kinds drop out and a save from an older client keeps the stored filter (this PR; `sketch/relationGlyphLayout.test.ts`, `sketch/relationGlyphs.test.ts`, `viewer/sketchRelations.test.ts`, `interaction/machine.test.ts`, `test/app-settings.test.ts`, `test/api-routes.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-relation-glyphs.spec.ts`). The anchored-entity rule (ZC-041) is delivered as decided: a relation now applies as it is added, in one undoable edit with its solved geometry, and its first pick stays put — a point pick holds that point, an object pick the whole object (a circle also keeps its radius) — so reversing the picks of Equal reverses which side keeps its size. Single-pick relations solve free. When holding the first pick makes the relation unsatisfiable the solve falls back to free and says so; when it cannot solve at all, nothing changes and the culprit is named, as for a driving dimension. A held solve's DOF counts held points as fixed, so it is not shown as the sketch status (this PR; `test/sketch-anchored-solve.test.ts` on the pinned kernel, `test/e2e/sketch-constraint-set.spec.ts`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Existing GCS detailed results; product S-3 and execution Slice D.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| <a id="s04"></a>S04 | Partial | Give rectangles/polygons stable point identity or a defined decomposition path; constraints and downstream region references survive revision and replay. Delivered for new drawings (research-audit Stage 2c, ZC-006/007/008/011; schema v26): by the owner's decision recorded in the [composite identity design](docs/plans/sketch-composite-identity-plan.md), the Rectangle tool's corner, centre and three-point types and a new viewport Polygon tool (P, sides 3–64, circumradius convention) draw real lines held by persisted relations. Those are corner coincidents plus horizontal/vertical sides, perpendicular/parallel sides for the rotated three-point type, or vertices on a construction circumcircle with equal sides. An additive `SketchNode.composites` record names the lines as one object, with ordered corners and sides by index. Each shape commits as one undoable transaction with pre-assigned ids. Selecting a side opens the shape's resize card, which moves every member in one solved edit, keeping the centre (centre rectangle, polygon) or corner 1. Deleting a member dissolves the record and leaves the lines. The record never enters GCS or the exact-history digest. Saved parametric rectangle/polygon objects load unchanged and are never decomposed. Evidence: `test/sketch-composites.test.ts` (pinned-kernel solves are non-redundant with exactly 4/4/5/4 free DOF; (0,0)–(20,12) bounds 240 with perimeter 64; centre (5,7) 20×12 has corners (−5,1)/(15,13) and keeps its centre on resize; a hexagon of circumradius 10 has sides of 10; collinear third-point refusal; replay, reload, undo and delete-dissolve), `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/composites.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-composite-tools.spec.ts`. Open: composite corner/side targets for snaps and S02-D dimensions; region-provenance tokens and explicit conversion for legacy parametric objects. S04-B named composite targets (PR #800) retain an explicit composite identity, corner/side index and complete ordered topology witness for new held-line rectangles and polygons. References and snaps fail by name on missing, ambiguous or changed ownership. Mixed primitive/composite distances and angles, projected modes, raw expressions and label placement survive resize, replay, undo/redo and reopen. Integration introduces targets at schema v36, after reference-distance modes v35, with independent positive carrier-version checks over current, readable-history and saved/nested replay data. Drawing annotations and reference images remain authored data. Legacy conversion and boundary provenance follow in the next slice. The combined retained-content regression uses actual linked projected edges, named composite corners/sides and all distance modes, plus drawing/image/PNG/folder history. A source resize and composite reshape preserve target identities and exact profiles through undo, replay and native backup (`test/schema-composite-folders-retention.test.ts`). Integer schema proof and collaboration HTTP/WebSocket refusal/acceptance are covered in `test/sketch-composite-reference-commands.test.ts`. Live reference Add validates canonical target identities and measures the matching project/version-qualified solved sketch view. It appends only an annotation; authored geometry, expressions and feature geometry digests stay unchanged. Composite command replay restores the same strict target identities without requiring transient solver output, so an annotation may remain unresolved until the exact build completes, as with saved annotations. Primitive-only replay keeps numerical validation. No payload flag bypasses live qualification. `test/sketch-composite-reference-resolved.test.ts` uses a real linked edge and mixed expression/numeric seeds whose solved width is 32 while canonical seeds remain 20. Add, full JSON command replay, undo/redo and native reopen preserve the 32-unit reference, exact volume 576, all authored inputs and feature digests. Missing, stale and foreign geometry proof refuses live Add. `test/sketch-composite-reference-restoration.test.ts` preserves nonmeeting live refusals and rejects malformed, reordered, foreign, missing, aliased or ambiguously owned targets, duplicate annotations, driving values and older schema carriers. S04-C explicit legacy conversion and boundary provenance (PR #801): converting rectangles or regular polygons creates independent owned primitive constraints, stable aliases and inert authored witnesses in one undoable command. Release shape constraints retains user constraints and aliases. Complete outer/hole boundary loops retain cyclic order and split-edge tokens; precise matching precedes legacy fallback. Conversion preserves selected and first-object consumers, source lineage, raw expressions and native replay/backup behavior. Integration introduces these fields at schema v37, preserving distance modes v35 and composite targets v36 as separate carrier boundaries. Current, readable-history and saved/nested replay copies require supported ownership and version proof; opening legacy documents performs no conversion and synthesizes no provenance. The supported S04 contract and named refusal boundaries are recorded in the composite-identity plan; ROADMAP.md remains the delivery-status ledger. Actual predecessor integration is covered by `test/schema-provenance-folders-retention.test.ts`: mixed-unit legacy conversion inside a linked projected outline preserves p1/b1 boundaries, aliases and raw source expressions. Source resize follows exact volume576→936 and an attached boss from x10→16 while v35/v36 measurements, folders, drawing/image/PNG/history content, undo, native backup and replay remain consistent. Collaboration HTTP/WebSocket refusal and acceptance use the same v37 proof (`sketch-provenance-validation.test.ts`). Conversion of a legacy shape beside source-driven ordinary geometry qualifies selected profiles against the current exact read view. A versioned command witness carries only changed numeric analysis geometry and a compact digest of authored model inputs. It is used only during profile analysis; source witnesses, neighboring expressions, geometry tolerances and persistent nodes are not replaced with solved coordinates. Live preparation requires current exact projection verdicts. Missing/stale/broken proof, changed model inputs, foreign entities, malformed numeric fields and topology mismatches refuse atomically. The existing resolver still enforces ordered outer/hole provenance and repeated boundary occurrences. Payloads without this optional witness keep their path. Native copies rebind project identity and artifact locations without changing model geometry, so those locations are excluded from the stale-input digest. Source checksums, modeling identities, parameters, constraints, consumer references and units remain bound. The digest is a content guard, not authentication of solver coordinates. `test/sketch-conversion-qualification-exact.test.ts` verifies the real solved 32-unit outline containing a 2x2 hole at x24..26: exact volume564 survives conversion, full replay, native backup import, copied-project replay and undo/redo while raw neighboring `2 cm` and `h` remain intact. Editing typed height afterward produces volume660 and replays identically. The browser regression in `test/e2e/sketch-legacy-conversion.spec.ts` drives the actual action and checks unchanged typed neighbors, volume, history and reopen. | Design/schema review first; product S-4. No silent conversion of legacy entities. |
| <a id="s05"></a>S05   | Partial  | Trim/extend, offset, sketch fillet/chamfer, mirror and linear/circular patterns; each tool preserves constraint/refusal/undo contracts. Deliver each tool as a slice. Delivered: Fillet and Chamfer on the kernel's 2D operations for line pairs (Offset first shipped there for closed line loops), each one undoable transaction, with fillets writing coincidence, line-to-arc tangency and radius constraints (#333; `packages/kernel-adapter/src/sketch-2d-ops.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/edits.test.ts`). Delivered (research-audit Stage 2b, ZC-018/019/021/022): Trim, Mirror, Linear pattern and Circular pattern, app-side exact geometry folded with Offset into one "More modify tools" rail slot, each one undoable transaction. Trim cuts a line, arc or circle back to the nearest crossings either side of the click (deleting it when nothing crosses), keeps the entity's identity where it can, moves relations on a split's far end to the new piece, drops only those on removed points, and holds each cut end on its cutter. Mirror links the image with symmetric relations (coincident on the axis; equal radius for rounds); an on-axis joint's hold on the axis is kept only when a solve of the candidate on the pinned kernel shows it adds rank, so locks, coincidence chains and axes fixed through dimensions leave no redundant relation (`settleOptionalRelations`). Patterns take spacing and count, or count and total angle (rotated orientation), and tie each instance to the source with equal (plus parallel for linear lines). Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/modify.test.ts`, `test/sketch-modify-tools.test.ts` (solved on the pinned kernel), `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts`. Delivered (research-audit Stage 2b, ZC-014/015/024): Offset is now app-side exact geometry for a circle (radius r ± d; an inward distance that reaches the radius is refused with nothing changed), one line or arc, or, with Chain on, the run of lines and arcs joined end to end with the pick (closed or open, stopping at a branch; construction and regular curves walk separately, so a centre rectangle's diagonals never break its outline). A Miter or Round join is chosen on a strip beside the rail while Offset is armed (Miter default; a corner next to an arc is rounded); overlapping neighbours are trimmed to their crossing. Positive is outward for a closed loop, a circle or a lone arc, otherwise towards the side of the picked curve the click landed on. Every result is witnessed (no point nearer the source than the distance, no self-crossing) or refused. The copies join by coincidence and link back as optional relations (parallel, concentric, round-join centre on the source corner and tangent to its lines) kept only where a solve says they add rank. A 20 × 12 rectangle offset outward by 1 gives exactly 22 × 14 with Miter and radius-1 corner arcs with Round, in one undo step. Make construction / make regular: a Construction toggle on the entity editor flips one existing entity in one undo step; marking one side of a rectangle removes its profile and undo restores it. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/offset.test.ts`, `test/sketch-modify-tools.test.ts` (offset links solve without redundancy on the pinned kernel), `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts`. Open: extend, pattern by total extent, uniform orientation, a persistent pattern relation that also holds instance positions, multi-entity mirror/pattern/construction selections, a driving offset dimension, arc/circle inputs to Fillet/Chamfer, browser coverage of Fillet/Chamfer. Review follow-up: construction-toggle validation failures report the named refusal, preserve the last valid document, release the busy state and leave subsequent edits undoable (`test/e2e/sketch-delete-dependents.spec.ts`). Offset clearance now uses analytic minima over complete finite line/arc pairs; `offset.test.ts` covers narrow non-adjacent line and arc intrusions between the former sample locations while preserving valid smaller offsets. | S04 where composite identity is required; product S-5, design-review W15/phase 4. Research-audit Stage 0: deleting sketch geometry runs the same downstream rebuild check as an entity edit and refuses by name when a dependent feature would break (`test/e2e/sketch-delete-dependents.spec.ts`). |

| <a id="r01"></a>R01 | Partial | Datum planes, axes, points and named coordinate systems; define document nodes, source references and failure behavior before implementation. Test upstream edits/deletions and every consuming feature. The 2026-10-16 design defines Offset Plane (canonical/datum/qualified planar-face sources) and parallel Midplane, with Sketch, Split and Mirror consumers, explicit source repair and acceptance tests. Document and exact-build slice (R01-A/B, this PR): schema v19 adds a `datum-plane` feature kind in `featureOrder` (a recorded deviation from the design's separate history union; every other contract stands) with offset and parallel-midplane definitions, `reverseNormal`, and canonical, earlier-datum and lineage-plus-centroid face sources; sketches, Split and Mirror take a datum reference resolved at their history position by both the Remus build and the OCCT parity reference through one pure construction module, failing closed with the datum named; deleting a used datum, pointing at a later one, and a reorder that would replay a sketch before its datum are refused. Regression evidence: `datum-plane-geometry.test.ts`, `planeBasis.test.ts`, the datum cases in `affectedFeatureTargets.test.ts` and `boolean-evolution-lazy-probe.test.ts`, and `datum-plane-feature.test.ts` (derived frames, offset 10/0/−4, chained and reversed datums, midplane, nonparallel refusal, split and mirror volumes, edits through the prefix cache, suppression, delete/edit/reorder refusals, v19 boundary). The exact build publishes each resolved datum frame in the derived state, which the browser uses to display and re-enter datum-attached sketches, and edit preflight walks datum dependents. Interaction slice (R01-C): a Plane tool (Create fold) opens an Offset Plane / Midplane card with named source selects (principal planes, earlier datums, the picked qualified planar face), an expression offset and Flip normal; Done commits one `feature.datum-plane` command only after an exact preflight shows the datum's frame resolved, and reopening a datum from History or Items edits it in place, validated against every sketch, Split and Mirror built on it. Split and Mirror cards offer a Datum plane picker that stores the id (numeric fields hidden while one is chosen); the sketch plane prompt offers each resolved datum; Items gains a Planes filter with eye toggles, source and dependent counts; History rows show the offset or midplane, and History details say what the plane is built from; the viewport draws each resolved, shown datum as a non-pickable translucent quad. Regression evidence: `datumPlanes.test.ts`, `DatumPlaneForm.test.tsx`, the replay and forward-reference cases in `datum-plane-feature.test.ts`, and `datum-planes.spec.ts` (create at 8, split halves z 0–8/8–20, mirror and sketch by id, edit to 12 moves the split, undo/redo, reload). AI operations slice (R01-D, this PR): behind the dark `AI_PATCH_DATUM_PLANE_ENABLED` flag the assistant gains `add_datum_plane` (offset plane or midplane from principal planes, digest datums or same-proposal `$datum` aliases), `add_datum_sketch` and `add_datum_mirror`, compiled to the same datum, sketch and mirror commands the Plane tool uses with ids minted up front so later operations reference a datum before it runs; plain datum ids must name a live datum plane in the digest. Regression evidence: `ai-datum-plane-ops.test.ts` (strict-output parsing, four refusals, digest check, a parameter-driven datum padded and mirrored through the exact build, parameter edit moving the pad, one-step undo), the datum cases in `assistant.test.ts` and `describe.test.ts`, and the pinned flag list. Viewport slice (this PR): clicking a datum plane where nothing in the model is under the pointer selects it (a translucent quad through the part never takes a click meant for a face behind it); a selected offset datum with a plain-number offset shows the face-offset pin on its resolved origin along its resolved normal, and dragging it fills the open Plane card's Offset (signed for a reversed datum, expressions left alone) for the card's own validated Apply; an offset plane whose source face no longer resolves offers "Re-pick source face…" in its history menu, which re-attaches it to the picked planar face, keeping its distance and direction, through one validated, undoable edit. Regression evidence: `datumPlaneRig.test.ts`, `datumPlanes.test.ts`, `DatumPlaneForm.test.tsx`, `datum-face-repair.test.ts` (a datum broken by deleting its block, re-picked onto another block's top) and `datum-plane-viewport.spec.ts` (drag, Apply, viewport pick, undo). Follow-up slice (this PR): the selected plane and its pin are drawn where the open Plane card's Offset would put them, following the pin during a drag, staying there after release and moving with a typed number until Apply rebuilds it (an expression, a flipped normal or a changed source falls back to the built frame); a midplane whose face source no longer resolves offers "Re-pick source face A…" and "B…" for each face source, re-attaching only that one through the same validated, undoable edit (a re-pick that would collapse the midplane onto its other source is refused); and behind the same dark flag the assistant gains `add_datum_split`, cutting a body at a datum with `localId`/`secondLocalId` aliases for the two halves, compiled to the Split command the card uses. Regression evidence: the draft-offset case in `DatumPlaneForm.test.tsx`, the slot refusal in `datumPlanes.test.ts`, the midplane case in `datum-face-repair.test.ts`, the split cases in `ai-datum-plane-ops.test.ts` (halves z 0–8/8–20, the back half mirrored by alias, a double split refused), the datum cases in `describe.test.ts` and `assistant.test.ts`, and the live-follow steps in `datum-plane-viewport.spec.ts`. Point-plane slice (#740, schema v25): Three points and Parallel at point planes from typed points or edge-end vertices by lineage, with collinear/coincident refusals; evidence in `datum-plane-point-constructors.test.ts`, `datum-point-planes` parity and e2e specs. Datum-axis slice (this PR, schema v28, additive): a `datum-axis` feature kind resolved once at its history position into a line published as `datumAxes`, with four constructors — through two points (typed or vertex), along a straight edge by lineage (end 0 → end 1), the axis of a cylinder or cone face by lineage (canonical, kernel-neutral sense) and where two planes meet (nA × nB through the point nearest A's origin) — each with Reverse direction; coincident points, curved/closed edges, non-revolution faces, missing sources and parallel planes are refused by name with no line published. Consumers hold the axis by id: a revolve's `axisRef` (axis must lie in the sketch plane, else refused), a circular pattern's `axisRef` (copies turn about the datum line) and a Move's `axisRotation`, created from the new Rotate about axis card and editable on every Move; the Revolve, Circular pattern and Move cards list earlier axes. An Axis tool (Create fold) opens a card that takes typed points, picked edge ends, a picked straight edge, a picked cylinder or two planes, commits only after an exact preflight shows the line resolved, and reopens from History; Items gains an Axes filter, History rows say how each axis is built, the viewport draws resolved axes as non-pickable dashed lines, deleting a used axis (or a plane an axis is built from) is refused with its dependents named, edits walk axis dependents, and a lost edge or face source offers "Re-pick source edge/face…". The Remus build and the OCCT parity reference share the construction rules (`packages/geometry`) and source resolvers (`face-attachment.ts`). Regression evidence: `datum-axis-geometry.test.ts` (two points, edge, canonical surface sense, XZ∩YZ = +Z, offset and oblique intersections on their analytic lines, parallel/antiparallel refusals on both sides of the tolerance, rotation about an axis, revolve-axis-in-plane rule), `datum-axis-feature.test.ts` (v28 round trip and v26 document unchanged, structural refusals, expression points, plane-intersection lines, parallel and coincident refusals, an edge axis following a block edit, a cylinder axis, planar-face and lost-source refusals by name, revolve annuli about axes at x = 0 and x = 20, the out-of-plane refusal, circular patterns about x = 10 (full ring and 90°), a Move turned 180° before its translation and reversed by Reverse direction, an axis edit moving its Move, delete refusals), `datum-axis-sources.test.ts`, the axis case in `boolean-evolution-lazy-probe.test.ts`, `test/parity/datum-axes.spec.ts` (revolves, an edge-axis pattern, a cylinder-axis Move and the parallel refusal in both kernels), `datumAxes.test.ts`, `DatumAxisForm.test.tsx`, `FeatureForms.datumAxes.test.tsx`, `datumAxisRig.test.ts` and `test/e2e/datum-axes.spec.ts` (a Two planes axis revolving a circle, a typed two-point axis turning a block 180° with Rotate about axis and ringing it with a circular pattern, an axis edit moving both, a coincident refusal creating nothing, undo). AI datum geometry slice (this PR): separately negotiated `add_datum_plane_v2` supports qualified face, typed-point and edge-vertex sources for all four shipped plane definitions; `add_datum_axis` supports all four shipped axis constructors. Full-definition plane/axis updates retain feature identity, and datum-axis revolve, circular-pattern and Move operations share the manual commands. Plane/axis aliases are typed and ordered; exact digest source proof, retained historical-source checks, own datum resolution and affected-consumer preflight refuse stale, ambiguous, forward and degenerate proposals atomically. Legacy plane/numeric operation contracts remain unchanged. Evidence: `packages/ai-contracts/src/datum-geometry.test.ts`, `packages/command-system/src/ai-datum-operations.test.ts`, `test/ai-datum-geometry-exact.test.ts`, `aiPatchPreflight.test.ts`, capability negotiation in `assistant.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/ai-datum-geometry.spec.ts` for Preview/Apply, refusal, undo/redo and reopen. Vertex-source recovery is separately in review (#802); broader reference constructors below remain open. Target catalogue from the comparison: planes offset from a face, midplane, through three points, on a curve at a point, perpendicular to an edge at a point, through an edge at an angle, parallel to a face at a point; axes through two points, at two planes' intersection, of a cylinder/cone, along an edge, perpendicular to a face at a point. Open: planes on a curve, perpendicular to an edge and through an edge at an angle; the axis perpendicular to a face at a point; datum points and coordinate systems. For supported attached datums, expose the defining source and qualified dependents through U06; upstream deletion or ambiguity keeps a named broken reference and explicit repair path. No proximity-based rebinding. Datum planes and axes draw in the "solid frame" look: a 32% fill and 2.5 px frame per plane, a 3 px line per axis, a dashed continuation through the part, a name chip on each, the selection hue when selected and a per-theme `--color-datum`; the world axes and origin planes are unchanged (`datumPlaneRig.test.ts`, `datumAxisRig.test.ts`, `stagePalette.test.ts`). Datum vertex repair (PR #802): History re-picks individual or all stored edge-vertex slots of three-point/parallel-at-point planes and two-point axes without changing datum identity, typed points, raw expressions, direction flags or untouched sources. Multiple lost slots stage before one exact-preflighted, undoable feature update. Invalid, ambiguous or closed-edge picks, degenerate geometry, failed affected consumers and stale/cancelled sessions cannot publish partial changes. Evidence: `test/datum-vertex-repair.test.ts`, `test/datum-vertex-repair-exact.test.ts` and `test/e2e/datum-vertex-repair.spec.ts`, covering downstream exact geometry, retry/cancellation, undo/replay/backup/reopen and compact controls. Boundary provenance recovery remains separate. | [Datum geometry plan](docs/plans/datum-geometry-plan.md); Product R-1; K05 for topology-derived references. Canonical datum definitions can proceed independently. |
| <a id="f01"></a>F01   | Open     | Review follow-up: the to-face editor checks a separate exact replay at the extrusion's history position, discards stale replies, and keeps Apply disabled while the check is pending. Downstream translation/rotation, immutable-source and browser edit regressions cover this contract (`test/extrude-to-face-draft.test.ts`, `test/e2e/extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts`). Extrude to-face/to-next/through-all/offset-from-face, start-from-plane and start/end offsets, and per-side draft as bounded slices; extend the inferred add/cut rule to revolve, sweep and loft with one override badge (U01 owns the badge). Preserve blind/two-sided/symmetric replay and explicit operation intent; test moving/deleted targets. Qualification design and pinned-kernel support-function probe for the through-all slice: [F01 through-all qualification](docs/plans/f01-through-all-qualification.md). Through-all slice (this PR): schema v20 adds `endCondition: 'through-all'` with a required `throughAllSide` (forward, backward or both) for a cut with exactly one explicit target; absent stays blind byte-for-byte, and new-body/add, missing target, symmetric or back-distance combinations are refused at entry. Each rebuild projects the target's exact bounds on the resolved sketch normal into a finite span and sweeps it through the existing cut, overlap and coplanar-retry path, so resizing or moving the target moves the cut and nothing derived is stored; a side that misses the target, or a deleted target, refuses by name. The extrude editor offers Extent (Distance / Through all) and Direction for an existing cut, and the history row reads "Through all". Regression evidence: `extrude-through-all.test.ts` (forward/backward/both volumes, a grown and a rotated target, refusals, blind round trip and undo), `ExtrudeForm.test.tsx`, `featureSummary.test.ts` and `extrude-through-all.spec.ts`. Create slice (this PR): choosing Cut while creating an extrude offers the same Extent and Direction, commits with a dormant distance (no distance needs typing) and reports "Cut the region through all of the target."; the operation is never inferred for a through-all cut. Regression evidence: `ExtrudeForm.test.tsx`, `extrudeInference.test.ts` and the create flow in `extrude-through-all.spec.ts`. Intersect slice (ZC-060, this PR): schema v24 adds `operation: 'intersect'`, which keeps only the material the extrusion shares with its one explicit target and consumes that target; it is always explicit, never inferred, and through-all stays cut-only. An unknown stored operation now refuses by name on create, edit and rebuild instead of falling through to the cut arm. The exact builder takes the typed exact intersect, refuses an extrusion that shares nothing (or would leave nothing) by name, and never pierces it; the OCCT parity reference models it as `common` with its target. The extrude card offers Intersect beside Add and Cut. Regression evidence: `solid-result-options.test.ts` (10 × 10 profile through a 10-deep block = 1000 by closed form, consumed target, refusals), `occt-solid-result-options.spec.ts` (both kernels against the same closed form), `ExtrudeForm.test.tsx` and `solid-result-options.spec.ts` (2000 → 1000 with the block consumed). To-face and draft slice (ZC-062, ZC-064, this PR): schema v29 adds `endCondition: 'to-face'` with a required `toFace` (a planar face named by verified lineage, the R01 face-source shape) and an optional `draftAngleDeg`; absent fields are the v28 extrude and load byte for byte. Each rebuild re-finds the face at the feature's history position and derives the distance from the sketch plane to it, so moving the face moves the extent and nothing derived is stored; a deleted, ambiguous, non-planar or no-longer-parallel face, or one lying on the sketch plane, refuses as "Target face is unresolved:" and the feature is skipped. That explicit state shows in the card and the history warning, and a History menu "Re-pick face…" (or the card's Re-pick face) replaces only the face through one validated, undoable edit. Draft tapers a one-sided blind or to-face extrusion about the sketch plane (positive narrows away from it): planar walls through the kernel draft, a single round wall as the exact cone loft between tapered cap circles; mixed curved/straight walls, a taper that closes the section, symmetric/two-sided and through-all refuse by name. A drafted extrude publishes hash-only references. The extrude card offers Extent → To face (any operation) with a viewport face pick, and a Draft angle; dragging the viewport arrow returns the extent to Distance, and the history row reads "To face · 2° draft". The OCCT parity reference resolves the same face through its own lineage and builds OCCT's drafted prism. Regression evidence: `extrude-to-face-draft.test.ts` (closed-form prismatoid, cone frustum, 19.1251 cap, moved/tilted/deleted face, re-pick repair, byte-identical load, edit command), `occt-extrude-options.spec.ts` (both kernels against the closed forms), `stage4-analytic-oracles.test.ts` (swept circle 80π, ruled loft 3066.67), `ExtrudeForm.test.tsx`, `featureSummary.test.ts` and `extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts`. Browser acceptance with U01: a mixed-unit expression such as 5 mm + 2 cm = 25 mm must keep expression intent, resolved units, preview, committed geometry, undo/redo and reopen consistent. Audit cycle and incompatible-dimension refusals without model loss; no grammar or schema expansion is implied by the comparison ([FreeCAD E04](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md#hands-on-evidence)). Audit delivered ([record](docs/reviews/f05-expression-audit-2026-10-02.md), executable in `packages/document-core/src/expression-audit.test.ts`): length/angle unit suffixes (`5 mm + 2 cm` = 25 mm, imperial word and `1' 2"` forms) convert into document units, dimensional mismatches refuse on the existing throw path, and `asin`/`acos`/`atan`/`atan2`/`mod`/`avg`/`sign`/`pi()` are added; a 56-input old-versus-new table pins legacy results bit-identical (this PR). A caller without document units refuses a length suffix rather than assuming millimetres. Typed parameters (Stage 1a, ZC-093/094/096, this PR): schema v21 adds an optional `quantity` (length, angle or number) to each parameter. A typed parameter carries its dimension into every expression that reads it, so `width + 5 mm` and `width + 1 in` evaluate while a length in an angle field, a typed number with units and `width + 5` (a bare number added to a length) are refused on the existing throw path; absent stays the unitless parameter every older document holds, so no document is rewritten or retyped on load. A quantity change is refused, naming the use, when any stored expression that reads the parameter would no longer hold its field's dimension or stay coherent (every expression field is now tagged length/angle/number), and parameters reading it are re-checked the same way. Each unitless parameter is offered the quantity its own units or all of its uses agree on, in the Build sidebar's type picker, and applied only when the owner picks it. A reference cycle is reported as one (`a → b → a`) instead of an unknown identifier. Regression evidence: `packages/document-core/src/typed-parameters.test.ts` (mixed units, untyped behaviour unchanged, field refusals, quantity refusals by field/bare number/dependent parameter, named cycles, suggestions), `test/typed-parameters.test.ts` (exact build of a `width + 5 mm` box, an inch edit, a refused clear and undo), `ParameterRows.test.tsx` and `typed-parameters.spec.ts`. Open: document-unit threading for the remaining modeling helpers/previews; quantity in the AI parameter operations; replay still rounds a count expression that later evaluates to a fraction (ZC-095 advisory). The sketch/extrusion F05/U01 slice now supplies document units to entity fields, History sketch/text editors, text creation and placement, and extrusion summaries, semantic validity checks, active sketch/region rendering, form previews, exact whole-object profiles, extrusion side lineage and two-sided spans; length, angle and dimensionless count fields use the existing typed evaluator and keep authored expressions. Regression evidence: `SketchEntityEditor.test.tsx`, `ExtrudeForm.test.tsx`, `test/sketch-extrude-unit-expressions.test.ts`, `test/parity/sketch-extrude-unit-context.spec.ts` and `test/e2e/sketch-extrude-unit-context.spec.ts` cover equivalent mm/cm/inch geometry, dimensional refusals, preview/commit, Enter/Escape, undo/redo and reopen. This is a caller-integration slice; grammar, parameter schema and document-wide units are unchanged. Delivered 2026-10-11 (G5 in `docs/reviews/design-review-2026-10-11.md`, PR #626; this PR #635): a parameter can be defined from the expression field that first names it. Every `ExprInput` replaces "Unknown identifier" with "`width` is not defined yet · Define width = [last value]" when the only problem is undefined parameter names, decided by the evaluator's own parse with the missing names recorded as it reads them, never by sample values (`undefinedParameterIn` in `apps/web/src/lib/model.ts`, so `sqrt(width - 1000)` still offers `width`); syntax, trailing-token, reserved-name and unit/dimension refusals keep their text. The definition runs the Parameters panel's validated set as its own undo step before the feature, never overwrites an existing name, and Inspector edit drafts survive it. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/model.test.ts`, `ExprInput.test.tsx`, `PrimitiveForm.test.tsx`, `fieldParameterDefinitions.test.ts`, `test/e2e/define-parameter-from-field.spec.ts`. Still open: parameter unit types; a definition and its feature land as two undo steps by decision. 2026-10-11 queue integration: Integrated review #635 and #638: define parameters from expression fields as a separate undo step, parse the full expression before offering a definition, atomically refuse names added during an awaited patch, and resolve primitive unit suffixes in document units in both Remus and the OCCT parity reference. Coverage: model, ExprInput, inline-parameter-definition-race, primitive-unit-expressions and imperial-unit-expressions. 14 October review follow-up TEST-3 (this PR): 121 independently authored field cases across 32 feature, direct-edit and sketch variants now prove parameter reference discovery, deletion refusal, exact rename, source/literal preservation and serialized command replay (`test/parameter-expression-fields.test.ts`). A disposable mutation removing fourteen visitor field keys leaves the existing rename/delete suites green but fails seventeen new cases, reproducing and closing the coverage gap from the [project review](docs/reviews/project-review-2026-10-14.md). Datum planes and axes are covered per definition kind (canonical source offsets and point coordinates), so deleting any datum visitor call fails its cases too. Runtime behavior is unchanged. Pattern count advisory (ZC-095, this PR): entry already refuses a fractional count, and a count expression that later evaluates to one (`seats / 2` after `seats` changes) still rounds as saved documents rely on, but now raises a typed advisory on the pattern naming the value and the rounded count; regression evidence `pattern-count-advisory.test.ts`. | Existing parameter/expression tests; U01 for field behaviour; [comparison §2.8](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md). | <a id="i01"></a>I01 | Partial | Project model edges/silhouettes into sketches, then add spline/ellipse/slot/point entities as individual slices; add offsetting a body edge or edge chain into the active sketch; a projection carries a linked/unlinked choice; preserve source linkage and explicit broken-reference behavior. Delivered: ellipse, elliptical arc, fit spline and control-point spline entities (research-audit Stage 2d, ZC-004/005/010; schema v27). Additive `ellipse` (centre, two radii, rotation, optional parameter range) and `spline` (fit or control method, degree 2/3, points, closed) sketch objects; stored documents are never rewritten. One geometry module derives every curve: a chord-length global interpolation (periodic when closed) and clamped uniform control splines, normalized to exact clamped B-splines with knot insertion for pieces, reversal and Bézier spans. Region analysis splits them against every kind (Newton-refined crossings plus endpoint projection; a tangential touch is not found) with exact Green areas, direction-canonical fingerprints and a self-intersection refusal; regions carry new `ellipse`/`spline` curves. The exact builder lifts true ellipse edges and the computed NURBS through `liftCurve2dToPlane`, cutting a clockwise full ellipse into quarters and a closed spline into halves because the pinned extrude chord-splits any other closed single edge into facets; the OCCT parity reference cuts the same pieces. Viewport: one curve rail slot (O ellipse, S spline, again steps the type) with Ellipse/Arc/Fit/Control tiles; ellipse is centre, axis end, other radius (then arc start and end); spline is clicks, Enter or a second click on the last point to finish, a click on the first point to close. GCS: an ellipse is a kernel ellipse held by its centre (coincident, lock, point-on-ellipse, concentric, equal radii); an open spline exposes its end points; interior points carry no relation. The entity card edits radii/rotation/arc range and each spline point; modify tools, revolve, sweep paths and the assistant refuse the new kinds by name. Sketch SVG writes elliptical-arc and per-span Q/C commands with an exact extent; sketch DXF (R12) refuses both by name, since R12 has no ELLIPSE or SPLINE. Evidence: `test/sketch-curve-entities.test.ts` (fit passes through its points with bit-exact ends, closed fit is C2 through its seam, πab regions rotated or not, half ellipse and quarter segment closed forms, circle hole in an ellipse, line crossing, spline closed/open/split areas, self-intersection, stable fingerprints), `test/sketch-curve-extrude.test.ts` (πab·h, taller-than-wide restatement, half and 220° arcs, ellipse and spline hole loops that a following box subtract accepts, closed spline as two walls), `test/parity/occt-reference/occt-curve-entities.spec.ts` (both kernels against the oracle with equal face counts), `test/sketch-curve-constraints.test.ts` (centre coincident, point on ellipse, concentric+equal, spline ends joined with interior points untouched, refusals, v27 round trip, sketch move), `test/sketch-svg-export.test.ts`, `test/sketch-dxf-export.test.ts`, web unit tests for the tools, card, rail and constraint picking, and `test/e2e/sketch-curve-tools.spec.ts`. Open: spline curve constraints (tangency, point-on-spline; Remus B75), trim/offset/mirror/pattern of the new curves, DXF R2000 ELLIPSE/SPLINE, slots, points and projection. Delivered: sketch projection with a linked/unlinked choice (research-audit Stage 2d, ZC-013; schema v33). An additive `projection` record on line, arc, circle and ellipse objects stores the source body and edge by schema-v5 lineage plus `linked` or `unlinked`; stored documents are never rewritten. The sketch rail's Project tool (U; its strip picks Linked or Unlinked) projects a picked model edge, or every boundary edge of a picked face, along the sketch normal. It follows the Construction toggle like every other drawing tool. Straight edges become lines. Circles and arcs become circles and arcs when parallel to the sketch, and exact ellipses and elliptical arcs when tilted. A line along the normal, a circle seen edge-on and any other curve kind are refused by name. Linked: at the sketch's history position the exact build re-finds each edge through verified lineage only (no hash match, no proximity). Every later feature in that build reads the re-projected geometry, so a downstream extrude follows an upstream resize in the same rebuild. The state is published as `derived.sketchProjections` (current, moved or broken). A move is written back to the object as a normalization with no undo step of its own. Line ends keep their names when an edge comes back reversed. The solver holds linked objects fixed, and drag, trim, fillet and chamfer refuse them until they are unlinked. Broken (source deleted, unavailable at the sketch's place in the history, or no longer the same curve kind): the stored geometry, which is the last valid projection, keeps building, and the sketch carries an advisory. Repair is an explicit Re-pick source on the entity card. It is validated by a rebuild before it commits and keeps the object id. Unlink instead keeps the geometry as an independent copy. A new linked pick is refused when its edge does not exist at the sketch's place in the history. Unlinked: copied once, ordinary editable geometry, source kept as provenance. U06: the Objects sketch row counts its projections and says when one is broken. Each projected entity is listed under it with its source feature and its linked/independent/broken role. The History row of a sketch with a broken projection is flagged. Evidence: `test/sketch-projection.test.ts` (exact kernel: a pick re-projects to exactly what it stored; a linked outline follows w 20→32 with the extrude at 32·10·3 in the same rebuild, then settles current after write-back; reversed line ends; unlinked never moves; a deleted source is broken with the last valid 25·10·3 plate still built and four advisories; an orphaned lineage refuses; history-position refusal; exact rim circle; v33 round trip and malformed-record refusals), `test/sketch-projection-geometry.test.ts` (closed-form line, circle, arc-side, tilted-circle r·cos θ ellipse and elliptical-arc oracles, refusals, tolerance and end-order rules), `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/projection.test.ts`, `ObjectsPanel.projections.test.tsx`, `HistoryTimeline.test.tsx`, `SketchEntityEditor.test.tsx`, `SketchToolRail.test.tsx`, `machine.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/sketch-projection.spec.ts` (projects a box's top face linked, widens the box from History and sees the stored outline follow, deletes the box and sees broken in Objects, History and the card, then unlinks). Remaining: silhouette projection of curved faces, spline and ellipse edge projection, and offsetting a body edge chain into a sketch. Constrained ordinary geometry now follows refreshed linked projections during exact history rebuilds. Every linked entity stays fixed, including current and broken links at their retained positions; only a successful solve publishes overrides. Unsatisfied relations refuse dependent features and affected exports without partial projection write-back (`test/sketch-projection-constraints.test.ts`). Cold rebuild, undo, replay, verified edge lineage, exact-volume and SVG/DXF export regressions cover the lifecycle. Solved ordinary neighbours also reach the sketch viewport through a project/version-qualified session view, so entity cards, endpoint snaps, point measurements and closed-profile picks agree with the rebuilt solid and exports. The view preserves authored values/history, is omitted from persistence, and is retained across geometry-preserving edits only after canonical geometry equivalence is proved. `test/e2e/sketch-projection-constraints.spec.ts` checks these reads after resize and reopen without a manual solve. Unlinking the last projection retains the solved shape in one undoable, replayable transaction; driver releases keep unchanged typed expressions. Exact regressions cover 576 volume after release, rollback without partial writes, undo/redo, cold replay/reopen/export, and an independent typed-height edit producing 672 volume. Saved linked projections demand Boolean lineage independently of transient selection, both through their producer reference and through a descendant source body. Exact-kernel cold reopen and cached rebuild regressions preserve the saved document, source reference and volume, while unlinked and unrelated controls retain lazy behavior (`boolean-evolution-lazy-probe.test.ts`, `sketch-projection.test.ts`). An explicit line re-pick retains the stored start/end orientation and construction role, preserving constrained endpoint identity (`apps/web/src/lib/sketch/projection.test.ts`). Projection source admission now requires the exact resolver's positive, canonical, hash-consistent edge reference; malformed references fail before native changes, account saves or HTTP/WebSocket collaboration storage. `sketch-projection-reference-validation.test.ts` covers zero/mismatched hashes, reversed witness endpoints, invalid closed axes and unsupported closed witnesses, unchanged storage, valid open/closed/null-axis controls and frozen ADR-011 hash values. The existing fingerprint algorithm and signature field order are shared with the kernel rather than changed; no valid document format, tolerance or schema version changes. Open circular edges published with only two tessellation endpoints are refused before a sketch copy is proposed: an endpoint cannot select the short versus complementary arc. Geometry and browser-pick regressions cover shallow parallel and tilted arcs in both directions, genuine interior-sample short-sweep controls, unchanged unlinked documents on refusal, and proven full circles with only closing endpoints. This narrows unsupported input; it does not fit or approximate an arc or change tolerances. CadPatch single and multi-profile extrusions resolve their sample points against the same project/version-qualified solved sketch view advertised in the assistant digest. Only reads use the session view; commands, authored expressions and history keep their original base. Append-only consumers and new or unchanged parameter bindings retain that qualified read snapshot. Qualified reads are tracked per sketch through its earlier feature, body, datum and linked-object dependencies, including in-place Moves. Renames and unrelated sketch/feature dimensions preserve them; edits to the sketch or its transitive geometry sources require an exact rebuild before profile selection. Existing parameter and structural edits conservatively require that rebuild. Exact-kernel regressions cover points outside the old outline, overlapping old/new outlines with independent area/volume oracles, repeated consumers, unchanged authored nodes, undo/redo/replay and wrong-project/old-version refusal controls. Consumers that only need a sketch id keep their existing command semantics and do not require profile-geometry reads. A selected body still exports alone. `lib/exportScope.test.ts`, `ExportDialog.test.tsx`. An Export units preference (default millimetres, so existing exports are unchanged) scales STEP, which declares inches as a 25.4 mm `CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT`; 3MF, through its unit attribute; DXF, through `$INSUNITS`; and the bare numbers of STL, OBJ and PLY. GLB stays in metres and SVG keeps its physical size. The mesh dialog adds Max facet angle: the kernel's 20° default sends nothing new, and a tighter angle re-meshes through the indexed-mesh translator. Its check reports the triangle count and the deviation measured against the exact faces. `docs/exchange-loss-contract.md` states what STEP, STL, 3MF, OBJ, PLY, GLB, DXF and SVG each keep and drop, and `test/exchange-round-trip.test.ts` checks every line through the shared harness `test/support/exchange-harness.ts`, including a 25.4 mm STEP block that round-trips as 1 in. | <a id="i05"></a>I05 | Partial  | Reference images: place PNG/JPG (first PDF page through the existing rasterizer) on a plane or face with a gizmo for scale/move/rotate and an opacity slider, listed as an item, usable as a tracing underlay in sketches and as a split tool later. Stored as an artifact, not in the document body. Research-audit Stage 6 slice (this PR, schema v32): the Create fold's Image tool places a PNG or JPEG (sniffed by content, at most 20 MB and 8192 px a side) on Top (XY), or on the flat face selected when it opens; its card moves it to any principal plane (with offset), a resolved datum plane (followed by id) or the selected face (followed by lineage, or a fixed frame for a face without identity), sets position U/V, rotation, width (height follows the aspect) and opacity as a live draft applied in one undoable edit, and scales it from two points picked on the picture and a known distance. Objects lists images with show/hide (view state, like datums), an Images filter and name search. The record lives in `ProjectDocument.referenceImages` and holds only a SHA-256 reference: the bytes go to the content-addressed source blob store and a `reference-image` artifact (PNG/JPEG only, checked at upload-session creation and finalize), never inline, and a browser without blob storage is refused. Images are never features or bodies, are excluded from the geometry rebuild key, share snapshots and diagnostics, are never raycast and sit outside the PNG export's allowlist; STEP/mesh/drawing exports cannot see them. Backups carry the picture as a source and refuse one without it; blob cleanup keeps every picture the document or its undo history names. A device-only picture (local project, or a failed upload) counts as a local-only source and is archived, with its id rewritten through history, by the next account save or local-project adoption; a duplicate or branch caches the source's pictures and holds them under device-local ids instead of depending on the source project's artifact, and an account duplicate uploads them into the new project and stores the rewritten copy before reporting it made. An insert is validated in full (including the 64-image cap) before any byte is stored or uploaded, and a texture that could not load retries when the image's artifact id changes. Regression evidence: `test/reference-images.test.ts` (schema, commands, undo/replay, validation at load and the account edge, history snapshots, geometry key, share/diagnostics, upload content types, two-point scale), `referenceImageDisplay.test.ts`, `referenceImageRig.test.ts`, `referenceImageInsert.test.ts`, `ReferenceImageForm.test.tsx`, `ObjectsPanel.images.test.tsx`, `projectBackupTransfer.test.ts` and `test/e2e/reference-images.spec.ts`. Remaining: a viewport gizmo for move/rotate/scale (fields only today), PDF first-page import, sketch tracing snaps and split-by-image, share-link delivery of pictures, and a server-side artifact copy for account duplicates (today the duplicating device uploads the pictures). | U06 items; the pdf.js rasterizer already used for assistant ingestion; [comparison §2.9](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md).                                                                                              |

## File interoperability

This lane has its own bounded sequence within the delivery milestones above:
complete [D04](#d04) DXF sketch exchange, [I07](#i07) SVG and [I06](#i06)
IGES, alongside [I02](#i02) STEP fidelity. Qualify [I10](#i10) before selecting
native-CAD implementations; the first package is [I11](#i11) Parasolid,
[I12](#i12) ACIS, [I13](#i13) Rhino and [I14](#i14) SolidWorks part import.
Inventor/JT follow demonstrated demand; Creo/NX/CATIA/Solid Edge are retained
as later targets. [I08](#i08) PLY export is a small independent slice; PDF
remains [D01](#d01), and [I09](#i09) USDZ is deferred visualization work.

Import means qualified geometry and supported metadata, not recovered source
feature history. Per-format direction/version claims require fixtures; native
assembly references must resolve explicitly, and mesh-only input cannot become
an exact solid merely by translation. Details and source evidence live in the
[format specification](docs/plans/file-format-interoperability.md); this table
is the only delivery-status ledger. Existing I04 owns export controls; I07 owns
SVG geometry, replacing the previously unnamed SVG slice in D04/I04.

Delivered (export half, ZC-190, with the ZC-192 view PNG): File › Export image… (also in the command palette) writes one saved sketch as SVG through the D04 sketch extraction, so it refuses the same sketches by the same names (text, construction-only, stale attachment…); lines, circles and arcs stay exact `<line>`/`<circle>`/elliptical-arc `<path>` elements (never sampled) in DOCUMENT units, with v negated for SVG's y-down axis, a viewBox over the exact curve extent (arc axis extremes included) plus half a 0.25 mm stroke, and a physical width/height that opens at 1:1 (metres stated in millimetres); construction geometry is left out and a transparent-background option drops the white backing rect. SVG review follow-up: normalize finite arc starting angles before bounded quadrant checks, and retain nearly full turns with two arcs when their formatted endpoints coincide. Browser downloads prove both cases remain visible. PNG rendering preserves per-material tone mapping, including the unmodified backdrop; a native-resolution browser comparison fails by 16 colour steps before the fix and matches within one rounding step afterwards. A second-sketch History selection defaults the dialog to that sketch and downloads its distinct geometry; the browser regression fails against the prior default. Selected-body images also exclude uncommitted Move-copy projections; a viewport regression checks capture scope and restores their visibility afterwards. PNG capture also suspends sketch/profile selection and hover styling, including profile boundaries and pick markers, and restores the exact live styles after success or renderer failure. The same dialog renders the current view as a PNG at a chosen size (View, 2× view, 1920×1080, 3840×2160 or typed, up to 8192 px a side and 40 MP) offscreen on the live renderer — MSAA render target, then the renderer's own tone mapping and sRGB encoding with alpha un-premultiplied — and restores the camera, visibility and clear state in the same synchronous call, so the live canvas never presents a capture frame; a wider/narrower image widens the frame rather than cropping it, gizmos/rigs/measurements and selection glow are left out, the grid is optional, and the background can be transparent. Scope follows #707: a selected body draws alone, otherwise the visible bodies with hidden ones counted as left out. The PNG is written by an in-app encoder (8-bit RGBA, IHDR/IDAT/IEND only, so no metadata) to keep straight alpha exact. Desktop saves accept `.svg`/`.png`. Evidence (this PR): `test/sketch-svg-export.test.ts` (lines/arcs/circles, sweep and large-arc flags, arc bounds, units incl. inch and metre, background, adapter refusals), `apps/web/src/lib/pngEncode.test.ts` (size, colour type, CRCs, exact straight alpha), `apps/web/src/components/viewer/viewportImage.test.ts` (aspect fit keeps the view's extent, restore), `apps/web/src/lib/imageExport.test.ts` (size limits, scope), `ImageExportDialog.test.tsx`, `geometryWorker.test.ts` (svg route) and `image-export.spec.ts` (downloads a 640×360 transparent PNG with transparent corners, opaque body pixels and an unchanged live canvas, an all-opaque PNG, and the imported slot's SVG with exact arcs and 1:1 size). ## 3. Drawings

| ID                  | Status   | Outcome and completion evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Depends on / legacy item                                                                                      |

| <a id="d01"></a>D01 | Partial | [Drawing MVP design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md) records the contract and this slice's decisions; Peter chose the Draw workspace mode and accepted both departures (2026-10-19): the fingerprint fence with explicit Update instead of checkpoint replay, and worker-side visibility against the display tolerance, labelled approximate, instead of `projectEdges`. Canonical drawing tessellation (follow-up to #753): views no longer project from the viewport's display mesh, which holds an earlier deflection through edits within 25 % of it while a cold reload re-meshes at the nominal value (so a sheet could read ready after a reload yet export different bytes). A `drawing-tessellation` geometry-worker request meshes each sheet body at its nominal display deflection for its own exact bounds and puts faces, triangles, vertices and edge polylines in an order that depends only on their coordinates, so checkpoint replay, a full rebuild and a cold reload give the same arrays; the Draw workspace caches it per fingerprint, so a hidden-line toggle does not mesh again. The fingerprint is unchanged (no tessellation signature, existing sheets stay ready): the canonical mesh is a function of the exact geometry it already fences. The display path is untouched; a drawing request leaves the next sync's checkpoint restore and measurement reuse unchanged. Evidence: `test/drawing-canonical-tessellation.test.ts` (a sheet exported in a session after a held-deflection parameter edit and after a checkpoint-replayed edit, then rebuilt cold in a fresh kernel: byte-identical PDF and SVG, while the display-mesh input demonstrably differs; refusal of a consumed body; permutation invariance), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (projects from the requested tessellation, meshed once across a hidden-line toggle; a refusal shows after two retries), and `test/e2e/drawings.spec.ts` "exports the same bytes after a reload re-meshes the model" (R20→R21 disc, export, browser reload, export: identical bytes; fails when fed display meshes). Foundation slice delivered (research-audit Stage 7, ZC-139–142, 164; schema v30): `ProjectDocument.drawings` holds sheets with ISO A4–A0 or ANSI A–D paper and orientation, a shared integer scale, first/third projection angle and title-block text (title, drawing number, revision, author, date; scale, sheet, units and projection fill from the sheet), and per-view orientation (six principal views or the true isometric), bodies, paper-mm centre, hidden-line toggle and an aligned base for projected views. A new **Draw** workspace mode (sheets in a left column, the sheet canvas over the stage with the viewer kept mounted, properties on the right; lazy chunk) makes a four-view starter scaled to fit (front base, top and side folded on the projection angle's sides, isometric free), moves views by drag, fields or arrow keys with projected views kept on their base's row/column, and exports a sheet as dependency-free vector PDF (base-14 Helvetica, WinAnsi; other characters become "?") or SVG in millimetres, byte-stable across reload. Projection runs in its own worker from the canonical drawing tessellation: kernel-sampled edge polylines plus curved-face silhouettes, occlusion against every body's triangles (approximate to the display tolerance) — the pinned `projectEdges` tests occlusion only locally and draws no silhouettes, recorded as a diagnostic test. Model fence: each sheet stores a fingerprint of its bodies' persistent topology hashes and extents; an edited model shows a stale state, draws outlines only and refuses export until an explicit Update (undoable). Evidence: `test/drawing-projection.test.ts` (box principal-view bounds, isometric far-corner hidden runs, cylinder silhouettes, U-part and two-body occlusion, drilled-plate bore), `test/drawing-sheets.test.ts` (schema, validation on load and at the account edge, commands with undo/redo/replay, alignment, fingerprint, 1:2 and inch transform, starter layout, PDF/SVG structure and reload byte-stability), `test/e2e/drawings.spec.ts`. Open: checkpoint-snapshot replay of an old model (the fence refuses instead), per-view scale and custom view angles, exact silhouettes (Remus ask, ZC-145), drawing on share links; title-block layouts and D02 annotations stay with D02. Production QA drawing fixes: views are flagged by their whole footprint and export is refused while one runs off the paper, with an undoable "Fit views to sheet"; the composed page and PDF are clipped to the paper; the title block prints a full ISO date; ⌀ and other common symbols print as Latin-1 stand-ins with a warning for the rest; the sheet zooms and pans; view labels are documented as editor-only (`test/drawing-sheets.test.ts`, `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx`). | Design sheet/view document model first; product D-1. No dependency on finishing every M-row. |
| <a id="d02"></a>D02 | Partial | Associative drawing dimensions, centerlines, notes, title blocks and hole callouts; model edits update or visibly invalidate annotations. Comparison decisions: refresh on an explicit Update command with visible staleness is the accepted minimum; the dimension set is line length, point-to-point, point-to-line, line-to-line, arc angle, 3-point angle, line-to-line angle, radius, diameter and min/max distance, each with prefix/suffix and a tolerance (symmetrical, deviation, limits, basic); geometry annotations are 2-point, 2-line, 3-point and circular centerlines, center marks and intersection marks that also serve as dimension references; notes and sheet images; units, angle format, precision, decimal separator and per-class line widths are drawing properties. First handoff slice: place a dimension on a D01 view, preserve its placement/formatting through offline reopen, and update or visibly invalidate it after an explicit source rebind before PDF export. Invalid annotation references retain presentation state and offer explicit qualified repair/re-pick, never nearest-edge rebinding. Title-block fields (title, units, scale, projection angle, sheet size, date) fill automatically until edited. Layouts, fields and repair remain implementation scope, not delivered design evidence ([FreeCAD E06](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md#hands-on-evidence)). First slice delivered (research-audit Stage 7, ZC-146/163; schema v31; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#implementation-decisions-d02-annotations-schema-v31)): `DrawingSheet.annotations` holds horizontal, vertical and aligned two-point dimensions, radius, diameter and angle dimensions, and text notes on the paper or in a view with an optional leader. Each stores only the edges it measures (body id, ADR-011 hash and ADR-013 persistent reference, resolved lineage-first through the fail-closed `resolveEdge` ladder) and its placement relative to its view, never a value: values are read from exact edge vertices and published circle data each time a ready sheet is drawn, so a stale sheet shows none until its explicit Update, and a parameter-widened box reads its new width after Update. A reference that resolves to no edge or to several, a body no longer in the view, a circle now edge-on or edges now parallel keep the annotation in place, marked broken with a reason; export is refused until it is re-picked (placement and format kept) or deleted, and nothing rebinds to a nearby edge. Picking offers only edges the view draws, prefers the nearer of coincident edges and refuses an edge whose identity another shares; aligned spans and angles that slope into a principal view, and every length or angle on the isometric view, are refused rather than read foreshortened (horizontal and vertical spans along a model axis stay exact). Dimension strokes, filled arrowheads and rotated text go through the one page model into the preview, PDF and SVG; a sheet without annotations exports byte-identical to D01. Evidence: `test/drawing-annotations.test.ts` (schema pin and additivity, refusals on load and at the account edge, commands with undo/redo/replay, view delete, horizontal/vertical/aligned/angle/radius/diameter values on real kernel bodies, lineage survival across a parameter edit, broken reasons, picking, iso refusal, layout, PDF/SVG text and reload byte-stability), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (drawn value, broken state with export refusal and Re-pick, stale sheet, keyboard move/delete, Escape) and `test/e2e/drawing-dimensions.spec.ts` (pick and place horizontal, vertical and leader note on a box; delete/undo; SVG/PDF contents; widen → stale with no values → Update → new value; reload). Open: tolerances, prefix/suffix, centrelines/centre marks as references, point-to-line and min/max distances, title-block layouts, hole callouts and drawing-level unit/precision properties. Finishing-body repair (PR #890): an explicit, undoable sheet Update follows unique one-to-one hole/fillet/chamfer/shell/draft/offset results, retaining subshape witnesses, annotation placement and export guards. Additive Thicken results are excluded. Missing, ambiguous and removed edges still require repair. Draw-mode revision saves use the drawing permission guard; new-sheet controls wait for ready geometry. Documents use schema-v39 (built on v38) to fence replay from older clients that would ignore body replacements. Tests: `test/drawing-body-succession.test.ts`, `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx`, `design-review-fixes.spec.ts`. Production verification remains pending. | D01, dimension semantics from S01/S02; M01 for hole metadata. Product D-2. |

Delivered slices: exact section curves export as DXF (#342) and the writer refuses non-finite/oversized coordinates and zero-wire input by name (#349; `test/dxf-writer.test.ts`). The first whole-sketch export contract is now source-grounded in [`docs/plans/sketch-dxf-export-plan.md`](docs/plans/sketch-dxf-export-plan.md): exact local-plane LINE/CIRCLE/ARC output, exact rectangle/polygon line expansion, millimetre conversion, construction exclusion, and fail-closed unsupported/text/plane cases. SVG import/export is owned by I07. Runtime whole-sketch export is delivered in the adapter and worker: `exportSketchDxf` lowers one saved sketch to exact local-plane LINE/CIRCLE/ARC in millimetres through the existing `writeDxf`, expands rectangles/polygons to their edge lines, excludes construction geometry, and refuses by name (`sketch-not-found`, `parameters-invalid`, `stale-plane-attachment`, `invalid-plane-frame`, `unsupported-object`, `invalid-geometry`, `construction-only`, `writer-refused`) (this PR; `test/sketch-dxf-export.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/sketchDxf.test.ts`). DXF sketch import (this PR): an ASCII DXF imports through Import CAD files or a drop as one undoable sketch on Top (XY) named after the file. LINE, CIRCLE and ARC map exactly; LWPOLYLINE/POLYLINE segments become lines, and their bulges the arcs they describe; `$INSUNITS` (inch, foot, mm, cm, m and smaller units) converts into the document units, and a file declaring none is read in the document units and says so. Annotations and blocks (TEXT, MTEXT, DIMENSION, HATCH, INSERT…) are left out and counted, curves a sketch has no exact object for (ELLIPSE, SPLINE, 3D polylines, entities off the XY plane) are refused and counted, and binary DXF, a missing ENTITIES section, malformed group codes, unknown units, more than 2,000 curves or over 20 MB refuse the file by name. Review hardening (#723): reject every nonzero elevation (including legacy vertices), refuse curve/spline-fit parent flags and generated fitted/control vertices before expansion, and require positive CIRCLE/ARC radii. Regression evidence: `test/dxf-import.test.ts` (round trip through the sketch DXF writer, bulge direction, a slot closing into one region, units, counts, refusals, signed/tiny elevations, fitted flags/vertices and zero/negative radii) and `dxf-sketch-import.spec.ts` (inch slot imported and extruded). The sketch menu export entry, flattened views, Bézier/ellipse/spline import and a user choice of units for unitless files remain open. | Sketch entity support; D01 only for drawing-view export. Product D-4; can precede D01 for sketch-only slices. |
| <a id="d05"></a>D05 | Deferred | Drawing dimension audit → reviewed parameter overrides without a fresh proposal; retain measured-vs-inferred provenance.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | D02/F03; product D-5 and former TODO AI follow-up.                                                            |

## Parallel kernel and web reliability lane

These are consumer requirements. Before a Remus implementation task, refresh its
own ledger and the exact OpenZCAD pin: inherited P-Class numbers and old defect
reports are not proof that the current upstream kernel is missing an operation.
A kernel merge closes a consumer row only after pin adoption and product tests.
