Delivered: pattern instances carry `pattern.face.instance.N.*` lineage from the kernel's pattern operations with fingerprints unchanged (#331); two-operand booleans read the kernel's entity evolution on a copy and accept it only when the probe body matches the shipped one on face/edge counts, types and volume, so faces and edges downstream of a boolean carry named provenance (#340); a `modified` edge whose exact witness is unchanged now carries its source like a `preserved` one, because the kernel reports `modified` for an edge whose neighbouring face it re-trimmed (the front corners of a fused L-bracket; `remus-boolean-evolution.test.ts`). The probe now runs on demand: only the viewport's idle rebuild opts into skipping it for a boolean whose result nothing downstream references (exports, previews, demo seeding, AI preflight and the parity corpus keep full lineage), while a face/edge pick or started command on a body raises a transient, never-persisted lineage demand that forces the producing boolean to probe (the demand is part of the history digest and worker cache key), and fillet/chamfer commits re-read the current lineage by hash so a pick made before the probed rebuild still persists a named `boolean.edge.*` reference, failing closed to hash-only (this PR; `test/boolean-evolution-demand.test.ts`, `test/boolean-evolution-lazy-probe.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/topologyCommitRefresh.test.ts`). Face-distance dimension edits carry face references through the kernel's journaled move-faces history: each move reads its source-to-result face map back against the solid the kernel actually built (symmetric moves compose both legs), each claim re-verifies against the source solid's own measured witness, and an independent unchanged-witness derivation runs alongside so a result face the two name differently publishes neither; merges, unbound sources and stale references stay hash-only (#398; `test/face-distance-lineage.test.ts`). Parameter replay now carries uniquely verified native-hole/offset/bore-resize/modifier faces and boundary edges, atomically upgrades legacy picks in Tweak, and retains fail-closed ambiguity checks (`test/stepped-bore-parameter-replay.test.ts`, `test/e2e/parameter-lineage-repair.spec.ts`; [proof and limits](docs/plans/parameter-reference-replay.md)). A bracket of two boxes, a union, an inside-corner fillet and two holes now follows `width` 80 → 120 with no warnings, and so does one with every edge rounded (`test/bracket-width-resize.test.ts`, `remus-boolean-evolution.test.ts`, `exact-kernel-adapter.test.ts`); parity: `boolean-on-nurbs-import` 16 → 22 named edges, `boss-crossing-a-wall` 10 → 14. Pass-through supplies a body only: a reference to topology the suppressed feature made (its fillet band, its chamfer bevel) still refuses with the existing stale/missing-face sentence. Optional mass properties now run on Inspector demand, and accepted union display meshes are reused within a bounded per-sync cache; [geometry latency evidence](docs/geometry-latency.md) records the installed-package comparison. Kernel byte ceiling step 1 (2026-10, paired with esaueng/remus#1028): raw 11 MiB review / 12 MiB hard, gzip 4.5 MiB hard, Brotli 3 MiB review, per-pin growth review unchanged; seven-point evidence and runway in the [ceiling change record](docs/kernel-wasm-size-policy.md#ceiling-change-2026-10-step-1). Launcher font-parser split (2026-10-14 project review PERF-3): opentype.js left the preloaded `geometry` chunk (296,291 → 58,131 B raw; start-screen transfer 558,128 → 490,776 B and FCP 990 → 928 ms at 4× CPU, 9 Mbps/150 ms RTT), and `report-bundle-sizes --check` now fails when a launcher chunk carries it (`test/bundle-size-policy.test.ts`). 14 October review follow-up PERF-4: the entry chunk is within a few hundred bytes of its budget and App.tsx is about half of it, because the start screen loads the whole workspace; split a thin launcher and preload the workspace on idle or hover ([project review](docs/reviews/project-review-2026-10-14.md)). The device-only library paint shown before account discovery now uses the same pinned-then-custom-order comparator as the merged listing, so reloading no longer draws cards in storage key order and reshuffles them when the account listing lands. Projects only the account holds still appear when that listing arrives. Coverage: `localProjectStore.test.ts` (saved shelf order, not key order). Library startup publishes existing device summaries while account discovery continues, then returns the cloud listing before encrypted card metadata has finished. Names and thumbnail references still pass the existing signature, epoch, authority and rollback checks, with four verifications in flight and session-scoped, batched presentation updates. Health, authentication config, settings, collaboration config and speculative chunk prefetch no longer block the initial library. Selecting a project immediately shows its named loading screen with cancellation; document reconciliation still precedes hydration, and the file count loads afterward. Regression coverage includes 150 encrypted cards, delayed optional services, cached rows, rejected/stale metadata, local settings changed during discovery, shared viewers awaiting capabilities, cancellation during device writes, superseded navigation and late configuration responses after retries or cloud-mode changes (`progressive-loading.spec.ts`, `apiProjectListing.test.ts`, `progressiveShelf.test.ts`, `projectShelf.test.ts`, `cloudConfigurationReads.test.ts`). Original-account production timing and target-hardware median/p95 qualification remain open. Library refresh preserves cached project names and card order while encrypted metadata is unverified, without changing transport versions, storage formats, organization precedence or late-verification conflict fences. Thumbnails keep their decoded image during refresh and on returning to the library, switch only after the replacement decodes, and retain visibility-based loading. The bounded memory cache scopes protected previews to the account and key session; account changes, key teardown and unsupported formats cannot reuse protected images or accept late results. Regression coverage exercises provisional listings, verified renames, decode failures, empty projects, stale requests, remounts and session invalidation (`PartThumbnail.test.tsx`, `apiProjectListing.test.ts`, `projectShelf.test.ts`, and the smoke-tagged thumbnail continuity journey in `progressive-loading.spec.ts`). Previously verified cloud-only titles are cached as account-scoped device presentation hints, like local document summaries, and appear with the HTTP listing while encrypted metadata is still pending. Pinned and visible cards are verified first; current signatures, authority, rollback checks and document loads remain authoritative. Name hints never change versions, thumbnail references or permissions, and clear at sign-out. Coverage includes 150 cached titles while the encryption account request is held, cache isolation, unavailable storage and newer device renames (`projectShelfNames.test.ts`). Account erasure, sign-out with or without a stored-device choice, and cross-tab key teardown also remove title hints (`accountErasure.test.ts`, `signOut.test.ts`, `encryption-session.spec.ts`). Target-hardware median/p95 qualification remains open. Startup readiness signals (production QA F021–F023): `oz:model-visible` and `oz:input-ready` User Timing marks mark the first exact model frame and the first fully analysed frame per opened project (`startupReadiness.test.ts`). `scripts/measure-startup.mjs` reports them with long tasks, LCP and network over N production-build runs, warm or cold, with optional throttling and per-task trace attribution. The warm Heat Sink reload's long tasks are the viewer's PMREM environment and first-frame shader links, not main-thread WASM. They do not delay readiness on the measured laptop. LCP is documented as no workspace readiness metric ([performance baseline](docs/performance-baseline.md#readiness-marks)). The lazy-chunk prefetch skips chunks the page already requested and waits for the model (`lazyChunkPrefetch.test.ts`). Target-GPU budgets remain open. Deployment tooling qualification: the root declares the same Wrangler 4.147.0 already installed for the web package, so Workers Builds' default root-level `npx wrangler versions upload` resolves the lockfile CLI rather than downloading a different version. The deploy-safety smoke regression runs that root command offline with an empty npm cache. Readiness and prefetch tests also run in unit smoke CI. Provider asset-upload success is verified separately from a passing build and dry run. | Kernel W3/W4; ADR-015 and [performance record](docs/performance-baseline.md). Exact-delta and runner-profile regressions retain every other reviewed workflow byte; activation and real queue verification remain pending. |

The unchanged scenario passed 30 local repetitions, including the CI worker configuration and a temporary CPU-throttled diagnostic, so its hosted failure's exact timing cause remains unconfirmed. With the explicit readiness assertions, 20 no-retry CI-configured repetitions and all three copy browser cases passed locally. The four Apply/Cancel transition checks and all copy-state and body-count assertions remain intact, with unchanged deadlines and retry configuration. The flaky-test budget and fleet rollout qualification remain open. The disposable fleet Git fixture dispatches ordinary reads directly to real Git, avoiding a Python interpreter launch for each operation. Delayed fetches retain the same Python implementation, and the dispatcher retains the missing immutable object guard before preparation. Offline, stale-pin, main-ancestry, exact-fetch count and real Vitest control/prepared assertions remain intact. This addresses interpreter overhead observed alongside a Hosted cold-evidence test timeout and deadline-control setup failure. A serial local comparison reduced the cold regression from 3.31 to 2.71 seconds and the nested deadline experiment from 7.83 to 7.53 seconds; those measurements do not guarantee Hosted timing. The five-second test, ten-second setup, nine-second child-process limit and six-second delayed-fetch control are unchanged, as are active workflow pins and runtime routing. Focused verification passes all 34 policy/history tests. Required local lint, type checking, root tests (7,312 passed, eight skipped), web tests (3,828 passed), parity corpus and build pass. The Python 3.9 fixture and the direct/queue policy suites also pass. Real shallow Git-history fixtures complement the fleet setup deadline regression: cold and partly warm checkouts retain a distinct candidate HEAD, prepare both immutable snapshots through a single bounded main-history transfer, and work offline once prepared. Missing comparison data and fetched off-main active or comparison pins fail closed. Comparison provenance uses the same immutable pin definition as the queue-policy oracle. Worktree edits cannot replace pinned bytes, including a policy larger than 128 KiB. Active workflows, pins, routing assertions and existing test/setup/job deadlines are unchanged. The direct preflight entrypoint also prepares both absent snapshots and their main ancestry in one transfer before Vitest setup, with a real cold-checkout regression. The main-history cutoff precedes both reviewed fixture pins and is an acquisition hint only. Neither a cached snapshot nor the cutoff authorizes a pin: complete immutable reads and actual main-ancestry checks still determine provenance. Cold setup excludes an unrelated older asset, without filtering or truncating workflow blobs. A synthetic candidate HEAD may stay shallow after main is ready; prepared setup remains offline in that case. These cases cover the cold standalone preflight and the unchanged ten-second setup hook without downloading all earlier repository history. UTC `Z` timestamps remain compatible with Python 3.9. 2026-10-22 CI reliability: floating-panel browser tests poll for non-null rendered bounds within the existing assertion budget. A handle re-arm can hide its value-chip HUD for one frame immediately after a visibility assertion; the measurement now waits for that rendered state without changing test timeouts, retries, geometry, overlap or pointer-reachability assertions. The unchanged live-handle case failed 4 of 10 local attempts before the fix; it passed 20 of 20 afterward with retries disabled, and all eight floating-panel cases passed. This repairs the readiness race without closing the separate flaky-test-budget work. CI routing tests prepare immutable Git objects and main history in one main fetch for a cold checkout in suite setup, then retain every routing and provenance assertion under the existing five-second test deadline. An automated six-second fixture fetch reproduces the old timeout; the prepared case passes with unchanged test, setup and job deadlines. The no-network regression proves cold evidence fails closed, all routing checks pass without fetching after preparation with a shallow PR merge HEAD (including when main equals the pin), both initially missing workflow objects become readable after the same fetch, and a stale main ref still rejects the pin (`test/trusted-pr-policy.test.ts`, `scripts/test-fleet-test-fixtures.py`). Active workflow bytes, pins, runner routing and required checks are unchanged. Full local repository verification and the protected PR/queue runs remain delivery gates. The existing fleet-policy provenance reader normalizes Git's UTC `Z` suffix to `+00:00` before parsing its shallow-history date. The cold-fixture regression exposed Python 3.9 rejecting that timestamp before the fetch refusal could run. The unchanged offline, stale-pin and real six-second deadline assertions pass with Python 3.9 and 3.12. Explicit timezone offsets, ancestor checks, immutable workflow pins and test deadlines retain their existing behavior. | Camera 2.3 and E6; absence of old symbols may already close the proposed cleanup. |

| <a id="l11"></a>L11 | Partial | End-to-end encrypted project data: the browser encrypts documents, revisions, imported sources, names, thumbnails, measurements, view state and archived exports, so the Worker, D1, R2 and the collaboration room hold only ciphertext and act as blob store, access control and relay. Design and settled product decisions: [ADR-024](docs/adrs/ADR-024-end-to-end-encrypted-projects.md) (opt-in per account during the beta, assistant asks per project, names encrypted, no escrow, off switch beta-only, dormant accounts encrypt on next sign-in). Delivered: Phase 0, the ADR ([PR #743](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/743)), and Phase 1a, client-minted projects and save points ([PR #745](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/745)). Group key management uses MLS (RFC 9420) through `mls-rs` 0.56 with the RustCrypto provider, pinned exactly (decided 2026-10-19 on the spike in [PR #748](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/748)); the custom roster and epoch protocol is superseded, and the Phase 8 outside review covers the MLS integration and the pinned version. The [spike report](docs/plans/mls-spike-2026-10.md) holds the library comparison, measurements and reproduction steps. The MLS group-key layer is built, not yet wired in (Phase 3/5 groundwork, [PR #784](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/784)): `packages/mls` compiles `mls-rs` 0.56 + RustCrypto (suite 0x0002) to a committed, reproducible WASM (`packages/mls/build.sh --check`) run in a lazily started module Worker, with the roles GroupContext extension and the ADR's commit and rejoin policy in `MlsRules`/`IdentityProvider`, exporter project keys, epoch authenticators, history-archive epoch records and session state sealed under a non-extractable AES-GCM key; evidence: `packages/mls/src/mls.test.ts` (54 scenario steps in Node, in-process and through the Worker protocol, including every rejoin refusal, a removed device failing both rules, the per-leaf removal cutoff, a re-added member, the last-leaf exception and removal records carried to joiners), `sealed.test.ts`, `pkg.test.ts`, and the same steps in a module Worker in Chromium and WebKit (`pnpm --filter @openzcad/mls test:browser`). Phase 1 moves server-side document logic into the browser while data is still plaintext: duplicate, branch and adopt (Phase 1b, [PR #759](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/759): the Worker stores the browser-built copy or re-homed adoption after owner, id, save-point, lineage and import-localization checks (`assertClientProjectCopy`/`assertClientAdoption`; 400 `SAVE_POINT_INVALID`, 409 `PROJECT_ID_TAKEN`/`REVISION_ID_CONFLICT`), copy rollback and finalize compare the browser's digest after the store verifies it against the normalized copy, and the client-listed imported sources must cover every canonical and undo/redo source identity before insertion; evidence: `test/client-built-copies-and-adoptions.test.ts`, the browser-built cases in `test/imported-source-duplication.test.ts` and `test/e2e/account-copy.spec.ts`), and import-asset splitting (Phase 1c, in progress: the browser splits embedded STEP text and mesh arrays out of every document it writes (named save, autosave, new project, adoption, copy) into gzip `storageAssets` beside the storage projection; the Worker checks each upload's size and SHA-256 against its bytes, refuses unreferenced, missing, duplicate, oversize or mismatched uploads with 400 `STORAGE_ASSET_INVALID`, rebuilds the document in memory from the uploads for the existing checks, and stores the projection only when it hydrates to exactly the document it keeps, under the same content-addressed keys, so stored projects need no migration; load hydration, room checkpoints and server-built documents still split server-side; evidence: `test/project-storage-upload.test.ts`, `test/client-split-storage.test.ts`). Next: validation and the room merge, each proved by the existing suites. Phases 2–8 and their acceptance live in the ADR; the production migration (Phase 7) needs its own authorization. Phase 3 tail, slice 3T-1 (account-key history and revocation replacement), behind the off-by-default `E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED` flag: every account-key rotation (device or passkey removal, read-only revocation, recovery-key replacement) now wraps the replaced account key under its successor with the `previous-account-key` AES-KW purpose and stores it in the new `e2ee_account_key_history` table (migration `0030_e2ee_account_key_history.sql`, not applied anywhere remote; rows reference the user, survive identity pruning, are erased with the other key tables and fenced during erasure). Keys are named by identity generation and the device-list sequence of the entry that first committed to them (`accountKeySequence`). The Worker refuses a rotation or identity replacement that omits or misnames its history wrap, refuses one on a setup or a non-rotating append, stores it in the same batch, and serves only the caller's own rows (`GET /api/e2ee/account-key-history`); routes stay closed and erasure keeps working while 0030 is missing. `openAccountKeyChain` walks back from the unlocked key to any older one, checking current-identity keys against their certified commitments. A device revocation can name its replacement (`Revocation.replacedBy`, signed device-list entry version 2, used only when a replacement is named, so version 1 entries and vectors are unchanged byte for byte); `removeDevice` names the removing certified device. `replaceRecoveryKey` now holds the same device lock as the other rotations. The per-project revocation cutoff statement (`"OZR"`, identity-signed (projectId, cutoffEpoch) pairs bound to user, generation and revoking entry) is defined with encode, sign and verify only; storage and enforcement follow in Phase 4. Evidence: `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts` (three rotations open all four keys, across five replaced and pruned identities; a removed device's key opens none of the newer ones; tampered or missing rows refused; Worker refusals; only the caller's history served; erasure and cascade; the erasure fence; the pre-0030 state; the replacement lock serializing a removal and a second replacement; a history-write case in the mutation-authority matrix), `packages/crypto/src/devices.test.ts`, the version 2 entry and cutoff vectors in `packages/crypto/src/vectors.test.ts`, the backward-only chain in `wrap.test.ts`, the new cases in `fuzz.test.ts`, and `test/d1-migration-split.test.ts`. 3, first slice (account keys and devices, [PR #785](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/785)), ships behind the off-by-default `E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED` flag (plus the `E2EE_CANARY_EMAILS` secret allowlist), and D1 migration `0029_e2ee_account_keys.sql` must be applied to production, with the owner's authorization, before the flag is turned on: an identity-signed, hash-chained device list (`packages/crypto/src/devices.ts`) certifies non-extractable per-device signing and unlock keys, the recovery-key target, the encryption key and a commitment to the account key; the account key is sealed to every certified unlock target and the identity key only to the recovery-key target; browsers pin the list (rollback and forks refused, a replaced identity reported); Settings › Encryption sets up keys with a confirmed recovery key, removes devices with account- and encryption-key rotation, replaces the recovery key (new identity, fresh list, transition statement), asks whether to keep the device unlocked at sign-out, and approves devices by full-hash QR scan or the commitment-based six-digit comparison; the Worker stores ciphertext and public keys only and checks ownership, sizes, chain validity and wrap completeness. Evidence: `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts` (flows against the real Worker routes and migrations), `packages/crypto/src/devices.test.ts`, the device-list and paired-approval vectors in `packages/crypto/src/vectors.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/qrCode.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/encryption-settings.spec.ts` with `test/e2e/encryption-device-approval.spec.ts` (the `test/e2e/e2ee-fixtures.ts` fixtures seed device keys so other suites never run setup). Pin-persistence repair: every pin update atomically checks the actual stored identity and chain ancestry; an authorized identity change is bound to its prior pin snapshot, including approval requests. Stale loads and writes fail closed without downgrading the pin or retaining unlocked keys. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/deviceStore.test.ts`, the cross-tab cases in `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/encryption-pins.spec.ts` (two tabs with shared IndexedDB). Follow-up review repairs bind every key/approval mutation to revocable session authority and the erasure fence in SQL, preserve the durable device keys across concurrent setup and lost-response retries, and release camera tracks even when capture resolves after cancellation. Evidence: the mutation-authority and setup races in `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts`, both store backends in `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/deviceStore.test.ts`, and the camera cases in `test/e2e/encryption-device-approval.spec.ts`. No additional migration is needed; the flag stays off. Final review also binds comparison grants to their responder and current head, makes approval quotas atomic, prevents rejected completion replays from rewriting wraps, and requires paired account/encryption rotation in the shared verifier. Evidence: the approval race cases in `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts` and one-sided signed-chain cases in `packages/crypto/src/devices.test.ts`. Read-only approvals remain visible for the life of their wraps, with a recovery-authorized revoke-all action that rotates both keys and requires read-only devices to reapprove. Unknown or partial E2EE schema readiness prevents profile-erasure success and retains the retry fence. Evidence: lifecycle, collision and erasure-fault cases in `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts` and the read-only revocation browser flow. Sign-out drains pending saves before applying the explicit device-key choice and revoking the session; failed key reads or deletions remain visible and retryable, and rejected logout restores eligible autosave work without clearing an existing refusal. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/signOut.test.ts`, Settings tests, `test/cloud-project-autosave.test.ts` and the injected deletion-failure browser case. Not in this slice: passkey PRF unlock, desktop Keychain storage, project-key epochs on removal (Phases 4–5), contacts and MLS. Phase Integration repair: request admission fixes the expiry instant across every key-write statement while live session, revocation and erasure guards remain transactional. Account identity, chain, current wraps, approved-device metadata and previous heads are read in one D1 batch. Real SQLite regressions cover expiry crossing during setup and rotation for development, browser and desktop authority, plus account reads racing rotation and identity replacement. Existing cross-tab pin persistence and stale-wrap/lost-completion regressions remain active. Replacement retains the current and three previous identity generations; deleting older identity rows in the same guarded batch cascades their entries and wraps. Repeated device-authorized replacements, previous-generation visibility, cascading removal and isolation from another account are covered by the key-route regressions. Phase 1f, account loads checked in the browser: `api.loadProject` and `api.loadRevision` run `assertStorableDocument` on every document the account returns, and the share-link client does the same for the shared model. A version this browser cannot open is reported as the unreadable-account-copy error (503 `PROJECT_DOCUMENT_UNAVAILABLE`), so opening keeps the device copy in repair mode and autosave waits rather than replacing either copy; older documents still migrate as they always have. Loads on the server stop failing on such a document: `normalizedForLoad` returns it as stored when this build cannot normalize it, so the browser decides instead of the request ending in a 500. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/accountDocumentLoads.test.ts`, the shared-model case in `apps/web/src/lib/projectShareClient.test.ts`, and the `normalizedForLoad` cases in `test/storable-document.test.ts`. Phase 6, slice 6-1, per-project assistant consent on encrypted projects (plan default M6: per user, per project), lifting decision D7 one project at a time. The consent copy lists everything an assistant request sends (`apps/web/src/lib/assistant/consentCopy.ts`): the prompt, up to 12 earlier conversation turns, the model digest (project id, name, units, version, schema version, parameters, every feature's full definition including sketch text, bodies with volumes, bounding boxes and measured faces and edges, the measured edit catalog, warnings) and the selection, attached images and drawing pages with their file names, the client's operation list and a retry flag; it names the provider route (the user's own key, or the deployment's provider and model, and the endpoint's host), says that OpenZCAD's server reads the request in plaintext to pass it on with its own instructions, any custom instructions and an account-derived identifier, and that what was sent cannot be recalled. `ASSISTANT_CONSENT_COPY_VERSION` (1) is pinned to the exact request and digest field lists by tests, including a compile-time map of `CadDocumentDigest`'s keys. The record `{userId, projectId, allowed, copyVersion, route, sequence}` is canonical JSON MAC'd with HMAC-SHA-256 under an HKDF of the account key with its own label (`OpenZCAD E2EE v1 assistant consent mac`, fixed vector in `packages/crypto/src/assistant-consent.test.ts`); browsers pin the sequence per project and send only on a record that verifies, is not below the pin, allows the project and names the current copy and route, re-reading it before every request, so a server-forged consent never makes a browser send. Migration `0041_e2ee_assistant_consents.sql` stores one row per user and project (erasure fences, forward-only sequence, removed by profile erasure and project purge); `/api/e2ee/assistant-consents` (behind `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, 503 before 0041) lists, reads and stores the caller's records for encrypted projects it owns or belongs to, and `…/:projectId/members` shows co-members who allowed it. The Worker's assistant gate admits a format-2 project only with a live row for that user, current copy and the route the request would take (409 `ENCRYPTED_PROJECT_ASSISTANT_CONSENT_REQUIRED`); without the flag or 0041 it refuses as before, and plaintext requests are unchanged. UI: a consent dialog on first use, an "Allowed for this project" chip with Stop in the assistant panel (Stop aborts the request in flight and writes the next sequence with `allowed = false`, which marks the Worker row revoked), an Assistant row in the project properties, and Settings › Encryption › "Assistant access" with Revoke; consent is asked again after a revocation or a copy, key source, provider, model or endpoint change (the route carries a SHA-256 digest of all four in full, so no two routes collide). ADR-024's assistant section now lists the full payload. Evidence: `packages/crypto/src/assistant-consent.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-assistant-consent.test.ts` (refusal without consent, after revocation, for another user, for another route and after the upstream endpoint changes; migration-missing and flag-off cases with plaintext requests reaching the provider unchanged; erasure and purge), `apps/web/src/components/assistant/AssistantPanel.consent.test.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/assistant/AssistantPanel.encrypted.test.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/assistant/consentCopy.test.ts` (route collisions), `apps/web/src/components/assistant/AssistantConsentDialog.test.tsx` (no decline while an allow is stored), `packages/shared/src/sha256-sync.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/AssistantAccessSettings.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/encrypted-assistant-consent.spec.ts` (the intercepted request carries exactly the listed fields, and nothing is sent after Stop or Revoke). Device removal (slices 4-10 and 5-7) now stores the revocation and its identity-signed cutoff statement in one write, with no migration and behind the same flags (`E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED`, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, migration 0036). Before, revoking a device (`POST /api/e2ee/device-list`) and storing its statement (`PUT /api/e2ee/revocation-cutoffs`) were two requests, and a browser that went offline in between left a revoked device without a statement, which co-members could not tell from a device revoked before the account joined their project, so they kept writing under a key the removed device may still derive. The removal participant (`deviceRemoval.ts`) now signs the statement for the entry about to be appended (`cutoffsFor`, sharing its signing with `written`) and the append carries it (`E2eeAppendDeviceListRequest.cutoffs`); `accountKeys.ts` sends it and, on `E2EE_CUTOFFS_INCOMPLETE`, signs once more and retries, and Settings' wrappers (`pageDeviceRemoval.ts`, `projectRemovalStep.ts`) forward it. The Worker (`appendedRevocationCutoffs` in `e2eeDeviceRemoval.ts`, called by `handleAppend`) requires it when the entry revokes a device of an account with encrypted projects owned or shared (recycle bin included): missing is refused with 409 `E2EE_CUTOFFS_REQUIRED`; it must verify against the list including the entry, name that entry, stay within the next key epoch (400 otherwise) and name every such project at its next key epoch (409 `E2EE_CUTOFFS_INCOMPLETE` otherwise); it is inserted in the entry's own D1 batch under the same guard. `written` and `complete` still sign and store the statement again (raising cutoffs after a late rotation); passkey and read-only rotations, accounts without encrypted projects, accounts with encrypted projects switched off and databases before migration 0036 are unchanged, and the device-list body limit grows by one statement's size. Evidence: `test/e2ee-device-removal.test.ts` (a revocation without cutoffs, from a participant or none, is refused and leaves entries, wraps, history and cutoffs unchanged; statements missing a project, an epoch behind or ahead, naming an earlier revoking entry or with another signature are refused with nothing written; a removal cut off the moment its entry lands leaves the statement already stored, so the account's other device reads the removed phone's earlier versions and rotates before its next write; a project created between the listing and the append is refused once and signed again; passkey removal and an account without encrypted projects are unchanged), `test/e2ee-shared-device-removal.test.ts` (a member's removal cut off right after the append: the owner reads the statement, keeps reading the phone's earlier version, and is refused `E2EE_MEMBER_ROTATION_PENDING` by its browser and the Worker's fence until another of the member's devices rotates) and `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/projectRemovalStep.test.ts`. Each named project's cutoff must also carry its current newest head (one at an older version, another hash or none, perhaps signed before the device being removed saved again, is refused with 409 `E2EE_CUTOFFS_INCOMPLETE`), and the device-list entry's insert is fenced on a fingerprint of the account's encrypted projects with their key epochs and heads, so a project created, shared, committed to or saved to after the check leaves the whole append unwritten and answered `E2EE_CUTOFFS_INCOMPLETE` for the browser to sign again (`test/e2ee-device-removal.test.ts`, #911 review). Issue #817 follow-up to the archive verifier in PR #839: provisional Welcome and external-rejoin admission exposes only archive decryption until the existing `JoinArchive` verifies; normal operations and confirmation remain blocked across sealed-state restoration. Malformed downloads preserve an accepted provisional rejoin for retry after its original PSK expires, while verified policy rejection or discard restores prior state. Session contents use version 2 and reject legacy unverified cutoffs; `EpochRecordV1` and the archive API stay unchanged. Rust refuses revoked directory devices without a cutoff, preserving zero. Archive messages reject trailing bytes. Evidence: `packages/mls/src/{scenarios,boundary.test}.ts` and native archive regressions, plus the regenerated pinned WASM. Application integration and checkpoint replay remain open. Real-browser coverage for the E2EE packages, with Firefox added. `pnpm --filter @openzcad/mls test:browser` runs the MLS scenarios in a real module Worker in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox (61/61 steps in each; `-- --chromium-only` runs Chromium alone). New `pnpm --filter @openzcad/crypto test:browser` runs `packages/crypto/browser/scenarios.ts` (39 steps) on each engine's own WebCrypto and web streams in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox: chunked, incremental and streamed envelopes and content blobs (gzip and none, tampering, truncation, a gzip bomb); AES-KW and AES-GCM wraps, seal and open; project-envelope, version-chain, device-list, approval-grant and identity-transition signatures; the Phase 4-2 project data formats (`project-info` and `key-archive` envelopes with their body codecs, the epoch binding recomputed from a key archive, document-body revision hashes, the `rejoin-psk`, `archived-project-key` and `previous-account-key` wraps with every field bound, and `verifyVersionLog` / `verifyAuthoredLogRecord` refusing a rollback, a fork, a gap, a reorder, a falling epoch, a forged or unknown signer and a revoked device past its cutoff); the external-key seam; and the fixed vectors, the new ones included, byte-identical in every engine. AES-KW refusal is covered independently of the engine: all 11 KW purposes refuse a wrong parent key, another context and three flipped bytes on the engine as it is, and again with `crypto.subtle.unwrapKey` replaced by a stand-in that returns a zeroed key, each refusal following an unwrap that returned a key (the re-wrap check from #847). The one raw-WebCrypto step, an engine probe, reports a warning instead of failing when the engine skips the RFC 3394 integrity check; any other step that warns fails. Results on Windows (Playwright 1.63): Chromium 153 and Firefox 155 39/39 passed; WebKit 26.6 38 passed with 1 warning (its AES-KW unwrap returns zeroed keys), and the package refused all 55 bad unwraps there through the re-wrap check alone. `test/crypto-browser-scenarios.test.ts` runs every step in Node under `pnpm test` and requires that none warns. Both browser runs need `pnpm exec playwright install chromium webkit firefox` and stay out of `pnpm test` and CI. Phase 1e, document validation in the browser: `assertStorableDocument` (`@openzcad/document-core`) runs every content check the account and the collaboration room made (structure, envelope, schema version, revision and checkpoint history, undo history, sketch provenance, reference dimensions, text budget, drawing, Objects folder and reference-image sections, content schema, imported STEP and mesh sources, normalization first, so older documents migrate as they always have on load, then the content checks on the result). The browser runs it before every account write (`splitProjectStorageUpload`), before every room submission, named room save and kept version, and on every document the room hands it: a version it cannot open becomes a refusal that keeps the last good version (state frames drop the document, acknowledgements drop the echo, merge bases are dropped), while a newer client's document still asks the user to update. Autosave treats such a refusal as permanent for that edit (`refused`, never `offline`) and resumes with the first newer edit that passes. The server checks are unchanged in this slice; later slices validate account loads in the browser and then remove them. Evidence: `test/storable-document.test.ts` (including older documents that must still open), the browser-refusal case in `test/cloud-project-autosave.test.ts`, and the refusal and no-publish cases in `apps/web/src/lib/useCollaboration.test.tsx`. Phase 1d, the room merge in the browser ([PR #827](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/827)): the collaboration room orders versions and never merges; a submission that descends from an older room version it still holds is refused as a conflict carrying that `base`, and the submitting browser merges base, latest and its current document (edits made while the stale submission was in flight included) with `mergeCollaborationDocuments`, now in `@openzcad/document-core`, then resubmits on the latest version over the socket or, when oversize, over HTTP. A named save that is draining the stale edit waits for the merged resubmission. A merge that conflicts or fails validation, a base other than the one submitted on, or a fourth stale conflict in a row without an acknowledgement falls back to the manual conflict dialog; a pre-Phase 1 browser gets that dialog where the room used to merge for it. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/useCollaboration.test.tsx` (merge with and without in-flight edits, unmergeable edits, a foreign base, the retry cap, the HTTP path, base parsing), the draining case in `useCollaborationCheckpoint.test.tsx`, and the room-side cases in `test/cloudflare-adapters.test.ts`, `test/project-edit-lease.test.ts`, `test/collaboration-room-socket.test.ts`, `test/object-folders-collaboration.test.ts` and `test/mesh-artifact-discard.test.ts` (a late edit merged after a copy completion advanced the room). Next: remove the remaining server-side document validation and `normalizeDocument` from the save path, keeping envelope and size checks. Phase 5, slice 5-1, contacts and verified pins, behind `E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED`, with migration 0037 `e2ee_contact_entries`. `packages/crypto/src/contacts.ts` adds the identity-signed, hash-chained contacts list ("OZK" v1, documented in the package README): each entry verifies a contact's identity key with the device-list head it was compared at, moves that head forward, records a key change or withdraws trust; only a verified contact can advance, change or be untrusted, and only under the identity it was verified with. `verifyContactsList` checks the chain under the identity key and, with a pin, refuses a rolled-back or forked list; `contactDeviceListPin` gives the pin a verified contact's device list must extend. The Worker (`apps/web/worker/e2eeContacts.ts`) serves `GET`/`POST /api/e2ee/contacts` (the caller's list for its current identity generation and the people it may look up; an append must verify under the identity and the list's rules and extend the stored head, checked again with the generation and the session/erasure predicate in the insert) and `GET /api/e2ee/users/:id/device-list`, answered only for co-members of a project or the two sides of a live invitation (decision M2), with one identical 404 for everyone else, a missing id and a malformed one. A cached schema probe keeps ordinary traffic, the other key routes, device-list lookups and erasure working without 0037 (only the contacts list answers 503, and the migration is noticed without a redeploy); erasure deletes the list with the profile, and a replaced identity starts a fresh list. In the browser, `contacts.ts` and `contactPins.ts` keep seen pins and the own list pin in their own IndexedDB database (`openzcad-e2ee-contacts`); a contact's device list must extend both the signed verified head and the newest head this browser accepted, a verified contact whose identity changed drops back to seen with the downgrade kept locally until the key change is signed, signing asks for a passkey or the recovery key (`withIdentityKey`), and only a verified contact yields a key to wrap to. Settings › Encryption gains a Contacts list (`EncryptionContacts.tsx`) with the mutual comparison: both people's 40-digit codes over identity key and device-list head, their QR form and a scan or paste check. Evidence: `packages/crypto/src/contacts.test.ts`, `vectors.test.ts` (a fixed entry and hash with a recorded identity signature re-checked with raw WebCrypto), `fuzz.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/contactPins.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-contacts.test.ts` (real Worker and SQLite with Node devices of two accounts: forged, stale, foreign-generation and malformed appends refused; M2 lookups for owner, members, co-members and pending invitations, revoked invitations and strangers refused with one 404; mutual digit-group verification; a rolled-back, forked or emptied contacts list refused; a changed key back to seen, wraps blocked, then the key change and a new verification signed; a verified contact's device list rolled back behind the pin refused; migrations only through the latest present, with only the probe naming the new table; erasure and identity replacement) and `test/e2e/encryption-contacts.spec.ts` (two accounts in two browsers verify each other by digit groups with their recovery keys, and the verification survives a reload). Phase 4, slice 4-6, encrypted content blobs with HMAC dedupe: D1 migration `0034_e2ee_content_blobs.sql` adds `e2ee_content_blobs` (per project and blob id: kind, opaque object key `e2ee/<projectId>/<blobId>`, ciphertext bytes, an optional dedupe tag unique per project, and a committed or deleting state), `e2ee_version_blobs` (the blob ids each accepted version's header names, the reference count garbage collection reads) and an `e2ee_blob_kind` marker on `upload_sessions`, with triggers that refuse a reference to an uncommitted blob, a claim on a blob a retained version names, the removal of an unclaimed blob, any change to what the Worker accepted, and an encrypted session becoming an artifact; blobs share the artifact quota and reservation accounting of migrations 0017 and 0022, counting ciphertext, and carry erasure fences. `apps/web/worker/e2eeContentBlobs.ts` serves `/api/e2ee/projects/:id/blobs` (`GET ?tag=`), `blobs/uploads` (POST: an upload session for the blob id the browser proposes, the same one on a retry, or the committed blob), `blobs/:blobId/finalize` (POST) and `blobs/:blobId` (GET ciphertext), behind the key gate, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, migrations 0031–0034 (503 without them), the session/erasure write predicate, owner only and format 2 only; the bytes go up through the ordinary single-part and multipart upload routes, and finalize reads only the stored object's head (`parseEnvelopeShape`, new in `@openzcad/crypto`) to require a content blob of the session's kind named by the session's id, within its kind's ceiling, exactly `envelopeBodyLength` long and, uncompressed, its whole declared size, discarding a refused object with its session. Document versions now accept header `blobIds` only when every one is a committed blob of the project, recorded in the version's own batch; after each save, garbage collection drops references of pruned versions and removes blobs no retained version names once they are a day old (`E2EE_UNREFERENCED_BLOB_GRACE_MS`), and a dedupe lookup offers only blobs it would keep for half that again. Closing 4-1's gap, plaintext upload sessions, artifact finalize, listing, metadata and downloads refuse format-2 projects, and an encrypted session refuses a plaintext project; purge and erasure remove blobs with their projects. In the browser, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/encryptedContentBlobs.ts` is `archiveArtifact`'s encrypting transport for STEP sources, mesh payloads and reference-image pictures: an `assetDedupeHash` tag under the project's dedupe secret (from the current key archive, `dedupeSecretForProject`), reuse of a tagged blob only when this page holds its reference for the same plaintext checksum, otherwise `encryptContentBlobStream` (`createContentBlobWriter`) under a fresh key, the upload session and finalize with the tag; the artifact id the document records is the blob id. Writes put the reference of every blob the document names in the body, the decode worker derives the header's `blobIds` from that list and `checkBlobReferences` runs on every write; verified reads register their bodies' references (`blobReferences.ts`), the page decrypts a document's sources in the decode worker before geometry workers rebuild (`preloadEncryptedImportSources`), and `exactSourceResolver.ts` and backups read blobs the same way. 4-5's refusal of imports, meshes and reference images is lifted; embedded payloads, original mesh files, thumbnails, exports, measurements and view state stay on the device. Ordinary uploads, imports, purge and erasure do not depend on 0034; the flag stays off; the migration is approved for the beta deploy. Evidence: `test/e2ee-content-blobs.test.ts` (Node devices with the in-process MLS engine over the real Worker, upload sessions and SQLite: a STEP file stored encrypted and opened on another device; multipart parts not aligned with chunks; swapped, flipped and truncated blobs refused by the reader; the Worker refusing a blob that names another id, is cut short, or is of another kind; dedupe hit and miss, a tag naming a blob with no known key, a tag the account points at another file, and stale unreferenced blobs not offered; garbage collection keeping referenced blobs and blobs a named save names, and refusing a version naming a collected blob; quota, usage, purge and erasure; format gates both ways; owner and flag; migrations only through 0033), `packages/crypto/src/envelope.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-decode-jobs.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-project-documents.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/exactSourceResolver.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/archiveArtifact.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/projectBackupTransfer.test.ts` and `test/e2e/encrypted-content-blobs.spec.ts`. Phase 4 groundwork, the decode worker (ADR-024, Decompression limits): a lazily started module Worker (`apps/web/src/worker/e2eeDecodeWorker.ts`) decrypts a project envelope of a given kind under that kind's plaintext ceiling (documents and share snapshots `MAX_CLOUD_PROJECT_DOCUMENT_BYTES`, the others `PLAINTEXT_CEILINGS`), decodes the UTF-8 a slice at a time through `JsonComplexityGuard` before `JSON.parse`, then runs `documentStructureError`; and it stream-decrypts a content blob under its kind's ceiling (128 MiB for imported sources, mesh payloads and exports; smaller for thumbnails), returning transferable 1 MiB pieces or a Blob only once the whole blob has authenticated. A declared size over the ceiling, a body that inflates past its declared size, or one that inflates to anything else is refused with the crypto package's `E2eeError` codes. The client (`apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/decodeWorker.ts`) runs one job at a time under a wall-clock budget: a job past it is rejected (`timeout`), the worker is terminated rather than awaited, and the next job runs on a fresh worker; a refusal or a crashed worker never wedges the queue, and callers keep their last good version. Nothing calls it yet. The worker chunk is listed in `LAZY_ENTRY_PATTERNS`, so the launcher can never preload it. Evidence: `test/e2ee-decode-jobs.test.ts` (a gzip bomb with a small declared size, too short and too long bodies, declared sizes over the document, measurements and thumbnail ceilings, JSON too deep and with too many values refused before parsing, a truncated final chunk, valid document and multi-chunk blob round trips), `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/decodeWorker.test.ts` (a fake Worker: overrun jobs terminate their worker and the next job runs on a new one, budgets start on dispatch, refusals keep the worker, crashes and unexpected replies replace it, dispose rejects every pending job) and `test/bundle-size-policy.test.ts`. Phase 3, desktop Keychain storage: the macOS app creates each device's signing (ECDSA P-256) and unlock (ECDH P-256) keys natively and keeps them in the Keychain as non-syncing generic passwords beside its refresh credential (`apps/desktop/device-keys`, Tauri commands `create_device_keys`, `sign_with_device_key`, `derive_with_device_key`, `delete_device_keys`); the WebView holds only public keys and a key id, and signs and unseals through the external keys `@openzcad/crypto` accepts. `keychainDeviceStore` keeps the IndexedDB device record with each private key replaced by its key id, rebuilds native handles on load, and removes Keychain keys no record references (a deleted or replaced device, a losing setup race). Browsers keep non-extractable WebCrypto keys. Evidence: the `openzcad-device-keys` crate tests (WebCrypto-computed vectors for public keys, ECDH and P1363 signatures; failure cleanup; id and peer validation), run on Linux by the dependency-audit workflow; the end-to-end "desktop Keychain device keys" case in `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts` (setup, unlock through the host's ECDH, a device-signed recovery-key replacement, forget); `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/keychainDeviceKeys.test.ts`. The Keychain calls themselves run only on macOS; the manual Apple Silicon workflow was not dispatched, by the maintainer's choice. Phase 3, desktop Keychain groundwork: `@openzcad/crypto` accepts device private keys held outside WebCrypto (`ExternalSigningKey`, `ExternalAgreementKey`), so the desktop app can keep its device signing and unlock keys in the macOS Keychain and sign or derive natively without the bytes reaching script. Envelope and device-statement signing (`ecdsaSign`) and sealed-key opening (`ecdhDeriveBits`) take either a `CryptoKey` or an external key; host results are length-checked and failures stay `E2eeError`s. Browsers keep non-extractable `CryptoKey`s unchanged. Evidence: `packages/crypto/src/external-keys.test.ts` (external signatures verify for envelopes and approval grants, a seal opens through a host that sees only the ephemeral public key, malformed or failing host results and wrong key forms are refused). Phase 4, slice 4-10, device removal across encrypted projects and the owner-only solo group reset (decision D9): D1 migration `0036_e2ee_revocation_cutoffs.sql` (0035 is held for slice 4-7) adds `e2ee_revocation_cutoffs` (per user, identity generation and revoking device-list entry: the identity-signed cutoff statement and its signature) and `e2ee_group_resets` (per project and new group generation: the identity-signed reset, the new key-epoch base and the replaced group's id and epoch), with user and project cascades, erasure fences, an immutable reset row, and a replacement of 0032's `e2ee_project_group_forward` trigger that lets a group row start over at epoch 0 only as the next generation, with a key-epoch base right after the replaced group's newest key epoch and its reset recorded (decision D10). The cutoff statement (`"OZR"`) gains version 2, used only when a cutoff names a head: each project's cutoff is the first key epoch the removed device must not know plus the newest head the removing browser accepted, and version 1 statements stay byte for byte; a new identity-signed `"OZX"` statement (`encodeGroupReset`, `signGroupReset`, `verifyGroupReset`) names the project, the replaced group's generation and newest key epoch and the signer's device-list head; `VersionCutoff` accepts a key epoch, with or without a head. `apps/web/worker/e2eeDeviceRemoval.ts` serves `GET`/`PUT /api/e2ee/revocation-cutoffs` (the caller's statements and each owned encrypted project's group state and newest head; a store verified against the stored device list with the identity key, for an entry that revokes a device, and refused as `E2EE_CUTOFF_REGRESSION` if it would drop a project or lower a cutoff) and `GET /api/e2ee/projects/:id/mls/resets`, `POST …/mls/reset` (owner only, refused while a member or another account's leaf exists, the signature verified, fenced on the group state it names; the new group's GroupInfo, leaf, wraps and key archive and the removal of the old generation's Commits land in one D1 batch), behind the key gate, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED` and migrations 0031–0033 and 0036 (503 without them); project purge and account erasure clear the new tables only after a cached probe. In the browser, `removeDevice`, `revokeReadOnlyDevices` and `removePasskey` take a participant step (`deviceRemoval.ts`): before the identity key is opened it catches every project up and refuses a removal it could not finish (encrypted projects, no migration 0036); once the new device list is accepted it signs and stores the cutoffs for every owned encrypted project with the identity key the removal opened (device removals only), then rotates each project: a Remove of the revoked device's leaves, or an empty Commit when only the account key changed, with the new epoch's project key and rejoin PSK under the new account key and a key archive with a new dedupe secret (decision D8), re-signing any cutoff whose rotation landed later; `projectGroups.ts` supplies verified cutoffs and `replacedBy` to the MLS directory (a revoked device without a signed cutoff has one only in a group whose epoch-0 key was wrapped under an account key from its revocation on, and is otherwise refused), and the store's version authorship takes the same cutoffs (a revoked device's versions only up to its cutoff head and below its cutoff key epoch); writers enforce rotation in `joinProjectGroup` and `commitProjectGroup`, so another device finishes a removal that went offline part-way before its next write, a pending Commit built before a removal is dropped rather than resent, a Commit a removed device sent at or after its rotation epoch is refused (`removed-device`), and state that can no longer process an earlier Commit of a since-removed device, or that belongs to a replaced group, rejoins with a fresh leaf from the archives; the last-leaf exception is used by the revocation's named replacement, whose external Commit removes the revoked leaf with the newest pre-revocation PSK; `resetProjectGroup` signs a reset with the identity key (`signGroupResetWithIdentity`) only after the exact plain-words confirmation and only while the newest group state and the leaf registry list nobody but the owner, carries every earlier project key from the account-key wraps into the new epoch 0's archive, and later reads find the earlier group's key-epoch base from the verified resets. Settings › Encryption runs the participant on removals and offers the reset under "Encrypted project recovery" (lazily loaded, hidden while encrypted projects are off). Ordinary traffic does not depend on 0036; the flags stay off; the migration is approved for the beta deploy. Evidence: `test/e2ee-device-removal.test.ts` (Node devices with the real account-key flows, the in-process MLS engine and the real Worker over SQLite: a removal across three projects with the cutoffs, rotation epochs, new-account-key wraps and new dedupe secrets checked and the Worker refusing a lowered, tampered or misnamed statement; a removal that goes offline after one rotation and is finished by another device's next write; a stolen device's Commit refused by the Worker and, served anyway, by the directory, and its later version refused by authorship for a pinned and a fresh browser, with no post-removal wrap opening under its account key; the last-leaf exception refused to a device that is not the named replacement and used by the one that is; a device that missed the removed device's earlier Commits rejoining; history under older account keys still read; read-only revocation rotating without a statement; D9 allowed for the owner of a solo group with history kept and a stale device rejoining, refused without confirmation, with another account's leaf, with a member or for another user; purge and erasure of the new rows; removal refused, and everything else working, with migrations only through 0034), `packages/crypto/src/devices.test.ts`, `packages/crypto/src/version-log.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/mls/directory.test.ts` and `apps/web/src/components/EncryptedProjectRecovery.test.tsx`. Phase 4, slice 4-8, copies, branches, adoption, trash and deletion of encrypted projects, with no new migration. `POST /api/e2ee/projects` takes an optional `copyFrom: { projectId, blobIds, revisionId? }` (`apps/web/worker/e2eeProjectCopies.ts`): the Worker checks that the caller owns the source (format 2, in the recycle bin or not, as a plaintext owner may copy one), that a branch's revision is one of its named saves, that every listed id is a committed blob of the source (409 `E2EE_COPY_BLOB_UNKNOWN` otherwise, before anything is copied) and that the new version's header names the import blobs and the new project info's header the thumbnail and export blobs among them, then copies each blob's object byte for byte, a part at a time, under the same blob id to `e2ee/<copy>/<blobId>`, and lands the project, its new solo MLS group, its first version, its project info, the copied blobs' rows (no dedupe tag; migration 0034's accounting counts them against the owner's artifact storage, 413 `ARTIFACT_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`) and their version references in one D1 batch fenced on the source still holding every listed blob; a copy lands next to its source on the shelf. Rollback and finalize compare envelope hashes, not document digests: a refused or failed copy removes the copied objects no committed row names, and a create whose first version (by its envelope hash) the account already stored is answered as created, so a retry after a lost acknowledgement succeeds instead of reading `PROJECT_ID_TAKEN`. An optional `adoption: { localProjectId }` binds a device project saved to an account that encrypts new projects in `project_adoptions` as a plaintext adoption does, answering a retry with 409 `ALREADY_ADOPTED` and its project id. In the browser, `cloudProjectCopy.ts` builds an encrypted source's copy with the Phase 1 builders from the verified, decrypted head (a branch first re-reads the current head, so its save state is proven part of the history this browser trusts now and the pin never moves back) and `withSourceBlobIds` puts the source's blob ids back where `duplicateProjectDocument` made them device-local; `encryptedProjectStore.ts`'s `copyProject` registers the source's references under the copy's id and writes the copy's first version and project info (a duplicate carries the source's thumbnail and archived exports, a branch neither; measurements stay behind, as a plaintext Duplicate carries none) under a new group with its own dedupe secret, re-sending the same bytes once after a network failure; `createProject` takes an adopted document (decision D2: `api.adoptProject` never sends it in plaintext to an opted-in account, and a Worker's `ENCRYPTED_PROJECTS_REQUIRED` moves it), after which the existing account-save step uploads the device-only sources as encrypted blobs and writes the next version. 4-5's `E2EE_LOCAL_ONLY` refusal of copies, its `E2EE_ADOPTION_UNAVAILABLE` and the shelf's hidden Duplicate are lifted. The owner now reads a format-2 project in the recycle bin (GET routes), as plaintext owners do, while writes keep refusing it; restore and purge use the ordinary routes, and purge, `deleteOwnedProjects` and account erasure already remove every 0032–0034 table and their R2 objects; the erasure preview now counts encrypted heads and named saves. Plaintext copy, adoption, trash and purge are unchanged and name no encrypted table. Evidence: `test/e2ee-encrypted-copies.test.ts` (Node devices with the in-process MLS engine over the real Worker and SQLite: a copy whose blob rows, ids and R2 bytes equal the source's with no upload or re-encryption, opened by another device and surviving the source's deletion; a foreign blob id and another account's source refused with nothing created or copied; quota rollback removing copied objects; a lost create acknowledgement answered as created; part-by-part object copies and their rollback; a branch that refreshes the head first, keeps the pin and lets the next save descend from the current head, and one refused when the current head no longer names its save state; adoption into an opted-in account with its device-only source following as a blob and `ALREADY_ADOPTED` on retry, and plaintext adoption refused under D2; trash, owner reads in the bin, refused writes, restore, open and save; purge removing the source's objects while its copy keeps its own; the erasure preview counting encrypted heads and saves, and erasure emptying every table and object; plaintext duplicate, adoption, trash, restore and purge naming no encrypted table, with and without migrations 0032–0034), `test/e2ee-project-groups.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/projectFormatLoads.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/projectFormat.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/encrypted-content-blobs.spec.ts` (Duplicate on the shelf of an encrypted project with a STEP import: the copy's blob is the source's ciphertext byte for byte, nothing is uploaded again, and a fresh browser opens the copy and rebuilds the import from the copy's own blob). Phase 4, slice 4-5, encrypted document versions: D1 migration `0033_e2ee_project_versions.sql` adds `e2ee_project_versions` (the version log of decision D1: per accepted document envelope its Worker-computed version hash, key epoch, prefix, v1 signature and signer, and the body pointer, inline below 256 KiB or an opaque R2 key `e2ee/<projectId>/v/<hash>`, cleared when pruned; immutable apart from pruning and erasure), `e2ee_project_revisions` (named saves by version; names stay inside the encrypted document) and `e2ee_project_envelopes` (the project-info envelope; forward-only sequences), with a quota trigger counting retained ciphertext against the owner's project storage, cascades and erasure fences. `apps/web/worker/e2eeProjectDocuments.ts` serves `POST /api/e2ee/projects` (the format-2 row with a fixed `document_json` marker and empty name, its epoch-0 MLS group, first version and project info in one D1 batch) and `/api/e2ee/projects/:id/` `document` (GET head plus the log since a version; PUT autosave), `versions`, `revisions` (POST named save; GET list), `revisions/:id` and `info`, behind the key gate, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, migrations 0031–0033 (503 without them), the session/erasure write predicate, owner only and format 2 only; every write is parsed (`parseEnvelope`), must be this project's document with no blob ids (refused until 4-6), move forward from `expectedVersion`, name the stored head's hash, use the group's current key epoch (fenced in the same batch on `e2ee_project_groups`) and carry a signature by a device the caller's newest list certifies; the log keeps every retained revision plus the last 2,000 heads (D6); envelopes travel as binary frames. In the browser, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/encryptedProjectStore.ts` implements the plaintext transport's shapes: writes validate the document, encode the body with the signed `revisionVersionHashes`, encrypt it in the decode worker under a fresh version key wrapped under the current project key (after catch-up or an external-Commit join), bind the epoch and sign with the certified device key via `ecdsaSign`; current-head reads verify the log from the pin (`verifyVersionLog`, `verifyAuthoredLogRecord` with cutoffs), the head's author (`verifyAuthoredEnvelope`), that the author's device held a leaf it certified in the head's epoch and the epoch binding, then decrypt through the decode worker, validate and advance the pin; historical reads must be the pinned head or in its signed revision map and never move the pin; a fresh browser warns when the head trails the account's activity; integrity failures read as an unreadable account copy, so autosave halts for repair. Pins (head hash, version, key epoch, project-info sequence; compare-and-swap) live in their own IndexedDB database, `openzcad-e2ee-versions`. `api.ts` dispatches on the storage format, opted-in accounts create encrypted projects (D2) and never send device projects, the shelf decrypts project-info names inline from `GET /api/projects`, `SUPPORTED_PROJECT_FORMATS` is `[1, 2]`, files, measurements, view state and copies of encrypted projects stay on the device and the live room does not connect for them; the assistant stays off (D7). Also fixed, from the #847 review: `verifyAuthoredEnvelope`/`verifyAuthoredLogRecord` copy and validate the cutoff map before their first await, and the decode worker reads each envelope kind with its body codec. Ordinary traffic does not depend on 0033; the flag stays off; the migration is approved for the beta deploy. Evidence: `test/e2ee-project-documents.test.ts` (Node devices with the in-process MLS engine over the real Worker routes and SQLite: create and cross-device load and save; rollback refused with the pin kept; a forked log reported; a head under an old epoch refused by the Worker and by the browser; a historical read that keeps the pin and a revision outside the pinned history refused; a revoked author outside its cutoff refused; 409 conflicts; refused writes keeping the last good version; quota, content-blob and read-only refusals; R2 bodies and pruning; retention; purge; erasure; owner-only access; D2; project-info rollback; heads the Worker accepts but the browser refuses; migrations only through 0032), `test/cloud-project-autosave.test.ts` (encrypted transport), `test/e2ee-decode-jobs.test.ts`, `packages/crypto/src/version-log.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/versionPins.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/encryptedDocumentsApi.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/projectFormatLoads.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/projectFormat.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/encrypted-projects.spec.ts`. Phase 4, editor encryption indicator: an end-to-end encrypted project (the page's `isEncryptedProject` marker or a listed summary's `storageFormat` 2) shows a lock beside its name in the top bar; plaintext projects show nothing new, and with the E2EE flags off nothing changes. The lock opens a short popover with the state in plain words and a link to Settings › Encryption, and carries an optional `projectStateCode` slot for the Phase 5 project state code (unused until a build derives one). States come from a crypto-free subscription, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/encryptionStatus.ts` (in the entry chunk with no crypto import, like `unlockedKeys.ts`): verified (the last head this page opened or wrote — an account load or save, a new project or copy, a head the live room relays or accepts — passed verification; accessible name "End-to-end encrypted"), warning (a `stale-head` verification warning, kept until the project is switched away from), locked (`E2EE_KEYS_LOCKED` when the store opens; prompts to unlock in Settings › Encryption), problem (an `EncryptedProjectIntegrityError` in its own words, or the `repair` save state that halts autosave), and not verified yet before any of these. `encryptionStatusReports.ts`, loaded with the store by `encryptedProjects.ts`, reports each outcome for the key session that opened the store only; everything is forgotten on key-session teardown, and other projects' outcomes on a project switch. No migration, no flag change, no new dependency; the wording claims only what verification checks (signed by one of your devices, never moved back from a version this browser has seen). Evidence: `apps/web/src/components/TopBar.test.tsx` (each state, the repair override, the state-code slot, Escape, and a plaintext project showing nothing), `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/encryptionStatus.test.ts` (warnings, integrity failures, locked keys, project switch, teardown reset but not on ready, the store wrapper on every head path and a late result after the session ended) and `test/e2e/encrypted-projects.spec.ts` (verified after create and after reopen from the account copy; absent for a plaintext project). Phase 5, slice 5-3b, the browser flow for invitations to encrypted projects, behind the same flags as 5-3a (`E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED`, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, the sharing rollout) and migration 0038, with no migration of its own. `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/invitations.ts`: the owner's certified device signs the "OZI" statement and keeps the invitee's address in this browser (`invitationStore.ts`; the server keeps its hash); the invitee accepts the `#encrypted-invite=` link, checks the statement against the owner's device list and, once the owner is a verified contact (5-1's mutual comparison), each of its certified devices publishes one KeyPackage whose leaf is certified from the group's current epoch (decision M3); the owner's browser shows "waiting for keys" or "waiting for you to verify", adds only a verified invitee, only for a statement one of its devices signed, from a claim it keeps until the Add lands, after checking each KeyPackage's account, device, leaf certification and hash, in one Add Commit with the statement's role that carries the Welcome and the owner's wraps of the new epoch (`addInvitee`, the directory including the claimed leaves). An added device joins by its Welcome (`joinProjectByWelcome`: `beginJoin`, the joined epoch's key archive through the provisional exporter, every earlier archive, `completeJoin`; an inviter or server that omits or alters history is refused and the KeyPackage session restored, #817) and posts its account's own wraps; the account's other devices rejoin by External Commit with the account-wrapped rejoin PSK. A pending Add is never resent without its statement. The sharing dialog's encrypted branch invites (email and role), lists pending invitations with Verify (Settings › Encryption at that person's code comparison) and Revoke, and adds ready invitees; share links and role changes are not offered there yet; Settings › Encryption › Invitations accepts the link, after setup when there are no keys; shared projects' names decrypt on the shelf. The slice 5-2 gap is fixed at its root: an epoch's wraps exist for an account only once one of its devices processed that epoch, and roles come from the epoch's key archive or the device's own session, so a browser whose session was behind a member's External Commit reported the member's version as written without an editing role; a read now catches the device's own session up first (which posts the account's wraps of each epoch) and a key that is truly missing is reported as such. The Worker adds the owner's KeyPackage counts, the group epoch for the invitee and `GET /api/e2ee/invitations/sent`. Plaintext sharing is unchanged. Evidence: `test/e2ee-invitations-browser.test.ts` (two accounts' Node devices, the in-process MLS engine and the real Worker routes over SQLite: the full flow with two invitee devices joining by Welcome and a third rejoining by External Commit and writing, read by the owner's reloaded and fresh browsers; a viewer refused writes; an unverified invitee never added and nothing claimed; omitted history refused at the app and by `completeJoin`, then joining with the whole history; a KeyPackage with another device's leaf refused; a claim kept across a reload; another owner's invitation refused; the 5-2 gap's regression; the new list fields), `ProjectSharingDialog.test.tsx`, `encryptedInvitationLink.test.ts`, `AssistantAccessSettings.test.tsx`, and Playwright `encrypted-sharing.spec.ts` (owner and invitee contexts: invite from the dialog, the invitee sent to setup, accept, both verify by digit groups, the Add, the invitee's shelf names the project and opens it). Phase 5, slice 5-3a, invitations and KeyPackages for encrypted projects, Worker and crypto only (the browser flow is slice 5-3b), behind `E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED`, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED` and the account's project-sharing rollout, with migration 0038 `e2ee_invitations`, `e2ee_key_packages` and `e2ee_welcomes`. `packages/crypto/src/invitations.ts` adds the owner-signed invitation statement ("OZI" v1, documented in the package README): project, invitation id, owner, a labelled SHA-256 of the invitee's normalized email, role, at most seven days of validity, and the signing device's id and key hash, signed by that certified device with its own label; `verifyProjectInvitation` refuses non-canonical bytes, another signing key, a forged signature, an expired or future statement and another context with typed reasons. `apps/web/worker/e2eeInvitations.ts` serves `POST`/`GET /api/e2ee/projects/:id/invitations` and `DELETE …/invitations/:id` (owner only; the statement is verified against the owner's newest device list, the plaintext route's recipient rules and invitation budget apply, only the email hash is stored, and the email names the sender, never the project, with an `#encrypted-invite=` link), `POST /api/e2ee/invitations/accept` (binds the signed-in account whose email hashes to the statement's; no membership yet) and `GET /api/e2ee/invitations`, `POST …/key-packages` (the accepted invitee's certified devices, one KeyPackage and leaf per project and device, republishing replaces an unconsumed one), `POST …/key-packages/claim` (owner; one statement claims every unclaimed KeyPackage of the invitation, and a claimed one is never handed out again, also enforced by a trigger) and `GET …/welcome?deviceId=`. The Add form of `POST …/mls/commits` states the invitation, the claimed KeyPackages and the Welcome: the Worker refuses one that does not name exactly the owner's claimed, unconsumed KeyPackages (`E2EE_ADD_MISMATCH`), and the same conditions gate the Commit row inside the batch, which consumes the KeyPackages, registers their leaves, stores the Welcome per recipient KeyPackage, inserts the `project_members` row with the invitation's role, completes the invitation and logs the access event. Accepted encrypted invitations relate owner and invitee for device-list lookups (M2; a pending one relates nobody, since the server holds only the email hash); project purge and account erasure clear the new tables after a cached probe. The plaintext invitation routes are unchanged and keep refusing encrypted projects; an encrypted invitation's token is unknown to them. Ordinary traffic does not depend on 0038; the flags stay off and the migration needs its own authorization before it reaches production. Evidence: `test/e2ee-invitations.test.ts` (the real Worker over SQLite with owner-signed statements and Commits from the owner's in-process MLS engine: the email without the project name; forged, misdirected, uncertified, foreign-project and expired statements refused; acceptance bound to the email's account and refused to the owner and others; KeyPackages only from the invitee's certified devices, a second claim refused; the Add batch mirroring the member, leaves, Welcomes, consumed KeyPackages and completed invitation; Adds that omit, add or alter KeyPackages refused with nothing changed; a claim changed between the check and the batch landing nothing; revocation; erasure; and plaintext invitations, Commits and contacts lookups working with migrations only through 0037), `packages/crypto/src/invitations.test.ts`, `vectors.test.ts` (the email hash and a statement with a recorded signature re-checked with raw WebCrypto) and `fuzz.test.ts`. Phase 3, Keychain cleanup (the four findings deferred on #842): no flow leaves an unreachable Keychain key. Every device creation path (setup, enrollment, approval) goes through one candidate wrapper, `withNewDevice` in `accountKeys.ts`, which discards the new keys through the store's `discardCandidate` when the flow stops before the store write (the settings close or the session changes while the Keychain is creating or importing them); from the write on, `keychainDeviceStore` keeps a candidate's keys only if its stored record holds them, whether the write resolved or rejected, so a rejected IndexedDB `getOrCreateDevice`, compare-and-swap or save removes them too. A confirmed erasure that signs out also removes the device record and its Keychain keys, keys first, so a refused native removal keeps the record and key id for the account's next removal (user ids are stable per email). `openzcad-device-keys::create` returns a `CreateError` whose `key_id` names a signing key it stored but could not remove after a failed unlock-key write; the `create_device_keys` command passes it to the renderer as `NativeDeviceKeysError.keyId`, which retries the removal at once and before each later creation. Evidence: `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts` ("desktop Keychain device keys": session ending during setup, enrollment and approval key creation; a rejected IndexedDB setup write; erasure removing the device and native keys, and a refused removal retried), `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/keychainDeviceKeys.test.ts` (rejected writes, `discardCandidate`, leftover retry), `apps/web/src/lib/desktopBridge.test.ts`, and the crate tests for the double failure. The Keychain calls themselves run only on macOS; the manual Apple Silicon workflow was not dispatched. Phase 5, slice 5-5, member removal and leaving in end-to-end encrypted projects, behind the same flags as slices 5-2 to 5-4 (`E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED`, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, the sharing rollout), with migration 0039 `e2ee_mls_proposals`. The owner removes a member by an MLS Commit whose `dropUsers` takes the identity out of the roles with every leaf it holds (`removeMember` in `projectGroups.ts`; the sharing dialog's encrypted branch offers Remove, with the copy that removal keeps someone out of every later version and not what they already opened); the Worker deletes the member's `project_members` row with a `member-removed` event in the Commit's own batch (slice 5-4's stated-change check), and, gated on that event having landed, its account-wrapped keys of the project (housekeeping, not revocation) and its assistant consent rows (closing 6-1's gap).
