A member leaves by a SelfRemove proposal, never a Commit of its own (RFC 9420 §12.2): its device first removes the account's other leaves in a Commit of its own, then posts the proposal (`leaveProject`; Leave project… in the dialog, confirmed after saying access goes at once), which `POST /api/e2ee/projects/:id/mls/proposals` stores after checking its framing (a member's PublicMessage proposal of the SelfRemove type for this group and epoch, `parseSelfRemoveProposal`) and, in the same batch, drops the member's access (decision M4): the row, the wraps and the consent go then, not when someone commits. Every member Commit for an epoch must settle every proposal still pending in it, as applied by reference or as rejected by the committer's engine (`proposals`, `rejectedProposals`), or the Worker refuses it (409 `E2EE_PROPOSALS_PENDING`; the Commit row's insert is gated on exactly that set), so a Commit that raced a departure cannot leave it behind and a bad proposal cannot hold the group; an external Commit (a rejoin, which cannot carry proposals) is refused while one is pending (409 `E2EE_DEPARTURE_PENDING`). In the browser every Commit first fetches and processes the proposals of its epoch (only a SelfRemove of the sender's own leaf, for this group and epoch, reaches the engine, since any other cached proposal would make every Commit of the epoch break the policy), receivers process the proposals a Commit applied before it (`processCommit`), and writers commit pending departures before their next write (`ensureProjectRotated`, from the group record's `pendingProposals`); any member may commit one, a viewer included, and the page does whenever it reads the member's role (`commitPendingDepartures`). A departure leaves the identity listed in the roles without a leaf, which only the owner changes: the owner's next write drops identities that hold no leaf and that the server reports as departed, so no other device of a leaver can rejoin under the MLS rejoin rules. A Commit that applies a departure or drops someone replaces the dedupe secret (decision M5), and a pending Commit is discarded on crash recovery while proposals are pending. `ProjectGroupContext.onMembersRemoved` (and `subscribeMemberRemovals` in `encryptedProjects.ts`) is told whenever a Commit this page sent or processed took other members' last leaf: the hook for slice 5-10 to offer reissuing the project's share links. A removed or departed member's page reads no role (the Worker answers 404) and is held in View with "Waiting for project access"; a member who left goes back to the projects. Migration 0039 has a cached probe that answers "missing" on any failure (`isE2eeMlsProposalStorageReady`): without it the proposal listing is empty and says so, leaving answers 503, and Commits, the owner's removals, role changes and writes work as before; project purge deletes the table's rows and account erasure (`all`) clears their sender, as for Commits. Plaintext sharing, removal and roles are unchanged. Evidence: `test/e2ee-member-removal.test.ts` (Node devices of three accounts, the in-process MLS engine and the real Worker over SQLite: an owner's removal dropping the identity, its row, wraps and consent in the Commit, replacing the dedupe secret and telling the hook, after which the removed member reads no role, holds no key of the new epoch even when a lying server serves it the Commits, cannot rejoin to write and is refused by the Worker; a pending departure of the removed member committed before the drop; removals by non-owners refused by the browser and the Worker; a member leaving from one of its two devices, its other leaf removed first, its access gone at the proposal, the departure committed by a viewer reading its role with a new dedupe secret, the hook told on the viewer's and the owner's devices, and the identity dropped by the owner's next write; an editor committing a departure before its write; the owner's leaving, a non-SelfRemove message, a Commit that leaves a departure out and a rejoin while one is pending refused, and a proposal with a forged signature rejected while the real one is applied; without migration 0039, removals, role changes, writes and purge working and leaving answering 503; erasure and purge of stored proposals), `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/mls/epochRoles.test.ts` (the SelfRemove framing checked against the engine), `ProjectSharingDialog.test.tsx`, `d1-migration-split.test.ts`, and Playwright `encrypted-member-removal.spec.ts` (Alice removes Bob from the sharing dialog: his row and wraps go, the Worker answers his page 404 and his page holds him in View with "Waiting for project access"; Bob, a viewer, leaves from the dialog: his access goes when the proposal is stored, his page returns to the projects, and Alice's next edit commits the departure and drops him from the roles). Device-list lookups and the contacts list no longer outlive what they were for (#898 and #902 review): an accepted invitation relates its two sides only while it is open (an encrypted one until its Add completes it or it expires), so a removed member loses the relation and the email with the membership, while the owner and current members of a project still read the device list of anyone who held a leaf in it, so a removed member's versions keep verifying (`test/e2ee-contacts.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-member-lists.test.ts`). Phase 5, slice 5-4, role changes in end-to-end encrypted projects, behind the same flags as slices 5-2 to 5-3b (`E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED`, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, the sharing rollout), with no migration. The owner changes a member's role by an MLS Commit whose `setRoles` every member's policy accepts only from the owner (`changeMemberRole` in `projectGroups.ts`; the sharing dialog's encrypted branch now offers a role control per member, through `EncryptedSharingClient.changeRole`). A Commit states the membership change it makes (`E2eeMlsCommitRequest.roles` and `dropUsers`), and the Worker (`apps/web/worker/e2eeMembership.ts`) checks the statement and mirrors it into `project_members` in the Commit's own batch: only the owner states a change, only for current members, the mirror and a `member-role-changed` (or `member-removed`) access event land with the Commit or not at all, and the Commit row's insert is gated on every target still holding the role the check read. Every Commit carries its new epoch's GroupInfo; the Worker reads the roles extension from it (`parseGroupInfoContext` in `mls/epochRoles.ts`) and from the stored GroupInfo of the current epoch, and refuses (409 `E2EE_ROLES_MISMATCH`) any Commit whose new roles are not the current ones with exactly the stated changes applied (an Add's invitee with its invitation's role, the stated role changes and drops), so an unstated role change, or a stated one the Commit does not make, never reaches the mirror. Ownership never moves on an encrypted project: the browser refuses to build it and the Worker refuses an `owner` role, a change that targets the owner, and any new GroupInfo naming another owner (409 `E2EE_OWNERSHIP_TRANSFER_UNAVAILABLE`), though the MLS policy alone would allow a hand-over. A pending Commit that changes roles is discarded on crash recovery rather than resent without its statement. In the page, a member's role in someone else's encrypted project comes from the project's MLS roles (`memberships.ts` `ownProjectRole`: this device's session after catching up, or the epoch's key archive on a device without a leaf), never the server's mirror, read on open, on focus, every 20 s and when a write is refused as read-only (`useEncryptedMemberRole`): an editor edits and autosaves through the encrypted store (no room or lease), a viewer gets the existing read-only UI (View mode, no Build tools, edits refused with "This shared project is read-only"). The edit-lease rule that 5-3a carried over from the plaintext invitation route no longer applies to encrypted projects: it keeps a plaintext editor's writes in the lease-checked room instead of the account route, while an encrypted project's editors write versions the account accepts only on top of the head they read, and its room serves only the owner until slice 5-6 (which decides the room's leases); the canary recipient rule still applies, and editor invitations and role changes are offered in the encrypted dialog whatever the lease rollout. Plaintext sharing, roles and leases are unchanged. Evidence: `test/e2ee-member-roles.test.ts` (Node devices of three accounts, the in-process MLS engine and the real Worker over SQLite: an editor demoted, the mirror and event moved with the Commit, its earlier version verified by a fresh owner browser, its next write refused by its own browser before any request and, forced with leaked keys, by the Worker, and refused by every browser when a lying server keeps the editor row; a non-owner's role change refused by its browser, its MLS policy and the Worker; the owner's change sent unstated, and a stated change the Commit does not make, refused with nothing changed; ownership transfer refused by the browser and, stated or not, by the Worker; the account's role read from the MLS roles even when the mirror lies, also on a device without a leaf; a crash-pending role change discarded, not resent), `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/mls/epochRoles.test.ts` (GroupInfo roles parsed and checked against the engine), `ProjectSharingDialog.test.tsx`, the updated `test/e2ee-invitations.test.ts`, and Playwright `encrypted-roles.spec.ts` (Alice shares with Bob as an editor through slice 5-3b's flow; Bob models and his version lands; Alice demotes him from the sharing dialog; Bob's next edit is refused by his browser, which switches to View with the Build tools gone; a write forced from his page gets 403 `E2EE_PROJECT_READ_ONLY` from the Worker; Alice reopens and reads his earlier version). Phase 5, slice 5-2, member-aware storage for end-to-end encrypted projects, with no migration. The Worker's encrypted routes replace the owner-only check and `PROJECT_FENCE` with role-aware access from `project_members` (`requireEncryptedProjectAccess`, and `MEMBER_FENCE` and `WRITER_FENCE` inside every write statement). Any member reads documents, versions, revisions, project info, content blobs, measurements, view state, the MLS group, key archives, leaves and resets. Only the owner reads a project in the recycle bin. The owner and editors write document versions (account and room), revisions, project info, content blobs (counted against the owner's artifact storage) and measurements, and a viewer's write is refused with 403 `E2EE_PROJECT_READ_ONLY`. Any member keeps its own view state, as plaintext workspace sessions allow, and any member sends MLS Commits, since every member's browser runs the policy that decides what a Commit may do. Group creation and resets stay owner-only. Any member copies a project it reads, into a project of its own. `POST /api/e2ee/projects/:id/keys` stores the caller's own account-wrapped project key and rejoin PSK for an epoch of the current group, posted by a certified device and never replacing a stored wrap. `GET …/keys` still serves only the caller's own wraps. `GET /api/e2ee/projects/:id/revocation-cutoffs` serves the other members' identity-signed cutoff statements to members. On format-2 projects the plaintext member `PATCH` and `DELETE` (`/members/:id`) move from metadata to refused as sharing (`ENCRYPTED_PROJECT_SHARING_UNAVAILABLE`), since a role changes only by a Commit; plaintext projects are unchanged. In the browser, `projectGroups.ts` takes the co-members' verified device lists (`ProjectGroupContext.members`, which contacts and pins supply from slice 5-1; without it a project stays solo and another account's leaf is refused as before). With them, the MLS directory, key archives and authorship accept co-members' devices with their own verified revocation cutoffs. A device that processes someone else's Commit posts its account's own wraps of the new epoch (`postOwnEpochKeys`). `epochRoles` reads the roles extension from each epoch's archived GroupContext (`mls/epochRoles.ts`, a strict TLS parser checked against the engine), or from the device's own session for the epoch it holds. The store's `requireAuthority`, the version log check, and the check of project info and measurements written by co-members now require the role the author held in the version's own epoch. They no longer pass an author with no role entry, and they keep a demoted editor's earlier versions valid. A viewer's own browser refuses to send a write. Evidence: `test/e2ee-member-storage.test.ts` (Node devices of two accounts and an outsider; a member is added by the owner's `setRoles` Commit and joins by External Commit with a rejoin PSK it posts as its own, standing in for slice 5-3's Welcome): a member reads and an editor writes a version, project info, measurements and a file that the owner's fresh browser verifies, the owner posts its own wraps on processing the member's Commit, and a solo-configured browser refuses the member's leaf; a viewer's write is refused by its own browser, by the Worker (versions, blobs, measurements) and, when a lying server lets it in, by every browser; a demoted editor's version still verifies while the demoted browser stops writing; a removed member's later version is refused by an honest Worker and, served anyway, by every browser; the wrap route serves and stores only the caller's own wraps, keeps stored ones and refuses unknown epochs, foreign devices and outsiders; the cutoff route serves other members' statements to members only; an author with no role entry is refused even with a leaf. Also covered by `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/mls/epochRoles.test.ts` and the updated access cases in `test/e2ee-project-documents.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-project-groups.test.ts` and `test/e2ee-project-format.test.ts`. The flags stay off. Phase 7, slice 7-2, the browser engine that encrypts an existing plaintext project and its progress UI, behind 7-1's off-by-default `E2EE_MIGRATION_ENABLED` (no new flag, no migration). `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/projectMigrationEngine.ts` encrypts one project at a time through the existing encrypted clients (`encryptedProjectStore.ts`, `encryptedContentBlobs.ts`, `encryptedProjectInfo.ts`, `replacedEnvelopes.ts`), whose every request names the lease (`withMigrationLease`): it saves this page's pending edits to the project, starts (or resumes) the staging and renews the lease in the background; then, oldest first in the head's own order, stages each retained plaintext revision as an encrypted version plus a named save under its plaintext revision id (its document, names and reasons inside the ciphertext; decision M9), the first one creating the solo MLS group and project info, with every file a document names (STEP and mesh sources, reference pictures) stored first as a content blob through 4-6's dedupe tags and the document naming its blob id; then the head at `sourceVersion + 1` or later, the project info (name, thumbnail, archived exports), measurements and view state; then, before the cutover, refuses to proceed on staged work a device the account no longer lists signed or holds a leaf of (7-1's gap: a device removed while a project was staged is handled by abandoning and staging again from the plaintext, under a new group and dedupe secret, rather than rotating), and checks that the head's signed revision map names every retained save state; then cuts over and drives the purge to completion. The staged version log is the journal: a resumed run verifies the staged head, reads its signed revision map, opens each staged save state (so dedupe finds its files) and stages only the rest; `E2EE_MIGRATION_LEASE_LOST` resumes by starting again, `E2EE_MIGRATION_STALE` (and an unverifiable journal) abandons and starts over, forgetting the staged head's pins, cached keys and MLS state (`forgetStagedProject`, `VersionPinStore.forget`, `createProjectGroup` `replace`); shared projects are refused (decision M7; this base has no 5-3a invitations). The store gains `stageMigratedProject` (the group, first version and info through their own routes, as the lease requires) and `revisionMap`, and now maps a named-save head's own revision when it is read back (its body cannot name itself), which also fixes a reload-after-named-save gap of ordinary encrypted projects. The Worker's room lock now answers its plaintext version and a fresh start refuses with 409 `E2EE_MIGRATION_ROOM_AHEAD`, staging nothing, while the room holds edits the account never saved. `projectMigrations.ts` runs the engine once per browser (Web Lock), keeps the lease in this browser's storage so a reload resumes at once, and feeds Settings › Encryption › "Encrypt existing projects" (`ExistingProjectEncryption.tsx`: per-project state, save states and MB done of the total, pause and resume, errors with retry, cancel, a keep-this-tab-open note, and plain copy that plaintext copies may persist up to 30 days in backups, that lost keys mean lost cloud data, and to export an `.openzcad` backup first) and the shelf's lock badge while a project is encrypted. No flag was enabled, no migration was applied remotely and no real project was migrated. Evidence: `test/e2ee-project-migration-engine.test.ts` (real Worker, room class and SQLite with the in-process MLS engine: a project with a STEP import, three save states, a thumbnail, an export, measurements and a view state migrated, cut over and purged; plaintext routes answer 409; a scan of D1, R2 and room storage finds no plaintext; a fresh browser opens it with every save state, the STEP bytes, thumbnail, export, measurements and view state; resume after an interruption mid-staging with one stored STEP blob; pause and resume; a lease lost to another tab, which finishes; a stale project abandoned and restarted; a removed device's staging restarted; a shared project refused; a room holding unsaved edits refused; a named-save head read back), `apps/web/src/components/ExistingProjectEncryption.test.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/projectFormat.test.tsx` and Playwright `test/e2e/encrypted-migration.spec.ts` (migrate through the UI, reload mid-way, resume, open the result with its STEP import rebuilt). Follow-up (three #916 review findings): a fresh start now fails closed with 503 `PROJECT_ROOM_UNAVAILABLE`, staging nothing, unless the room confirms the version it holds or that it holds none, which the engine reports as a retryable failed start; a project being encrypted, or whose paused or stopped staging or unfinished purge keeps its plaintext locked, is held read-only in the editor (View, every edit refused with the reason), as is one whose autosave answers `PROJECT_MIGRATING` (now a refusal, not a conflict); and a project cut over before its purge completed stays listed as still finishing so Retry resumes the purge (evidence: `test/e2ee-project-migration-engine.test.ts`, `test/cloud-project-autosave.test.ts`, `ExistingProjectEncryption.test.tsx`, `projectFormat.test.tsx`, and the Playwright migration spec opening the project read-only mid-staging). Second follow-up (two #922 review findings): an open plaintext workspace now locks itself read-only as soon as another tab or device starts encrypting its project, from the room's 4003 close or its migration status (asked on open, on focus and every 30 s, only when migration is open to the account), keeping queued edits unsent until the status shows the staging abandoned, and a refresh with no in-memory record still finds a cut-over project whose purge is unfinished (its encrypted projects asked about four at a time, at most 24 per refresh) and lists it with Retry (evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/projectMigrationWatch.test.ts`, `useCollaboration.test.tsx`, `test/cloud-project-autosave.test.ts`, `ExistingProjectEncryption.test.tsx`, and the Playwright migration spec locking, then unlocking, the project open on another device). Third follow-up (two #924 review findings): the open workspace's migration watch, its lock and the autosave halt now follow the format of the document the workspace loaded rather than the format cache, so a cutover a listing learned of keeps the stale plaintext read-only until the project is closed or reopened (a project loaded encrypted is never held), and a project an earlier refresh only listed as waiting that another device cut over is asked about again within the bounded discovery and listed with Retry (evidence: `projectMigrationWatch.test.ts`, `ExistingProjectEncryption.test.tsx`). Phase 7, slice 7-3, the beta-only reverse path that turns an encrypted project back into plaintext, behind the new off-by-default `E2EE_REVERSE_ENABLED` (open only to accounts migration is open to, so it also needs `E2EE_MIGRATION_ENABLED` and encrypted projects; reported in `/api/e2ee/config` as `reverseEnabled` only once open and migration 0043 is installed; Phase 8 deletes it). It reuses migration 0042's state machine with direction `decrypt`; D1 migration `0043_e2ee_reverse_staging.sql` adds no table, only the view `e2ee_decrypt_staged_projects` (format-2 projects with a decrypt row `staging` or `abandoned`) and replaces migration 0022's artifact, document-object and storage-asset quota triggers, migration 0017's finalized-usage counters and migration 0042's version quota so the plaintext staged beside an encrypted project counts toward nothing until the cutover (mirroring decision M10; before any decrypt row the view is empty and every answer is unchanged). The start (`apps/web/worker/e2eeProjectMigrations.ts`) refuses unless the caller owns a live format-2 project with no members, pending invitations, live share links or (when migration 0038 is installed) pending encrypted invitations, and the account's "Encrypt new projects" opt-in is off (decision D2, 409 `E2EE_MIGRATION_OPTED_IN`), all fenced again in the insert; it locks the encrypted room. While staging, encrypted content writes (documents, versions, revisions, project info, measurements, view state, blobs; MLS key management keeps working) and the room answer 409 `PROJECT_MIGRATING` (`e2ee.ts`, `projectFormatGate.ts`), and the owner's lease holder writes the decrypted plaintext under the same project id through the ordinary plaintext routes naming the lease (`apps/web/worker/e2eeReverseStaging.ts`): save states (`POST …/revisions`, under their own revision ids) and the head (`PUT …/document`, recorded on the migration row by version and checksum) through `D1R2PersistenceService.stageDecryptedDocument`, files through the plaintext upload sessions and finalize, each fenced on the lease in its own SQL and never touching the project row; measurements and view state through `PUT …/migration/plaintext/details`; `GET …/migration/plaintext` is the journal. The cutover is one batch: every retained encrypted save state has a staged plaintext save state, the staged head is newer than the encrypted head, which (with the encrypted measurements) did not move, still unshared and not opted in; it flips `storage_format` to 1 at the staged head and moves the owner's artifact usage from the blobs' ciphertext to the staged files. The purge clears the room's encrypted ledger (a `format: 2` room migration request) and then drops groups, leaves, Commits, wraps, archives, resets, versions and their R2 bodies, envelopes, blobs and their R2 objects a bounded step at a time, through the cleanup an abandoned encrypt staging uses; an abandon drops the staged plaintext rows and objects the same way, and the hourly sweep finishes either. The cutover also refuses, with 413 `ARTIFACT_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` or `PROJECT_STORAGE_QUOTA` in the same statement that ends the staging exemption (non-retryable in the engine; the project stays encrypted and can be abandoned), when the owner's artifact usage or project storage after it would exceed their limit, a further staged upload is refused early once that limit is already reached, and a resumed staging reuses a journaled head of the same version and checksum while the purge's last step drops any staged head the cutover left unreferenced, a staged file's upload session records the decrypt lease that opened it and advances only under that live lease (never under a lease that took over, nor as an ordinary upload after the cutover), and a staged save state keeps the author the encrypted history records for it when that account still exists (all covered in `test/e2ee-project-migration-reverse.test.ts`). Project deletion now drops the migration row last, so a deleted mid-staging project's exempt files never leave usage behind. In the browser, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/projectDecryptionEngine.ts` reads through the verifying encrypted store, decrypts every blob a save state or the head names into an ordinary file (STEP archive, mesh payload, reference picture, thumbnail, archived exports) named in its metadata by its blob id, rewrites the documents to the files' artifact ids, stages oldest first, the head at `sourceVersion + 1` under the project info's name, then measurements and view state, cuts over (forgetting the encrypted head, pins and keys) and purges; it resumes from the journal, restarts on a moved head and holds the encrypting runner's Web Lock (`projectDecryption.ts`). Settings › Encryption › "Stop encrypting a project" (`StopProjectEncryption.tsx`) appears only when the flag is on, says plainly that the project's content will be stored readable by the server again, and asks for a confirmation per project. No flag was enabled, no migration was applied remotely and no real project was migrated. Evidence: `test/e2ee-project-migration-reverse.test.ts` (real Worker, room class and SQLite with the in-process MLS engine: a project encrypted by the 7-2 engine with a STEP import, three save states, a thumbnail, an export, measurements and a view state turned back into plaintext by a fresh device, reopened with every save state, the STEP bytes, thumbnail, export, measurements and view state, saving again, with no encrypted row, R2 object or room key left and usage equal to the files; encrypted writes, blob uploads and the room ticket refused while staging, staged files and documents exempt from quota; resume after an interruption without storing a file twice; a lapsed lease taken over; abandon dropping every staged row and object and leaving it writable; a moved head; the sweep finishing a dropped purge; deletion mid-staging; re-encryption afterwards; refusals for a member, a share link, the opt-in, another account, a plaintext and a trashed project; flag off and migration 0043 missing), `apps/web/src/components/StopProjectEncryption.test.tsx`, `test/e2ee-encrypted-copies.test.ts`, `test/d1-migration-split.test.ts`, `test/cloudflare-adapters.test.ts` and Playwright `test/e2e/encrypted-migration-reverse.spec.ts` (encrypt through the UI, stop encrypting through the UI after the warning and confirmation, then the project opens from the shelf as plaintext and rebuilds its STEP import). Phase 7, slice 7-4: encrypting shared plaintext projects (decisions M7(a) and M8), behind the existing off-by-default `E2EE_MIGRATION_ENABLED`; no new flag and no new D1 migration (it reuses migration 0038's encrypted invitations, KeyPackages and Welcomes and migration 0042's state). An existing member is re-added through slice 5-3's invitation machinery: the owner's device signs an invitation statement naming the member's email hash and current role, and `POST /api/e2ee/projects/:id/migration/invitations` (`apps/web/worker/e2eeMigrationMembers.ts`) stores it bound to that member at once (they are already a member, so no email and no usable token), replacing their older open one; the member's browser lists it with its other accepted invitations, verifies the owner (the mutual comparison) and publishes a KeyPackage from each device, which `e2eeInvitations.ts` now takes for a format-1 project only from a current member and only while migration is open. `GET …/migration/members` lists each member with their role, whether they have keys, their newest re-add invitation with its KeyPackage counts and whether this staging re-added them, plus the live plaintext links and pending invitations the cutover will revoke. 7-1's "no members" refusal is relaxed to "members must be ready": the start (fenced again in its insert) needs every member to hold a live re-add invitation with their current role and an unconsumed KeyPackage (or, on a resume, to be re-added already), so a shared project is never locked while the owner waits; pending plaintext invitations no longer refuse it, and only a pending unaccepted encrypted invitation (and whatever else main's `encryptedSharingSql` counts as sharing, such as live encrypted share links once that check lands) still does (`E2EE_MIGRATION_SHARED`). The cutover needs every member re-added by an Add Commit of this staging with their current role and a registered leaf (409 `E2EE_MIGRATION_MEMBERS_NOT_READY`, naming each member and the reason: `no-keys`, `not-invited`, `waiting-for-keys` or `not-readded`), and its one batch flips the format, mirrors the re-added members (role from their Add's invitation, an access event each), revokes plaintext links and pending invitations (decision M8) and ends any unused re-add invitation. The Add Commits are made under the migration lease (claim and Commit accept the staged project's lease holder); a staged Add leaves `project_members` and the access log to the cutover, and an abandoned staging drops its Welcomes, consumed KeyPackages and completed re-add invitations with its group, so a new staging re-adds everyone again. In the browser, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/migrationMembers.ts` readies members (making or renewing their re-add invitations), says where each stands (needs encryption, waiting for their keys, compare codes, ready, added back) and, just before the cutover, re-adds each by Welcome with slice 5-3b's `addInvitedMember` (which checks the statement, that the member is a verified contact and every claimed KeyPackage); the 7-2 engine waits in a new `members` phase until every member is ready and re-adds them in a `readding` phase, the migration staging clients now carry co-members' device lists and an invitation context, and after the cutover the progress lists the revoked links and invitations. Settings › Encryption › "Encrypt existing projects" lists each member of a waiting project with what they or the owner need to do, offers "Check again", and after the cutover offers encrypted links of the same modes (slice 5-10's `createShareLink`, new addresses) and encrypted invitations to the same addresses and roles. No flag was enabled, no migration was applied remotely and no real project was migrated. Evidence: `test/e2ee-project-migration-shared.test.ts` (real Worker, room class and SQLite with the in-process MLS engine: a project shared with an editor and a viewer waits until both published keys and were verified, naming who needs to do what, while nothing is staged or locked; then both are re-added and the migration completes with their roles kept, both join by Welcome, the editor saves and the viewer is refused; the plaintext link and pending invitation are revoked and encrypted ones issued; the cutover refuses a staging whose members were not re-added and completes once resumed; an abandon drops the re-adds and a new staging re-adds both; with migration closed the member routes answer 501 and no KeyPackage is taken), `test/e2ee-project-migrations.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-project-migration-engine.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/ExistingProjectEncryption.test.tsx` and Playwright `test/e2e/encrypted-migration-shared.spec.ts` (Alice starts encrypting a project shared with Bob, sees him listed as not ready; Bob compares codes with her and his browser publishes his keys; she compares codes with him and the project is encrypted, Bob is an editor of it, the plaintext link is revoked with an encrypted one offered, and Bob opens the encrypted project from his shelf). Restacked onto main after #918, #921, #922 and #912 (cherry-picks of the integration-branch commits, with 0d2145e2's room test folded in, which now runs against main's slice 5-6): the encrypt start and cutover keep main's encrypted-invitation check and exclude only re-add invitations, which are bound to a member; #922's `plaintextLocked` progress is carried through the `members` phase, so a resumed staging waiting for a member stays read-only and can still be cancelled, while a cut-over one cannot; #922's unfinished cutovers stay listed beside 7-4's rows offering encrypted links and invitations. Two follow-ups compose 7-4 with #918: the staged-leaf fence on device removal and recovery-key replacement now counts the account's leaves in any project staged for encryption, not only its own, so a member's device re-added to someone else's staged project is held back until the cutover or an abandon (`test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts`); and a staged re-add needs each added device certified in the member's newest device list and is fenced on that list in its batch (409 `E2EE_ADD_MISMATCH`, `test/e2ee-project-migration-shared.test.ts`). The re-add Commits go through 5-3b's `addInvitedMember` and so state their device list (#913). A further test shows a member's plaintext autosave, named save and room ticket refused with `PROJECT_MIGRATING` while the project is staged. Playwright `encrypted-migration-shared.spec.ts` needed no change on main (it creates no box and uses the retrying `closeSettings`). Phase 7, slice 7-1, migration state and the server's role in moving an existing plaintext project to encrypted storage, behind the new off-by-default `E2EE_MIGRATION_ENABLED` flag (only for accounts encrypted projects are open to; reported in `/api/e2ee/config` as `migrationEnabled` only once open and migration 0042 is installed). D1 migration `0042_e2ee_project_migrations.sql` adds `e2ee_project_migrations` (one row per project: direction `encrypt` or `decrypt`, state `staging`, `cutover`, `purging`, `done` or `abandoned` moving only along that machine, owner, device, a 32-byte lease with its expiry, the plaintext head and measurement revision staging began from, the staged head, the cutover's counts, a purge cursor and purge counts; project and user cascades, erasure fences that let profile erasure only forget the device and lease) and replaces migration 0033's version quota and 0034's blob accounting triggers so a format-1 project's staged ciphertext counts toward nothing until the cutover (decision M10); before 0042 every encrypted row belongs to a format-2 project, so their answers are unchanged. `apps/web/worker/e2eeProjectMigrations.ts` serves `/api/e2ee/projects/:id/migration` (`GET`; `POST` start, owner only, a live plaintext project with no members or pending invitations (decision M7) and nothing staged left over, or a resume once the lease lapsed; `…/lease` renew; `…/cutover`; `…/abandon`; `…/purge`). While a project is staged its plaintext writes, named saves, duplicates, sharing, uploads and room (tickets and sockets; the start also closes the room's connections) answer 409 `PROJECT_MIGRATING` through the format gate, and reads keep working; the encrypted-project routes of slices 4-4 to 4-7 (group create and Commits, wraps, versions, revisions, project info, content blobs and their upload sessions, measurements and view state) take ciphertext for that format-1 project only from the owner's device holding the live lease (`x-e2ee-migration-lease`, `e2eeMigrationLease.ts`), every write fencing on the lease in its own SQL; staged versions move the migration row's staged head, never `projects`, and the first builds on nothing. The cutover is one D1 batch that checks what the server can see (every retained plaintext revision id has a staged named save whose body is retained, decision M9; a staged head newer than the plaintext head, with its group and project info; the plaintext head and measurements unchanged since staging began; still no members or pending invitations), flips `storage_format` to 2 at the staged head, revokes plaintext share links and unaccepted invitations (decision M8) and starts counting the staged blobs as artifact usage. Abandon unlocks the plaintext at once and drops the staged rows, upload sessions and objects a bounded step per call. Purge, driven by the owner's browser and finished by the hourly scheduled sweep for one left idle, deletes a bounded step per call and resumes from its cursor: the room's plaintext keys through a new internal room endpoint, the row's name and document, revisions, R2 documents, assets and artifacts (keeping any object another project's row still names), plaintext upload sessions, measurements and workspace sessions; share links serve a projection built from the deleted document and are revoked. The server never transforms content. `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/projectMigrationsApi.ts` adds the thin client and `withMigrationLease`; the engine and UI are slice 7-2. Ordinary traffic does not depend on 0042 or the flag: off, no statement names the table; without the migration only a cached probe does. The flag stays off; no migration was applied remotely and no real project was migrated. Evidence: `test/e2ee-project-migrations.test.ts` (Node devices with the in-process MLS engine against the real Worker, its room class and SQLite: a lease conflict and hand-over with the old holder's writes, renewal and cutover refused; staging only by the lease holder, a lease lapsing between check and write landing nothing; `PROJECT_MIGRATING` for plaintext writes, named saves, room ticket and socket, share links, measurements and uploads while reads work; cutover refused until every save state is staged with its body, when the plaintext moved, when shared, and for a head not newer than the plaintext's; an atomic cutover rolled back whole by an injected failure, then flipping the format and revoking a link and an invitation, opened by a fresh device; abandon resuming after a failed delete, unlocking at once and dropping every staged row and object; purge resuming after an interruption and leaving no plaintext in D1, R2 or the room; an object another project names kept and a copy still working; the M10 quota exemption for versions and blobs and counting from the cutover on; profile and full erasure, recycle-bin purge and the sweep; flag off, and migrations only through 0041), `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/projectMigrationsApi.test.ts`, `test/cloudflare-adapters.test.ts` and `test/d1-migration-split.test.ts`. Review fixes (#914): a plaintext upload session opened before staging began answers 409 `PROJECT_MIGRATING` on its content, multipart, part, completion and finalize calls while the flag is on, and every statement that advances it or lands its artifact fences on the migration state in its own SQL, so one checked just before the start lands nothing after it (the session is kept: an abandon gives it back, the cutover's purge discards it); and a recovery-key replacement treats a project staged for encryption like an encrypted one, refusing with `E2EE_DEVICE_SIGNED_PROJECTS` to drop a device that signed its staged rows (`test/e2ee-project-migrations.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts`). An ordinary device revocation likewise refuses with `E2EE_DEVICE_SIGNED_PROJECTS` while the revoked device signed a staged project's rows (fenced in the append's batch, which also refuses with `E2EE_CUTOFFS_INCOMPLETE` if a cutover lands in between), and the hourly sweep abandons a staging with a lapsed lease whose owner may no longer migrate (say, removed from `E2EE_CANARY_EMAILS` with the flag still on), never touching a cutover or purge (`test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-project-migrations.test.ts`). Follow-up review fixes (#914): the start hands out a lease only after the project's room confirms its lock, otherwise answering 503 `PROJECT_ROOM_UNAVAILABLE` with a fresh staging back to abandoned (plaintext writable) or a resumed one left under a lapsed lease; device revocation and recovery-key replacement also wait while the device holds an MLS leaf in a staged project's group, pre-check and in-batch, even when it signed nothing; and an encrypted upload session opened during staging records its lease in its metadata, so after a takeover the old holder's part reservations, part records and completion refuse with `E2EE_MIGRATION_LEASE_LOST` (in their own SQL too), and the new holder opening the same blob drops the stale session and starts afresh (`test/e2ee-project-migrations.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts`). Phase 3 tail, the MLS client in the app (slice 3T-2): `apps/web` depends on `@openzcad/mls` and drives it from `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/mls/`, reachable only from code behind the off-by-default `E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED` availability (nothing calls it yet). `loader.ts` imports the package and starts its module Worker on first use, one per page; the wrapper, Worker and WASM stay out of the launcher (`LAZY_ENTRY_PATTERNS`, an `openzcad-mls` launcher marker and an approved 1.5 MiB lazy-asset budget for the 1,047,287-byte WASM). `sessionStore.ts` keeps a separate IndexedDB database, `openzcad-e2ee-mls` (the device-key database is untouched), with a non-extractable AES-256-GCM state key per account and, per account and project, the sealed session, the certified leaf and the bytes of any Commit the room has not ruled on; every operation runs under a per-project Web Lock and writes with compare-and-swap on a local revision, so a stale tab is refused and re-reads instead of forking. `certify.ts` certifies the leaf key with the stored device signing key through `ecdsaSign` (WebCrypto or Keychain-held keys), as `u8 1 \| r \|\| s` over the package's leaf certification payload. `directory.ts` builds the policy's `DeviceDirectory` from verified device lists plus leaf certifications, verifying each signature against the certifying device's key and refusing forged, unattributable or duplicate records; a revoked device needs a per-project revocation cutoff the device list cannot supply yet, so it is refused explicitly (`revocation-cutoff-missing`) rather than defaulted, and no `certifiedEpoch` or `replacedBy` is set (recovery Adds and the last-leaf exception stay unavailable until later slices bind them). Key-session teardown frees every session, forgets the state keys and stops the Worker; operations in flight fail without writing. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/mls/projectSessions.test.ts` (create, seal and reopen to the same project key; cross-tab re-read; a lock-less race refused by compare-and-swap with the loser re-reading; pending Commits confirmed or discarded across a reload; teardown), `directory.test.ts` (forged, wrong-project and wrong-device certifications, unknown devices and users, revoked devices with and without cutoffs, and the policy refusing uncertified and revoked leaves), `sessionStore.test.ts` (both backends), `test/bundle-size-policy.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/mls-client.spec.ts` (the built app's Worker under the shipped CSP creates a group, reloads and reopens the same key in Chromium, and in WebKit where installed). MLS leaf certifications bind the certification epoch (follow-up to slice 3T-2). The leaf certification payload is now version 2: the MLS encoding of the label `openzcad mls leaf certification v2`, project id, user id, device id, cipher suite, leaf key and a u64 `certifiedEpoch`, the group's current epoch when the device certifies (`leafCertificationPayload` and `MlsSession.certificationPayload` take it; version 1 is no longer produced). The app's certification is `u8 2 \| u64 certifiedEpoch \| r \|\| s`, so the signed epoch travels with the signature in the leaf credential; `certifyLeaf` signs from the epoch it is given (0 when creating a group), and `buildDeviceDirectory` sets `certifiedEpoch` for a leaf key only after the device's signature verifies over that epoch (two verified certifications of one key keep the earlier epoch). Recovery Adds therefore work for a key certified at or after the identity's latest leaf removal instead of always failing closed, and a server that rewrites the epoch breaks the signature. `packages/mls/pkg` was rebuilt with `build.sh` (Rust 1.96.0, wasm-bindgen 0.2.126); the lazy MLS WASM stays inside its 1.5 MiB budget. Evidence: `packages/mls/src/certification.test.ts` (pinned v2 payload vector, one payload per epoch, non-epochs refused), the main scenario step "the leaf certification payload binds the certified epoch" (in-process and Worker protocol), and `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/mls/directory.test.ts` (a certification whose claimed epoch differs from the signed one is refused, a version 1 certification is refused, and the policy accepts a recovery Add of a key certified after the latest removal and refuses one certified before it). Issue #817 follow-up: Welcome joins and external rejoins require a complete epoch archive. Public MLS transcript replay derives removals and binds them to the authenticated joining context and tree, refusing omitted or fabricated GroupInfo records. Failed joins restore one-use KeyPackages for a valid retry. ADR-024 records the original limitation and the remaining fork-consistency and historical-authorization limits. Local verification: five Rust regressions, 126 MLS Vitest tests, and 57 scenario steps each in Chromium and WebKit; a fresh Rust 1.96.0 build reproduces the committed WASM byte-for-byte. Existing live policy rules and assertions, deployment flags, archive V1 and sealed-state formats are preserved; no migration or standalone deployment was performed. Phase 3 passkey follow-up: recovery-authorized discoverable WebAuthn PRF enrollment, fresh-browser unlock and certification, and passkey/device/read-only revocation reuse the identity-certified unlock-target protocol and rotate both account and encryption keys when revoking. Credential-id hashes are bound by the signed target list; provider-visible metadata contains only random handles and labels. Failed enrollment identifies the provider credential and any possibly committed target for cleanup. Evidence: `test/e2ee-passkeys.test.ts`, passkey cases in `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts`, and real Chromium PRF controls in `test/e2e/encryption-passkeys.spec.ts`. Integration preserves the parent snapshot, pin CAS, session authority and same-device writer lock. Delayed PRF prompts cannot resume after their settings view closes, cross-tab session teardown or a subsequent authenticated session. Recovery/passkey certification re-reads durable device state and retains approval cancellation and compare-and-swap replacement. Temporary PRF and raw key buffers are cleared. `E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED` stays off; enabling it or manually applying migration 0029 still needs separate authorization. Real provider/browser qualification and the remaining E2EE phases are not claimed. Pending browser WebAuthn create/get ceremonies receive an abort signal tied to key-session teardown, releasing the same-device writer lock promptly so sign-out can revoke the session. Regression coverage includes pending enrollment, unlock and rotation at the account-key boundary, plus cross-tab sign-out while a real Chromium virtual-authenticator ceremony is waiting for presence. These controls do not establish real authenticator/provider qualification. A reviewable privacy-policy draft is in `docs/legal/privacy-policy-draft.md` (PR #787). Its factual clarifications distinguish account autosave from live room synchronization, and authentication validity/cleanup eligibility from traffic-triggered bounded deletion. Operator and legal questions remain explicit placeholders; the draft is not published or linked from the app. Phase 4 slice 4-2, crypto additions for project data (package only; no app, server or D1 change): `@openzcad/crypto` appends the `project-info` and `key-archive` project envelope kinds (codes 5 and 6, 1 MiB ceilings; the first four codes are unchanged), adds the `rejoin-psk`, `archived-project-key` and `previous-account-key` AES-KW wraps, renames the header's former roster-entry slot to `epochBindingHash` with no wire change and fills it from `epochBinding(projectId, keyEpoch, epochAuthenticator)`, and adds the version log (`versionLogRecord`, the `OZV` record codec and `verifyVersionLog`, which refuses gaps, non-increasing versions, falling key epochs, broken parent links, bad signatures and a missing pin with typed reasons), `verifyAuthoredEnvelope` (certified author device, revoked devices only within their per-project cutoff) and strict JSON body codecs for documents (with signed revision version hashes), project info and key archives that keep header blob ids equal to body references. AES-KW unwraps now re-wrap and compare, so an engine that skips the RFC 3394 integrity check (Playwright's Windows WebKit) still refuses a wrong key, context or tampered wrap. ADR-024 records these in its 2026-10-24 amendment. Evidence: `packages/crypto/src/version-log.test.ts`, `bodies.test.ts`, the new vectors in `vectors.test.ts` (existing vectors unchanged byte for byte), the project-data and non-verifying-engine cases in `wrap.test.ts`, and the log-record and body cases in `fuzz.test.ts`. Phase 4, slice 4-1, project storage format and the opt-in switch: D1 migration `0031_project_storage_format.sql` adds `projects.storage_format` (1 plaintext, 2 e2ee-v1; default 1) and `e2ee_project_preferences` (one MAC'd opt-in record per account, cascaded with the user, fenced during erasure, sequence only forward, removed by profile erasure). One guard, `assertPlaintextProject`, refuses every document, revision, measurement, view-state, artifact-list and room route of a format-2 project with 409 `PROJECT_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED` ("This project is end-to-end encrypted. Update OpenZCAD to open it."), both inside the persistence methods and in a Worker gate every `/api/projects/:id` route meets before its handler; invitations, share links, invitation acceptance and member room tickets refuse with 409 `ENCRYPTED_PROJECT_SHARING_UNAVAILABLE`, existing share links serve nothing, listings build format-2 summaries from row columns only (placeholder name, no thumbnail, `storageFormat: 2`), and the assistant route refuses format-2 projects (decision D7). Behind the new off-by-default `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED` flag (only for accounts the key routes are open to, reported in `/api/e2ee/config` as `projectsEnabled` and disabled until migration 0031 is applied), Settings › Encryption shows "Encrypt new projects": the browser writes `{userId, encryptNewProjects, sequence}` MAC'd with HMAC-SHA-256 under an HKDF of the account key (`OpenZCAD E2EE v1 project preferences mac`, test vector in `packages/crypto/src/account-records.test.ts`), pins the sequence locally and refuses a server copy with a bad MAC or an older sequence; once an account opts in, plaintext create, adopt and duplicate are refused with 409 `ENCRYPTED_PROJECTS_REQUIRED` (decision D2). Browsers declare `SUPPORTED_PROJECT_FORMATS = [1]`: a format-2 project is shown locked with "Update OpenZCAD to open this project", is never loaded, and loads that meet the refusal say so instead of entering repair. Ordinary project and upload traffic does not depend on 0031: the Worker probes for the column once per isolate, and until it exists no statement names it, every project reads as format 1, the opt-in routes answer 503 and `projectsEnabled` is false (the "without migration 0031" cases). No API path creates a format-2 project yet; the flag stays off and migration 0031 needs its own authorization before it reaches production. Evidence: `test/e2ee-project-format.test.ts` (a walk of every project route declared in the Worker router, for every method, against a seeded format-2 row; the persistence guards; sharing refusals; the socket ticket path; D2; the assistant refusal; the preference routes, erasure and migration checks), `test/d1-migration-split.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/ProjectEncryptionPreference.test.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/projectFormat.test.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/projectFormatLoads.test.ts` and `apps/web/src/components/assistant/AssistantPanel.encrypted.test.tsx`. Phase 4, slice 4-7, names, thumbnails, archived exports, measurements and view state of encrypted projects, with no new migration: the project-info envelope keeps its own chain (its header carries its sequence as the version and names the hash of the envelope it replaces, which the Worker computes from what it stores, 409 `E2EE_INFO_MISMATCH` otherwise) and its header may now name committed `thumbnail` and `export` blobs of the project, fenced in the write's own SQL, while garbage collection keeps every blob the current project info names and fences its claim on that info's sequence; thumbnails and exports ride 4-6's blob routes (`E2EE_UPLOADABLE_BLOB_KINDS` now lists both, as migration 0034 already allowed). `apps/web/worker/e2eeProjectEnvelopes.ts` serves the format-2 variants of the measurement and workspace-session routes over migration 0033's `e2ee_project_envelopes`: `GET`/`PUT /api/e2ee/projects/:id/measurements` (scoped to the project; the revision stays plaintext and fences writes with 409 `MEASUREMENT_REVISION_CONFLICT` as in plaintext; each envelope names the stored one's hash) and `GET …/view-states`, `PUT …/view-states/:sessionId` (scoped to user and session; the sequence only moves forward, only the device that started a session continues it, a view of a document version the account has not stored is refused, and the 30-day and 20-session limits stay), behind the key gate, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, migrations 0031–0033 (503 without them), the session/erasure write predicate, owner only and format 2 only, checking shape, kind, project, version, no blobs, the current key epoch and a certified signer; the plaintext routes are unchanged and keep refusing format 2. `@openzcad/crypto` adds the `measurements` and `view-state` body codecs (`MeasurementsBody`, `ViewStateBody`), which the decode worker now dispatches, and `ContentBlobOptions.blobId`. In the browser, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/encryptedProjectInfo.ts` reads the project info verified and pinned by sequence and hash (refusing an older or forked one, and one step past the pin that does not name the pinned hash), writes it read-modify-write on its chain (a rename keeps the thumbnail and exports), publishes captured thumbnails as thumbnail blobs and archives exports as export blobs listed newest first (at most 100), never as artifacts; the shelf decrypts names and thumbnails, the File menu lists and downloads exports through the decode worker and `TopBar.tsx` no longer links the plaintext download for them. `replacedEnvelopes.ts` gives measurements and view state the plaintext clients' shapes as signed envelopes sealed and opened through the store's kit (listed signer, epoch binding, kind, project, sequence), pinned per revision or session in `versionPins.ts`, which now also records the project-info hash (older pins read with none). 4-5's and 4-6's local-only refusals of thumbnails, exports, measurements and view state are lifted. Also fixed, 4-6's gap: a write the account refuses with 409 `E2EE_BLOB_UNKNOWN` stores each lost blob again from this device's source store (matching checksum and length) under its id and a fresh key, then retries once. Evidence: `test/e2ee-project-info.test.ts` (Node devices with the in-process MLS engine over the real Worker and SQLite: the info chain and a refused rollback and fork; a mismatched link; a rename keeping thumbnail and exports; a thumbnail stored only as an encrypted blob, never an `artifacts` row, decrypted by a fresh page; garbage collection keeping what the info names; an export round trip; measurements conflict, reconciliation and a refused rollback; view state decrypted on another device, device continuity, version fence, the 20-session limit and a swapped envelope left out; the re-upload on `E2EE_BLOB_UNKNOWN` and its refusals; flag, migration and owner gates), `packages/crypto/src/bodies.test.ts`, `envelope.test.ts`, `fuzz.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-decode-jobs.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-content-blobs.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/versionPins.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/cloudThumbnail.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/projectFormatLoads.test.ts` and `test/e2e/encrypted-content-blobs.spec.ts` (shelf names and thumbnails decrypted in a fresh browser). Crypto context fix, no migration and no flag change: the `rejoin-psk` AES-KW wrap context is now `{userId, projectId, keyEpoch}` instead of `{userId, projectId, epoch}`. It bound the MLS group epoch, which restarts at 0 after an identity-authorized group reset (decision D9, slice 4-10), so two generations' rejoin PSKs of the same MLS epoch shared one context and a server could serve the replaced group's wrap as the new group's. The key epoch (decision D10) is the monotonic project-key generation the wrap is already stored and looked up by (`e2ee_project_keys.key_epoch`), so the context now names exactly the row; the PSK's MLS epoch is still `keyEpoch - keyEpochBase`. Every wrap and unwrap goes through `projectGroups.ts` (`wrapEpochKeys` for group creation, Commits and External Commit rejoins, invitations' own-key posts, device-removal rotations and resets; `newestRejoinPsk` for rejoins). No rejoin-PSK wrap is stored while `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED` is off. Evidence: `packages/crypto/src/vectors.test.ts` (new fixed `rejoin-psk` vector), `packages/crypto/src/wrap.test.ts` (a wrap relabelled with another group generation's key epoch for the same MLS epoch does not open; the old `epoch` field is refused) and `test/e2ee-device-removal.test.ts` (after a reset the server serves the replaced group's MLS-epoch-0 rejoin wrap as the new group's: the rejoin fails with `authentication-failed`, and before this change it succeeded; the genuine wrap rejoins; the rotated wraps open under the new account key with the key-epoch context and not under the removed device's). Phase 4, slice 4-9, the collaboration room relays ciphertext for encrypted projects: `ProjectCollaborationRoom` gains a format-2 mode (`packages/cloudflare-adapters/src/e2ee-room.ts`). It keeps the latest signed document envelope and up to 20 older ones, each with its account log record, under keys of their own (`room:e2ee:meta`, `room:e2ee:v:<hash>`; never `room:latest`), reads only envelope headers through an account seam the Worker supplies (`apps/web/worker/e2eeRoom.ts`, the room class the Worker now exports), and never parses or normalizes content. It orders a submission by its header's previous-version hash and document version: one that builds on the head under the group's current key epoch (read from `e2ee_project_groups`) is written to the account through slice 4-5's own `writeVersion` (same version log, fences, signer check and quota; named saves through the revision path with a revision id, never `storeCollaborationCheckpoint`), bound to the session the socket or request was admitted under by its token hash, and only then acknowledged (`e2ee-ack`) and relayed (`e2ee-document`); one that builds on an older version gets `e2ee-conflict` with the latest and, when the room still holds it, the base, as ciphertext; an older key epoch is refused (`epoch-stale`). The account's log stays the one chain: the room adopts the account's head whenever it moved outside the room. A plaintext frame to a format-2 room and an envelope to a plaintext room are refused (`format-mismatch`), and a room that holds plaintext never turns encrypted. The 4-4 Commit route and 4-5's document and revision writes notify the room (`/_internal/e2ee-notice`), which broadcasts `e2ee-epoch` or the new head. The Worker opens the room for a format-2 project only to its owner and only while `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED` is on (ticket, socket, HTTP and the 4-1 format gate); members stay refused until Phase 5, and the room still needs `PROJECT_PERSONAL_SYNC_ENABLED` (or sharing) as before. In the browser, `useCollaboration` takes an `encrypted` codec (`apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/encryptedRoomCodec.ts`, loaded lazily) that seals with `encryptedProjectStore`'s encrypt/sign path in the decode worker and reads every relayed head as a current head (the account's log from the pin, author, leaf authority, epoch binding, decrypt and validate in the decode worker, pin forward); the hook's merge, kept-version and refusal semantics stay Phase 1's (#831/#841), and "Keep my version" seals the kept document on the room's head. `App.tsx` connects owners of encrypted projects, treats a room-acknowledged version as the account's, and lets autosave take over when the room stops carrying edits. Plaintext rooms are unchanged. No new migration; the flag stays off. Evidence: `test/e2ee-room.test.ts` (two Node devices of one owner with the in-process MLS engine against the real Worker, its room class and SQLite: relay through the account with pins moving; a stale edit merged from the returned base and latest; an old-epoch head refused after a Commit whose epoch the room broadcast, landing after catch-up; a named save through the revision path; owner-only and flag-off refusals; a tampered relayed head refused with the pin kept), `test/collaboration-room-socket.test.ts` and `test/cloudflare-adapters.test.ts` (ordering, conflict with base, epoch refusal, plaintext and envelope mismatches, epoch and head notices, storage keys, the HTTP named save, ledger retention), `apps/web/src/lib/useCollaborationEncrypted.test.tsx` (envelopes only, merge and reseal on the latest, reseal after an epoch move, kept versions, refusals, plaintext rooms never load the codec), and `test/e2e/encrypted-room.spec.ts` (two browser contexts of one owner under personal sync in the built app: each browser's real socket reaches the Worker's room class over the encrypted routes' SQLite through Playwright socket routing; an edit on one browser is written to the account by the room, with that browser's autosave standing down, and adopted live on the other; room storage and the account hold ciphertext only). Phase 5, slice 5-6, room access for members of end-to-end encrypted projects, behind the same flags as slices 4-9 and 5-2 to 5-5 (`E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED`, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, and for members the sharing rollout, as a plaintext project's members need it); no new migration. The Worker now opens a format-2 project's room to its members as well as its owner (ticket, socket, HTTP and the 4-1 format gate: `encryptedRoomHeaders` in `apps/web/worker/e2eeRoom.ts`, `projectFormatGate.ts`, the room's `refreshTicketAccess`): every member reads the encrypted head, editors submit, viewers only receive. The room re-checks the role on every submission from the Worker's mirror of the MLS roles (`canAuthor`, then the account write's own `requireEncryptedProjectAccess` in the same statement batch as the version), and each browser checks its own role from the MLS roles of the epoch it seals under before it seals anything (`requireOwnEditingRole` in the store's room seal); a refusal there (`EncryptedRoomCodecError` with `readOnly`) turns the hook read-only, and the page re-reads the role (`refreshEncryptedMemberRole`). Role notices: after a Commit (the existing `epoch` notice) or a member's SelfRemove proposal (a new `members` notice from `POST …/mls/proposals`) the room reads each member socket's role again (`refreshEncryptedRoles`): a demoted editor loses its lease and hears `lease-lost` (`role-changed`), so its browser stops submitting; a removed or departed member hears the same and its socket closes (1008); a promoted viewer gets a fresh `e2ee-state` in its new role. Edit leases (decision for review): the room's lease semantics apply as before (granted, renewed and used only while the role allows writing; enforcement follows `PROJECT_EDIT_LEASES_ENFORCED`), except that in an encrypted room the leases of different users no longer exclude each other, so the owner and its editors edit live together; the encrypted room orders envelopes by the version each names and never merges, a stale one comes back as a conflict its browser merges, and the account write is fenced on the version it builds on, so exclusivity bought nothing but a member falling back to account autosave and meeting a conflict with every relayed head. Plaintext rooms keep one author at a time. 4-9's gap is closed: a room write the account refuses with `E2EE_BLOB_UNKNOWN` comes back as `blob-unknown` with the missing blob ids, and the browser stores those blobs again from its own copy (`restoreBlobs`, as 4-7's account save does) and seals and sends the version once more, once. In `App.tsx` an encrypted member joins the room under the sharing rollout, its autosave follows the account version the room relays, and a role the room reports moves the page's role guard at once. Plaintext rooms, tickets and sharing are unchanged. Evidence: `test/e2ee-room.test.ts` (Node devices of an owner, an editor and a viewer with the in-process MLS engine against the real Worker, its room class and SQLite: a member editor's version relayed through the account to the owner and the viewer, signed by the editor, with non-exclusive leases; a viewer refused by its own browser and, forged, by the room, and the mirror read on every submission; a demotion by the owner's Commit stopping the editor's socket at once and its browser sealing nothing more, and a promoted viewer told and writing; a removal and a departure closing the member's socket; a lost blob stored again from the device and the version resubmitted; members opening the room only while encrypted projects are on), `test/collaboration-room-socket.test.ts` (members read the encrypted head in their role, leases do not exclude each other, an editor writes as itself, a viewer's submission never reaches the account), `apps/web/src/lib/useCollaborationEncrypted.test.tsx` (read-only on an own-role refusal and on the room's role notice, the blob repair and its single retry), and Playwright `encrypted-room-members.spec.ts` (Alice, Bob as an editor and Carol as a viewer, each browser's real socket reaching the Worker's room class over the encrypted routes' SQLite: Alice's and Bob's edits relayed live to the other two through the room, Bob's written by the room with his autosave standing down, Carol held read-only, room and account holding ciphertext only). Phase 5, slice 5-9, share-link crypto, package only (no app, Worker or migration change; nothing is enabled). `packages/crypto/src/share-links.ts` defines the ADR's share-link authority chain, documented byte for byte in the package README: the identity-signed share delegation ("OZS" v1: owner identity hash, sequence, issue and expiry times of at most 30 days apart, and the delegated owner device signing keys in full), the per-link share-authority statement signed by a delegated owner device ("OZA" v1: project, link id, share key hash, mode, owner identity hash, sequence, at most 7 days of validity, signer key hash and the author key hashes allowed to publish snapshots), and owner-signed link grants ("OZL" v1, carrying the key epoch they were made in) and permanent tombstones ("OZY" v1; first drafted as "OZX", which the solo group reset of slice 4-10 already used). Every format is canonical, size-bounded and refused with typed errors; signatures use their own labels. `verifySharedSnapshot` runs the visitor's check in the ADR's order (owner hash, delegation and expiry, delegated signer, statement signature, expiry and share key, named author, snapshot signature and project), `verifyShareLinkSet` checks a document's active set (owner signatures through a caller resolver, project and epoch, tombstones seen or carried, tombstones never dropped, editors carry the set unchanged), and refusals are `ShareLinkError`s with a reason. The document body codec gains version 2, written only when a document carries `shareLinks` (version 1 bytes and every existing vector unchanged; share snapshots never carry the set). The `share-version-key` wrap now binds the snapshot's key epoch too (no code had written one), `shareKeyHash` and `openShareKey` tie a per-epoch `share-key` wrap to its grant, and `sharedProjectSnapshotDocument` moved to `@openzcad/document-core` with a re-export from `@openzcad/persistence`, so callers are unchanged. Evidence: `packages/crypto/src/share-links.test.ts` (expired and not-yet-valid delegations and statements, a delegation over 30 days, forged and undelegated signers, another owner or link, an unnamed or forged snapshot author, another project or kind, tombstoned links, grants for another project or a later epoch, dropped tombstones, editor changes, non-canonical bytes), `vectors.test.ts` (a share key hash, a delegation, a statement, a grant and a tombstone with recorded signatures re-checked with raw WebCrypto, a version 2 body and the `share-key`/`share-version-key` wraps), `fuzz.test.ts`, `bodies.test.ts`, `wrap.test.ts`, `packages/document-core/src/shared-snapshot.test.ts` and `test/shared-snapshot-reexport.test.ts`. Phase 5, slice 5-11, the visitor page of share links to end-to-end encrypted projects, behind the same flags as slice 5-10 (`E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED` and the sharing rollout), with no new migration. The app reads `#share=<token>.<key>.<owner>` as an encrypted link (`shareLinkFromHash`, `isEncryptedShareLink` in `projectShareLink.ts`) and every plaintext `#share=<token>` exactly as before (same parser, route, session, Tweak workspace and device copy). An encrypted link is opened by `sharedSnapshotLoader.ts`, loaded only then: the anonymous `GET /api/e2ee/share/:token` (a 404 reads as "no longer available", as for plaintext links), `openSharedSnapshot` in the ADR's order (owner hash, delegation and expiry, delegated signer, statement and expiry, named author, snapshot signature and project), the share-version key opened with the share key and the snapshot decrypted in the decode worker (no MLS), then every file the document uses (imports and pictures) fetched from the new anonymous `GET /api/e2ee/share/:token/blobs/:blobId`, decrypted in the decode worker against the snapshot body's reference and checked against the document's checksum before it reaches the device source store; nothing opens until all have. Refusals are plain words by reason: an expired statement or delegation reads "The owner hasn't recently confirmed this link…", one from the future asks to check the device clock, a wrong key or owner in the fragment says the link is incomplete or changed, a forged snapshot or a file that does not match says it could not be verified. The workspace follows the verified statement's mode, never the server's: a view-only link pins View (Tweak locked, Make a copy on View's rail), a tweak link lands in Tweak as plaintext links do. Make a copy for a signed-in visitor of an encrypted link asks where (`SharedLinkCopyDialog`): into the account, saved as any device project is (slice 4-8's adoption: encrypted under the visitor's keys when the account encrypts new projects, decision D2, and an ordinary project otherwise, which the dialog says), or on this device only; signed-out visitors and plaintext links keep the device copy. The blob route goes through every gate of the snapshot route (format 2, not trashed, link live with a statement and snapshot, the owner's sharing switch, the 0040 and content-blob tables) and serves the owner's ciphertext only for a committed blob of the link's project that the stored snapshot's header names, else one opaque 404. Both anonymous encrypted routes are rate limited per client address with the existing public pattern (`enforceApiRateLimit` over `publicRequestBucket`; 60 snapshot and 300 blob reads a minute) once the flags serve them, and not at all while they are off; plaintext share routes are untouched. Evidence: `test/e2ee-share-link-visitor.test.ts` (the real Worker over SQLite with slice 5-10's Node devices: view and tweak links opened with the statement's mode even when the server claims another, the STEP file arriving through the blob route; a revoked link answering null and its blobs 404; expired statements and delegations refused as not recently confirmed, a future one as the clock; a wrong key or owner in the fragment, a forged snapshot, altered blob ciphertext and a swapped blob refused with nothing stored; the blob route refusing unnamed blobs, other links' tokens, malformed paths, flags and the owner's switch off; rate limits per address with plaintext links unlimited; Make a copy into an opted-in account, encrypted, its file a new blob of the copy that a reloaded device decrypts, and into another account as an ordinary project), `test/project-share-link.test.ts`, `SharedLinkCopyDialog.test.tsx`, and Playwright `encrypted-share-links.spec.ts` (the owner imports a STEP file into an encrypted project and creates a link from the dialog; a signed-out visitor in a fresh context opens it, the model rebuilds from the decrypted blob, View is pinned and only the anonymous encrypted routes are requested; a device copy opens Build; a visitor eight days ahead sees "The owner hasn't recently confirmed this link"; after the owner revokes it, the visitor sees the refusal), with `encrypted-share-links-owner.spec.ts` moved onto the shared `encrypted-share-links-fixtures.ts`. Phase 5, slice 5-10, share links of end-to-end encrypted projects, server and owner flow, behind the same flags as slices 5-2 to 5-5 (`E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED`, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, the sharing rollout), with migration 0040 `e2ee_share_links` (links with their token hash, mode, share key hash, owner fingerprint and newest share-authority statement; `e2ee_share_keys`, the share key wrapped per key epoch; `e2ee_share_snapshots`, the current snapshot per link, inline or in R2; `e2ee_share_delegations`, each account's newest identity-signed delegation). A link is `#share=<token>.<key>.<owner>` (base64url of 32 bytes each; the token is a labelled hash of the share key and the server keeps only the token's hash; `shareLinkFragment.ts`). The owner creates a link by an owner-signed grant at the current key epoch in a new version whose body carries the set (body version 2), then registers it with the share key wrapped under that epoch's project key, a statement and the first snapshot (`createShareLink` in `shareLinks.ts`); revoking signs a permanent tombstone in place of the grant in a new version, after which `DELETE …/share-links/:shareId` drops the snapshot, key wraps and statement at once and keeps the row so the id and token are never reused. The tombstone, not that one request, decides: while the server still lists a link the head tombstones, the owner's browser sends the `DELETE` again (`forgetRevokedShareLinks`) on the next revoke, whenever the sharing dialog loads the links and after a removal, revoking an already tombstoned link only re-sends it, and the dialog keeps the link listed as "Revocation pending" with the error until the server confirms (tested in `test/e2ee-share-links.test.ts` and `EncryptedShareLinks.test.tsx` with a forget that fails after the tombstone committed). Statements last at most seven days with a sequence that rises per link (the Worker refuses anything else), name the signing keys of the owner's and every current editor's devices from the pinned device lists, and are reissued when missing, within two days of expiry, when the author set changed (an editor removed, a device revoked or added) or when their signer left the delegation, and at once after a removal through slice 5-5's `onMembersRemoved` (`shareLinksAfterRemoval`). The delegation (identity key; 30 days, the owner's certified devices) is renewed with a passkey or the recovery key from the sharing dialog when missing, stale or in its last week, and at once on an owner device removal with the identity key the removal opened (`renewShareDelegationAfterRemoval`, `pageDeviceRemoval.ts`). Every saving editor republishes one share-snapshot envelope per active link of the head's set: the shared projection in a version 1 body, a fresh version key wrapped under the project and share keys (`wrapShareSnapshotKey`, `versionId` the decimal document version), signed by its device, under the current key epoch (the share key is wrapped again for it first). Every head the store accepts, from the account or the room, runs `verifyShareLinkSet` against the parent's verified set (the full rule for a direct parent, tombstones only across skipped versions), `authorIsOwner` from the epoch's roles and the tombstones this browser saw, which the version pins persist (`shareLinkSet.ts`; integrity reason `share-links`); writes carry the set unchanged, and a page save naming the head a share-link version replaced lands on top of it. When a link names an earlier owner identity every link is tombstoned and reissue is offered (`maintainShareLinks`). The anonymous `GET /api/e2ee/share/:token` serves the snapshot envelope with its signature, the visitor's wrap, the author's key, the statement, the owner's delegation and identity key, only for format-2 projects that are not trashed, while the owner's sharing switch and the deployment's flags are on (one opaque 404 otherwise); the plaintext `#share=<token>` route and its links are untouched and keep refusing format 2. The sharing dialog's encrypted branch mounts `EncryptedShareLinks` for the owner: create, copy, revoke, "Active until …" or "Needs confirming", Confirm sharing, and reissue after a removal or an identity change. Without migration 0040 the share-link routes answer 503, the anonymous route 404, and encrypted writes, plaintext links, erasure and purge work as before; purge deletes the rows and R2 bodies, erasure deletes the delegation and, with `all`, clears user ids. Evidence: `test/e2ee-share-links.test.ts` (Node devices, the in-process MLS engine and the real Worker over SQLite: a link created, read anonymously and verified end to end with `verifySharedSnapshot`, ciphertext only and no plaintext route; a stale page save landing on the share-link version; owner-only routes, rising sequences, stale epochs and viewers refused; a revocation removing the snapshot, the editor's browser remembering the tombstone, and a hostile editor's version re-listing the grant refused by the owner's page and by a reloaded one; an editor dropping a link refused; an editor's save republishing two links under its own key; removing an editor issuing a statement without its devices, its snapshot refused until the owner republishes, and reissue; an editor's revoked device dropping out; no delegation leaving the link unstated, expiry of statements and delegations, and a delegation of uncertified devices refused; an owner device removal renewing the delegation; forged and stale snapshots, statements, owner keys and wraps refused by the visitor; an identity change retiring and reissuing links; migration 0040 missing; erasure and purge), `EncryptedShareLinks.test.tsx`, `ProjectSharingDialog.test.tsx`, and Playwright `encrypted-share-links-owner.spec.ts` (the owner confirms sharing with the recovery key, creates a link from the dialog, the anonymous route serves a snapshot that verifies and decrypts, revocation leaves a 404, and autosave keeps working). Since the first share-key wrap per key epoch wins, `POST …/share-links/:shareId/keys` takes one only signed (`signShareKeyWrap`, "OZW") by a device the caller's newest device list certifies and fences the insert on that list's head, so a revoked device of an account that stays an editor cannot plant a wrap no member can open, and a member's browser takes the newest wrap that opens to the grant's key rather than failing on one that does not (tested in `test/e2ee-share-links.test.ts`, share-key wraps).
