Forgetting a link is permanent, so `DELETE …/share-links/:shareId` also needs, besides the owner account, a signature (`signShareLinkForget`, "OZF") over the project, the link and the current key epoch by a device the owner's newest device list certifies, fenced on that list's head, so an owner device removed while its session stays signed in cannot forget the links (tested in `test/e2ee-share-links.test.ts`, forgetting a link: a removed owner device refused, a signature for another link or epoch refused, a device-list change in the batch changing nothing). Phase 5, slice 5-7, device removal across shared end-to-end encrypted projects, with no migration and behind the same flags as slice 4-10 (`E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED`, `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, migration 0036); plaintext projects and the flags-off paths are unchanged. `GET /api/e2ee/revocation-cutoffs` now lists every encrypted project the caller owns or is a member of (shared ones marked `member`), so slice 4-10's removal participant (`deviceRemoval.ts`) signs one identity-signed cutoff statement naming owned and shared projects alike and rotates each: in a shared project the member's browser commits a Remove of its own identity's revoked leaves (the MLS policy allows it for editors and viewers) with its account's wraps of the new epoch under the new account key and a new dedupe secret, and the other members post their own wraps when they process it; a removal that goes offline part-way is finished by another of the member's devices before its next save or catch-up, as for owned projects. Co-members consume each other's statements (served by slice 5-2's `GET /api/e2ee/projects/:id/revocation-cutoffs`): the MLS directory, key archives and version authorship refuse the removed device's later Commits and versions and accept its earlier ones; `revokedCommitter` judges a co-member's removed device by that member's cutoffs (device ids are not unique across accounts); and a member whose browser was behind processes a co-member's pre-rotation Commits (from the since-removed device, or External Commits of its siblings with a pre-revocation rejoin PSK) with that device counted as active for that Commit only (`DirectoryInput.activeAt`), placed by the signed cutoff, instead of stopping or needing wraps of the current epoch it cannot have; an unsigned revocation never qualifies. Writers in a shared project refuse to write (409 `E2EE_MEMBER_ROTATION_PENDING`) while a co-member's removed device still holds a leaf or its signed cutoff names a later key epoch, since only that member's devices can rotate it. The slice 5-2 gap is closed with a usable, fail-closed rule: in a shared project (co-member lists given) a revoked device of any account that no signed statement names the project for gets `UNSIGNED_SHARED_CUTOFF` (every epoch so far) in every member's directory instead of refusing it, so the project stays usable for everyone, versions of such a device are still refused, and only that account's External Commits by rejoin PSK are refused (its further devices join by Welcome) until a statement names the project; neither slice 4-10's derivation from the account's own wraps (others cannot check it) nor a guess of 0 is used in shared projects, and solo projects keep slice 4-10's refusal. Decision D9 stays owner-only and solo-only. The rejoin-PSK wrap context is unchanged by this slice. Evidence: `test/e2ee-shared-device-removal.test.ts` (three accounts' Node devices with the real account-key flows, `removeDevice` and its participant, the in-process MLS engine and the real Worker over SQLite: a member removes a stolen phone across two of the owner's projects, the statement names both and is served to the owner and a viewer, the rotated project's Remove and wraps are checked, the owner's pinned and fresh browsers and a viewer who was behind read the phone's earlier version, the owner does not write while the other project waits for its rotation, the phone's later Commit is refused by the Worker and, served anyway, by the owner's directory, and its later version by every browser's authorship; a removal that goes offline after one rotation is finished by the member's desktop in an owned and a shared (viewer) project, after which the owner writes on, and the member cannot reset the shared project), `test/e2ee-shared-unsigned-cutoff.test.ts` (the real invitation flow with an invitee that removed a device before it was invited: its devices join by Welcome, the owner's reloaded and fresh browsers read and write and commit, the invitee's tablet's rejoin by PSK is refused before it reaches the server, and the invitee's solo project still refuses as before) and `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/mls/directory.test.ts`. The Worker counts a cutoff as done only once a Commit sent by its own account lands in the cutoff's key epoch or later, or the group was reset at or after it, since another member's unrelated Commit can take the predicted epoch while the revoked leaf is still in the group (`test/e2ee-shared-device-removal.test.ts`). Every member Commit states the device-list head its browser built it from (`E2eeMlsCommitRequest.deviceList`), and the Worker refuses one whose head is no longer the account's (409 `E2EE_DEVICE_LIST_CHANGED`) and fences its batch on that head, so a Commit from a page that loaded its account view before a removal on another device cannot land in the cutoff's epoch without the Remove and stand in for the rotation; the browser drops the refused Commit and reloads its account view, and its next Commit removes the revoked leaf (`test/e2ee-shared-device-removal.test.ts`). Phase 4, slice 4-4, solo MLS groups, the server sequencer and the project key store: D1 migration `0032_e2ee_project_groups.sql` adds `e2ee_project_groups` (one group per project: generation, key-epoch base, current epoch, the newest GroupInfo and its epoch, and the signed epoch-zero GroupInfo every join archive starts from), `e2ee_mls_commits` (primary key `(project_id, epoch)`, so the first Commit for an epoch wins; kinds `commit` and `external`; immutable except that erasure clears the sender), `e2ee_project_leaves` (leaf keys and their version-2 certifications), `e2ee_project_keys` (per user and key epoch, the project key and the rejoin PSK AES-KW-wrapped under a named account key, decision D1) and `e2ee_key_archives` (one signed `key-archive` envelope per key epoch), with project and user cascades, erasure fences on every insert and update, and a trigger that keeps the sequencer from moving backwards. `apps/web/worker/e2eeProjects.ts` serves `/api/e2ee/projects/:id/` `mls/group-info` (GET; POST creates epoch 0), `mls/commits` (POST; GET `?after=`), `mls/leaves`, `keys` (the caller's own wraps only) and `keys/archives`, behind the key rollout gate, the off-by-default `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, migrations 0031 and 0032 (503 without them; `projectsEnabled` in `/api/e2ee/config` now also needs 0032), the session/erasure write predicate, owner only (members 403, others 404) and format-2 projects only; a Commit, the `epoch = epoch + 1` update fenced on the epoch it names, the new GroupInfo, its wraps, its key archive and, for an external Commit, the joining leaf land in one D1 batch or not at all, a late Commit gets 409 `EPOCH_TAKEN` with the winner (also when two submissions for one epoch both passed the read and the second batch finds the fence moved or fails on the Commit log's primary key), and the sender must be a device the caller's newest device list certifies (a read-only approved device holds no leaf, decision D4). In the browser, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/projectGroups.ts` creates the group (leaf certified from epoch 0; safe to retry: a project that already has a group is joined, local state whose creation never reached the server is discarded through the per-project `MlsProjectSessions.discardSession` / `MlsSessionStore.deleteSessionIfCurrent`, a lost answer counts as created, and a device that lost the creation race joins the winner's group by external Commit; in-group state the server's leaf registry never saw is never caught up or committed from), joins an owner's own device by external Commit with the newest account-wrapped rejoin PSK and a join archive decrypted from the key archives (decision D3; the leaf certified from the group's current epoch, a stale leaf of the same device replaced), commits with the new epoch's exports read from a confirmed sealed copy of the pending session (`MlsProjectSessions.previewPending`), rebases a Commit or rejoin that lost its epoch, catches up in order, and recovers a crash by confirming a stored pending Commit equal to the server's winner, discarding it otherwise and resending one nobody ruled on; key epochs are the group's base plus its MLS epoch (decision D10), and older keys open from the account wraps (through `openAccountKeyChain` for older account keys) or a later archive, cached in `unlockedProjectKeys.ts` until key-session teardown. Ordinary traffic does not depend on 0032: only those routes, project purge and account erasure name the tables, each after a cached schema probe. No API path creates a format-2 project yet; the flag stays off and migration 0032 needs its own authorization before it reaches production. Evidence: `test/e2ee-project-groups.test.ts` (two Node devices of one owner with the in-process MLS engine over the real Worker routes and SQLite: create, external-Commit join while the first device is offline and catch-up; a duplicate epoch refused and rebased; two concurrent Commits for one epoch, the loser answered with the winner and rebased; creation retried after an offline or refused upload, a lost answer, a lost creation race, and state a crash left behind; a crash after acceptance confirmed on reload, a beaten pending Commit discarded, an unruled one resent; a stale GroupInfo rebuilt and inconsistent ones refused; a read-only device reading keys but refused a leaf by client and Worker; wraps never served to another user; non-owner, plaintext, trashed, flag-off and missing-migration refusals; a tampered archive refused; purge and erasure; and ordinary traffic, purge and erasure with migrations only through 0031), `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/projectGroups.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/mls/projectSessionsRejoin.test.ts` and `test/d1-migration-split.test.ts`. Phase 1 finished, the account stops checking document contents: saves, revisions, copies, adoption and room submissions keep only the envelope and the parts the account reads itself (document structure and size, a schema no newer than this deployment, the undo-history shape, imported STEP and mesh source references, and checkpoint metadata), and no save path runs `normalizeDocument` any more. Sketch provenance, reference dimensions, text budgets, drawings, Objects folders, reference images and the content schema are checked only by the browser (`assertStorableDocument`) before it writes and again before it opens, so the account stores what it receives unread, as it must once it stores ciphertext. Evidence: the server-acceptance cases in `test/api-routes.test.ts`, `test/schema-admission-storage-matrix.test.ts`, the schema compatibility suites (`test/schema-carrier-compatibility.test.ts`, `test/schema-folders-compatibility.test.ts`, `test/schema-projection-carriers.test.ts`, `test/schema-distance-mode-compatibility.test.ts`) and the collaboration validation suites, which now assert the browser refusal and that the account and the room store the document unread. Phase 8 leftovers (slice 8-C4, from #914, #918 and #925): the sweep keyset-pages lapsed stagings, at most ten pages of fifty per run, so stagings whose owners may still migrate no longer hide a withdrawn one behind them; the moves of a multipart upload to `completed` (after R2's own completion, and the reconciliation of a lost response) carry the lease the session was opened under, staging or decrypt, so a takeover during R2's completion answers the stale holder 409 `E2EE_MIGRATION_LEASE_LOST` and releases its session at once for the new holder; and a decrypt staging's part reservation carries its decrypt lease, so a stale request after a takeover reserves nothing (`test/e2ee-project-migrations.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-project-migration-reverse.test.ts`). ADR-024 Phase 8, slice 8-C5b-1: the MLS archive verifier (`packages/mls/crate/src/archive.rs`, `session.rs`) replays from an owner-signed checkpoint as well as from the epoch-0 GroupInfo. A checkpoint is a GroupInfo of epoch K > 0 whose signer is in its tree, is listed as owner by its roles, and is certified by a device that is unrevoked in the current directory, so a stolen owner leaf cannot backdate a checkpoint. Replay seeds removals from the checkpoint and requires exactly records K+1 through the target, and the final removal set must still match the joining GroupInfo exactly. Any other signer is refused as `checkpoint-invalid`. Empty records are now an error rather than a slice underflow in `complete_rejoin`. The committed `packages/mls/pkg` was rebuilt by the reproducible `mls-wasm` workflow on the pinned Linux toolchain (`build.sh --check`); only the WASM and `BUILD.json` changed, not the bindings. Evidence: seven new `checkpoint_tests` in the crate (join from a checkpoint keeps an epoch-2 removal only the checkpoint carries; wrong ranges, a non-owner signer, and a revoked or uncertified owner leaf are refused, with full replay still working; an owner checkpoint that omits removals gets `removal-history-mismatch`; a checkpoint from another chain is refused; rejoin and `complete_rejoin` work from a checkpoint), plus `packages/mls` vitest (142) and `test/e2ee-*.test.ts` (627) against the rebuilt WASM. Producing checkpoints in the app (C5b-2) and replaying them for old-version authorization (C5b-3) follow. Phase 8, slice 8-0, the outside-review packet: `docs/reviews/adr-024-e2ee-review-packet.md`, linked from ADR-024's Delivery phases. It gives the reviewer the target (a frozen review tag and commit, still to be set), the in-scope code, the verified pins (`mls-rs` =0.56.0, `mls-rs-core` =0.27.0, `mls-rs-codec` =0.7.0, `mls-rs-crypto-rustcrypto` =0.22.1, `wasm-bindgen` =0.2.126, Rust 1.96.0, cipher suite 0x0002, the committed WASM's hash), how to reproduce `packages/mls/pkg` with `packages/mls/build.sh --check`, the threat model and what product copy may and may not claim, every key flow and MLS integration point with code pointers, the tests, vectors, fuzzing and browser runs, the known limitations and open deferred findings, questions for the reviewer, and the intake rule that any open P0 or P1 finding blocks the default-on flag and the held deletion PR (8-5). Writing it found one divergence from the ADR, recorded there for the reviewer: a fresh device receives no contacts head in the approval exchange and accepts the server's newest identity-signed contacts list as verified. Checkpoint replay (slice 8-C5b) is marked in progress. Docs only; no flag, migration or code changed. The official configuration supports encrypted projects and opt-in migration for accounts selected by the private E2EE canary secret. Global keys, default-on, reverse migration and the plaintext freeze remain closed. Settings describes key setup and existing-project migration separately. Account selection is not committed; activation requires the qualified production version, paired recovery evidence and current-head protected delivery. The official beta offers opt-in encryption to every authenticated account through `E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED=true`, alongside the existing project and migration flags. Key setup, encrypting new projects and migrating existing projects remain separate user choices. Default-on encryption, plaintext freeze and reverse migration remain disabled; the outside review and remaining L11 acceptance are still open. `test/e2ee-cohort-config.test.ts` verifies availability without a canary selection and preserves coverage of the scoped fallback when global keys close. Existing Worker route coverage in `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts` verifies unauthenticated reads are refused when global keys are enabled. The public privacy page describes the all-account opt-in rollout, with `privacyPolicyPage.test.ts` tying that statement to the official flags. No account identifiers, migrations or dependencies are added. Device approval retains account-scoped browser observations across retries and Settings navigation, and uses an owned request snapshot through completion. Regression coverage in `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts` exercises record changes, equivalent encodings, concurrent starts, lost responses, session teardown, certification retries and both approval forms. No migration, rollout flag or deployment configuration changes. An accepted comparison response can be resumed after its reply is lost, including from the refreshed approval list. A Chromium regression completes approval without repeating the response request; changed response fields and comparisons from an earlier unlocked session remain refused. After sign-out or session lock interrupts a response before delivery, a still-requested approval can restart under the current unlocked session. The retry retains the original request binding, responder key and nonce, including when a stale requested read hides an accepted response. Account-key regressions cover both teardown paths, changed bindings and earlier-session responded/revealed refusals; a Chromium journey locks, rechecks the session and completes the unchanged comparison through Settings. The requesting device retains its comparison through confirmation and browser reload. Confirmation names the displayed request, so a stale tab cannot confirm a replacement request; interrupted operations preserve their original lifetime guard. Older unbound comparison requests must restart while their device keys remain stored. Coverage includes persisted metadata and atomic updates in browser and native-adapter stores, response retries, equivalent encodings, and requesting-device reload. Empty-head approval follow-up: an empty grant transfers no contacts-list trust. An existing nonempty recipient refuses it without altering its pin or pending evidence; a fresh or matching-empty recipient can finish device certification but remains unconfirmed. Local setup retains its own trusted empty root. Both stores cover each empty representation and confirmation flag. Real stale-setup approvals cover recipients before and after their first contacts load: a withheld verified prefix stays seen with no key usable for wrapping. Nonempty conflicts and retries retain local/server requests; exact nonempty heads and v1 grants retain their established paths. The store and real-flow regressions fail before the guard and pass after it. Local contact comparisons now check the contact pin store's result. If another inspection accepted a newer device-list head meanwhile, the comparison reports the race rather than reporting success without a local verification marker. A real second-device enrollment regression verifies the refused stale comparison and successful retry after inspecting the newer head. Contact-pin writes compare the full own-list snapshot (main pin and pending approval) in the same memory operation or IndexedDB transaction as the contact update. A replacement identity from an inspection invalidated by a newer approval cannot poison the contact pin before the request reports a fork. Store and real-flow regressions cover directory-response and pre-write races, each changed authority field, absent pins, preserved contact state and a subsequent honest response. Authenticated pending heads now impose a minimum usable contacts history, including when the approver was unconfirmed. Prefix and empty responses are refused, as are transactional advances below a concurrently added pending head. A second incompatible approval cannot erase unchecked evidence, and confirmation of the same pending head never downgrades it. Retained contacts books are checked against the current main and pending pins before exposing verified contact keys, including approvals arriving during a directory read. Matching complete ancestry consumes the obligation normally. Older assertions accepting a prefix below a pending head now require a visible fork refusal while retaining both heads. Outgoing device approval also refuses unresolved pending contacts evidence, so an intermediate device cannot pass an older confirmed head to a new browser. A real three-browser flow verifies the refusal, retained evidence and unsigned grant, then resolves the full list and successfully approves the new browser, which rejects the older server prefix. Main-anchor concurrency follow-up: `acceptList` supplies the exact observed identity, sequence, hash and confirmation state (including an absent pin), checked atomically before either identity replacement or confirmation inheritance. A concurrently replaced main pin refuses a stale advance without touching its evidence. Memory and IndexedDB regressions cover each changed field and absent/created pins; real contacts flows cover a concurrently confirmed fork, preserved pins, a refused fork retry and a successful same-chain retry. Concurrent approval follow-up: consuming a pending approval now requires the exact sequence, hash and confirmation state checked by the caller, compared within the pin-store transaction. An unobserved or changed head refuses the advance without changing either pin or pending evidence; future heads remain pending. Both memory and IndexedDB stores and real contacts flows cover matching and forked concurrent heads, retry, each changed token field, and continuation beyond the approved sequence. The contacts UI offers confirmation whenever its list is unconfirmed, including empty or entirely untrusted server responses. Approval polling forwards errors to the settings error handler and stops polling a deterministic invalid-grant conflict while retaining the request. Rendered regressions cover both list forms and both scan and already-confirmed compare polling paths (`EncryptionContacts.test.tsx`, `EncryptionEnrollment.test.tsx`). Review follow-up: `finishApproval` refuses a same-sequence, different-hash contacts head when the pin store returns false. It reports the conflicting lists through the existing encryption-flow error, keeps the local pin and pending approval, and neither cancels the server request nor reports success. `test/e2ee-contacts.test.ts` reproduces the conflict through real device approval, checks the preserved state, and checks that retrying still refuses it; existing matching-head and v1-grant flows remain covered. ADR-024 conformance, contacts heads in device approval (verified pins): a device approved by another device receives the approver's newest contacts head inside the authenticated approval exchange, and a device enrolled with only a passkey or the recovery key treats every verified pin as seen until another of the user's devices confirms the head or the user re-verifies the contact. The device approval grant ("OZG") gains version 2, which appends the approver's contacts head (u8 flags: head present, confirmed; u32 sequence and 32-byte entry hash), signed with the grant; version 1 grants from older builds still decode and approve, and a grant without a head keeps version 1 bytes (`packages/crypto/README.md`). The approved device pins the head (`contactPins.ts`: the list pin records whether it descends from a trusted start, and `EMPTY_CONTACTS_SEQUENCE` marks a list trusted while empty), so `acceptList` refuses an older signed list; setting up or replacing an identity pins its empty list as confirmed, a server-shown list on its own never confirms a pin, an approval head never replaces an existing pin (a browser with no pin under the identity adopts it; otherwise the head, older or newer, is kept as `pendingApproval`, and only a list that extends the pin and holds that exact entry once it reaches it lends the grant's confirmation, while a list holding a different entry there is refused as a fork, #936 review), and list pins stored before this read as confirmed. On an unconfirmed device `loadContacts` and `inspectContact` report verified contacts as seen (`ContactsBook.listConfirmed`), so no project key is wrapped to them, until a comparison made on that browser (`ContactPin.verifiedHere`) or another device confirms: an already certified device can ask for approval again, which the approver completes as certified with no recovery key or sealed account key, and Settings › Encryption › Contacts offers this as "Confirm from another device". No migration and no flag change. Evidence: `packages/crypto/src/devices.test.ts` (version 2 round trips, signed head, version 1 still read, tampering, relabelling and unknown flags refused), `packages/crypto/browser/scenarios.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/contactPins.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/EncryptionContacts.test.tsx` and `test/e2ee-contacts.test.ts` (real Worker and SQLite: an approved device shown an older signed list refuses it; a recovery-key device shows a contact verified elsewhere as seen with no key to wrap to until the laptop confirms through the approval exchange; re-verifying on that device counts; an older build's version 1 grant still approves and leaves the list unconfirmed; another browser with no pin now sees the verified contact as seen; a phone that signed an untrust and then asked a stale laptop to confirm keeps its newer pin, refuses a list served only through the laptop's head, and keeps Bob untrusted; a laptop confirmed at entry 1 approved by an unconfirmed phone on a longer fork keeps its pin, refuses the fork and still accepts the original chain, confirmed). ADR-024 audit follow-ups retain the off-by-default rollout and production deployment hold. The Commit sequencer packs predicate inputs into one JSON bind while preserving every session, owner/member, epoch, device-list, Add, membership and proposal predicate in its atomic write. Tests exercise 16 role changes plus 16 drops under D1's 100-bind ceiling and a stale-member race. Invitation quota is charged only after a guarded insert in the same batch; concurrent duplicate and final-quota-slot tests preserve refusal and limits. Device-list verification validates malformed/sparse entries as `E2eeError` and captures signed bytes before asynchronous verification. MLS admission uses shared checked-arithmetic replay budgets in native Rust and rejects oversized JS byte arrays before native copies. Record arrays are borrowed and counted before glue marshalling; two-million-entry and sparse-array regressions pin bounded allocation on every admission entry. Raw-WASM memory checks and the scenario matrix retain Welcome rollback and provisional-rejoin retry. The sender-check override is absent from the public session and refused by the production protocol; malicious-sender tests use an explicit test Worker. The committed package is regenerated with the pinned toolchain and manifest. Cross-host reproduction also fixes two environment-dependent inputs: a Git-built CLI's producer suffix and installed rust-src paths. The build requires the exact crates.io CLI version string and normalizes standard library paths, with executable build-boundary regressions and the existing byte-for-byte reproduction check retained. Checkpoint tests drive a real MLS group through 257 epochs, check the production manifest boundary at 255/256/257 and authenticate a suffix from the resulting checkpoint. A separate application Commit tests refresh after owner-device revocation. Signed v2-to-v1 link-set downgrades are refused on pinned, room and supplied-history reads without advancing pins. Staggered SelfRemove tests require a complete member drop or an explicit contention error. Crypto entry exports and exact canary-email near misses are pinned. Crypto/MLS specifications now describe v2 formats, authority manifests, cutoffs and caller-verification contracts. Recovery explains first-sight device-list freshness; desktop documentation distinguishes software Keychain keys and a renderer sign/derive capability from Secure Enclave or per-use consent. Raw MLS transfer limits, cutoff-head freshness and global freeze-bit visibility are explicit. Real Keychain ACL/signed-host qualification remains in the existing manual macOS workflow; browser and Linux checks do not prove those platform properties. No production flag, migration, dependency pin, merge protection or automatic deployment authorization changes. Isolated staging also exposed retained-save identity drift: encryption gives snapshots new signed versions, so migration now remaps checkpoint versions from the verified staged journal, including interruption/resume. Backup export uses the canonical saved version when its plaintext cache is stale. Migration and backup regressions preserve strict identity validation and saved geometry. Automatic runs use the controller's own run history to leave manually paused/failed projects stopped. The check runs after acquiring the Web Lock and refreshing, including a stop made while the automatic caller waits. Rows merely discovered after reload still resume staging and unfinished purges, even when Settings already listed them; explicit manual retries retain their normal behavior. Both paused and failed manual-run regressions reproduce against the preceding code. The automatic setup/unlock attention path opens the rendered Settings dialog as well as selecting and persisting its Encryption section. A failed migration-module download also opens Settings to expose its reload instruction, fenced to the initiating account. Rendered browser regressions start with Settings closed and verify both default-on setup and the blocked-download error; each fails against the preceding implementation. Preference-key lifetime follow-up: public preference reads/writes own and finally zero a key copy before their first await; automatic migration additionally owns a copy across its complete load-to-save prerequisite. The upload policy reacquires the cache after importing the preference module. Cache replacement still zeroes its old entry but cannot turn these in-flight operations into zero-key signing or authentication. Nine new regressions fail on the preceding code, then pass with the fix; coverage includes authentic and zero-key-forged records, cache replacement during pin/API I/O, migration continuity and cleanup on success/refusal/error. ADR-024 Phase 8, automatic sign-in migration: the authenticated browser checks all four availability gates before loading key or migration code. With default-on, it opens the existing mandatory recovery-key setup/unlock/certification UI when necessary, verifies and pins the MAC-protected new-project preference as enabled, then runs the existing journaled migration controller once per signed-in page session. An untrusted preference requires explicit true-only repair in Settings. Unfinished cutover purges are included after reload. Existing member-readiness, journal resume, Web Locks, progress, errors and pause controls remain in use; readiness notifications never restart a manual run or paused automatic run in the same session. Settings reads now have their own cancellation scope, so closing Settings preserves an independent background run. Account/key-session teardown still invalidates every scope and zeroes cached secrets; the automatic run's abort signal also prevents a delayed Web Lock callback from starting work. Migration staging refuses a different account returned by the session endpoint and is fenced to the initiating key session. Focused coverage includes closed gates, setup/certification continuation, real MAC verification and preference pinning before migration, forged-setting refusal, true-only repair, discovery errors, unfinished purges, manual runs, delayed account responses, delayed locks, in-flight cancellation, independent view scopes and cross-tab secret teardown. This does not activate production flags, run a production migration, implement the legacy read/freeze/removal slices, or replace the required outside crypto review. ADR-024 Phase 8-3 adds a browser legacy plaintext reader. Account and migration head/revision loads negotiate a raw storage frame on the existing URLs, with JSON compatibility for older Workers. The Worker relays inline D1 text or the compressed R2 snapshot and copied-source alias metadata without parsing CAD. Authenticated asset reads accept only exact project-scoped storage keys. The browser bounds downloads and decompression, checks lengths and checksums, hydrates imported payloads, normalizes history and reconciles source aliases. Existing account format refusals, project authorization, missing-revision semantics and the frozen-plaintext write gate remain enforced. Real-Worker regressions disable the old CAD loader and exercise inline/R2 heads and revisions, migration, malformed data, missing/corrupt assets, scope/format refusals and frozen reads. Browser API tests retain older JSON responses and transfer measurements. Chromium journeys open both storage forms and reconstruct an imported mesh. Types, focused lint and the complete production build with bundle budgets pass. Server pipeline retirement and production activation remain held for outside-review clearance; this slice does not complete the remaining historical MLS authorization work. Review regressions preserve raw-storage negotiation on older native hosts through the existing query protocol, without a Rust protocol change. Asset fetch and response-stream failures retain their original transport errors; HTTP asset failures preserve their status, while invalid/checksum-mismatched content retains the account-copy-unavailable classification. Migration reads use the same distinction. macOS verification remains deferred. The static-preview API fixture now retains successful creations (including prepared-document overrides) and hydrated saves, and serves those account heads on reload. Raw-read negotiation exposed the previous missing API, which had relied on the preview server's HTML fallback. The existing error assertions remain intact: fillet/reload journeys, both raw-reader journeys, six object journeys and all three unit-bearing text reopen journeys pass. Asset HTTP errors also preserve bounded JSON error codes and details, so finalizing encryption between the frame and asset reads takes the existing encrypted-format fallback immediately. The race regression failed before that correction and passes afterward for both account and migration reads. The holder import/reload/export regression explicitly removes its account copy before reopening, checks the exact expected missing-copy response, and still proves the restored local copy exports without a cloud upload. Copied-source alias metadata shares the importer's 128 MiB source limit, independently of document/frame download limits. Boundary regressions accept sources above the document-write cap through 128 MiB and refuse larger ones. Complete-backup profiling found repeated device-directory reads within one encrypted verification and serialized cloud metadata waits. A head, historical save or replaced envelope now carries its current verified directory through authority/archive binding checks; head link checks use the same directory and revocation cutoffs. No directory result is cached across reads. Backup catalogs, measurements and workspace sessions read concurrently after the verified head, while whole-project staging stays sequential with the existing memory bounds and atomic ZIP commits. Regression coverage tightens the real shared-reader request budget and preserves later-read removal/fork refusal; deferred metadata gates and failed-batch coverage protect scheduling and completeness. The affected group, authority, project-info and sharing suites are included in CI (`test/e2ee-member-lists.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/dataExport.test.ts`, `test/unit-ci-files.json`). Encrypted asset uploads and contact discovery now respect the actual D1 runtime. Upload creation and staged part reservations verify the identifier or token returned by their fenced writes, rather than D1's trigger-inclusive change count. Session, role, lease, erasure and quota predicates remain in those same statements. Contact discovery uses smaller membership and invitation CTEs within D1's compound SELECT ceiling, retaining one snapshot, the global ordering/cap and the existing email-visibility rule. `test/e2ee-d1-runtime.test.ts` applies the real migrations to an isolated workerd D1/R2 instance through the installed Wrangler. Regressions reproduce the former upload rejection, staged-part reservation failure and contacts query error; they also cover upload retry, fenced-out writes, ciphertext round-trip and reservation cleanup. Existing contacts and migration suites retain their authorization, privacy, quota and stale-lease coverage. Local staging with synthetic data resumed an interrupted imported-STEP migration after restoring disk-backed state, completed cutover and plaintext purge, and reopened the model. Rollout configuration, migrations and the Remus pin are unchanged. Production activation and outside-review clearance remain separate requirements. Share-link visitors now have a dedicated loading and failure page instead of falling through to Parts. Incomplete links are recognized as share destinations without sending a capability request or restoring an unrelated local project. Unavailable links explain possible expiry, revocation or model removal; other failures retain encrypted verification and owner-confirmation guidance. Retry keeps the link, Back to parts explicitly leaves it, and a failed replacement offers a return to the previous model. Browser regressions cover plaintext and encrypted unavailable links, malformed fragments, reload, network retry, cancellation, replacement races, encrypted-invitation navigation and retained local projects. Existing encrypted owner/visitor verification and publication contracts remain in force. An existing workspace stays mounted, hidden and inert beneath the destination; regressions verify that an in-flight assistant reply survives a failed share replacement and the return to the previous model. Phase 8 browser prerequisite: when `/api/e2ee/config` reports `defaultOn: true`, project creation and adoption choose the encrypted store before sending any plaintext request, independently of an unset, off or unavailable beta preference. Locked keys and inconsistent availability fail through that encrypted path without plaintext fallback. Plaintext-source duplicates and branches into an account requiring encryption report the existing `ENCRYPTED_PROJECTS_REQUIRED` refusal before sending their document; encrypted-source copies retain their existing path, and a device copy can be adopted encrypted. Settings shows required encryption as checked and unavailable for opt-out, with an explanation, while the flag-off preference flow and its MAC/pin verification remain intact. Regressions in `projectFormatLoads.test.ts` and `ProjectEncryptionPreference.test.tsx` fail before the change and cover those cases. This does not implement automatic sign-in migration or activate the rollout; those remain separate Phase 8 requirements. Phase 8 rollout follow-up: deployment preflight recognizes the merged `E2EE_DEFAULT_ON` Worker flag, requires migration to be enabled first, validates string values, and rejects enabling reverse migration alongside default-on. The self-host example keeps the flag false. The shutdown instructions disable default-on before authenticated opt-outs while keeping key and project routes available. `E2EE_PLAINTEXT_FROZEN` remains explicitly unimplemented and accepted only while off. The documentation distinguishes the implemented Worker gate from the unfinished browser rollout and mandatory outside review; no flag is enabled or remote migration run. Evidence: `test/deployment-config-validator.test.ts` covers the ordered rollout, default-on prerequisites, invalid values, reverse conflict and shipped configuration. Phase 8, slice 8-1, the Worker side of end-to-end encryption by default, behind the new off-by-default `E2EE_DEFAULT_ON`, held off until the outside review is confirmed done. `isE2eeDefaultOn(env, email)` (`packages/cloudflare-adapters`) is true only with the flag on and migration open to the account (`isE2eeMigrationEnabled`, so the key routes, encrypted projects and migration are all available first); `/api/e2ee/config` reports `defaultOn: true` only then and once migration 0042 is installed. Where it applies, the Worker refuses every plaintext project create, adoption and duplicate with 409 `ENCRYPTED_PROJECTS_REQUIRED` whatever the account's "Encrypt new projects" preference (extending decision D2's check in `apps/web/worker/index.ts`); refuses creating a plaintext share link or invitation on the caller's own format-1 project with 409 `E2EE_DEFAULT_ON` (a code in `packages/shared`; an encrypted project keeps its own sharing refusal and another account's project still answers 404); and a preference record turning "Encrypt new projects" off answers 409 `E2EE_DEFAULT_ON` and stores nothing, while turning it on still works (`e2eeProjectPreferences.ts`). The reverse path is closed as if `E2EE_REVERSE_ENABLED` were off for the account (`isE2eeReverseEnabled` now also requires default-on off): no decrypt start, cutover or staging write, and `reverseEnabled` is not reported. The hourly sweep abandons every decrypt staging of such an owner whatever its lease, fenced on the row still being a decrypt staging, and drops its staged plaintext as the existing abandon does (`e2eeProjectMigrations.ts`). With the flag off nothing changes. No D1 migration; `wrangler.jsonc` sets `"E2EE_DEFAULT_ON": "false"`. No flag was enabled and no migration was applied remotely. Evidence: `test/e2ee-default-on.test.ts` (real Worker over SQLite: `defaultOn` reported only when every dependency holds and migration 0042 is installed; plaintext create, version 1 and 2 adoption and duplicate refused with no, an off and an on preference, storing nothing; the opt-out refused and the opt-in stored; a decrypt start refused; the sweep abandoning a leased decrypt staging and leaving an encrypt staging; plaintext share links and invitations refused with no row stored and no email sent; and, with the flag off, creation, adoption, duplication, the opt-out, sharing and a leased decrypt staging as before), `test/e2ee-project-migration-reverse.test.ts` (a real decrypt staging with staged files, abandoned by the sweep under default-on with its plaintext dropped and the project still encrypted) and `test/cloudflare-adapters.test.ts`. Phase 8, slice 8-C3: in the packaged macOS app, encrypted content blobs (anonymous share-link and authenticated member downloads) use `desktop_blob_stream_open`, `_read` and `_cancel` instead of the buffered bridge's 50 MiB cap. The native allowlist preserves credential routing, the bounded pump holds at most 16 chunks, reads return about 1 MiB of raw bytes, the total ciphertext ceiling is 168 MiB, and connect/read idle timeouts are 30 seconds. Four streams run at once; further WebView requests wait for a slot. Renderer timers cancel bodies unread for a minute while that WebView remains alive. Size, progress and idle limits remain enforced by the existing callers. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/desktopBridge.test.ts` (including a 60 MiB blob, cancellation, failures, member downloads and queued admission) and Rust tests in `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/desktop_auth.rs` for route classification, pump bounds, limits, short bodies and release. No new flag, migration or dependency. Review follow-up: native blob admission checks an already-aborted signal before taking a slot or registering a waiter. `desktopBridge.test.ts` holds all four streams open, aborts a fifth request during the Tauri import await, and verifies prompt rejection with no native open or leftover waiter. The native stream lifetime after a WebView reload remains a deferred P2: stale entries are reclaimed only when another native open checks their age; independent native expiry or renderer teardown cancellation still needs implementation and native validation. Performance follow-up: bulk account downloads reuse their complete retained-revision catalog and overlap independent metadata reads; encrypted head/log verification reuses current verified membership inputs within one read, with a cold shared-project request budget and a next-read withdrawal regression (`test/e2ee-member-lists.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/dataExport.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/projectBackupDeviceCheckpoints.test.ts`). Post-revocation repair: Settings encryption-state changes invalidate the page clients that captured the previous account view, so sharing, invitations and project reads reopen against rotated keys without a reload; late superseded openings cannot overwrite fresh verification state (`apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/encryptedProjects.test.ts`). Review hardening for PR #983: authorship cutoffs use the same verified device lists as signature decisions. Retired page views refuse captured and in-flight file/document access; unlocked rechecks lazily reopen direct file readers and reconnect encrypted rooms while preserving unsent edits. Regressions cover lock races, superseded openings, historical signatures, current cutoffs, and fresh reads without a page reload. Transient opening failures preserve retryable encrypted file access while the account remains unlocked; a later key lock still retires that reader and clears its file references. Bulk backup catalogs follow the document snapshot so a concurrent save cannot be omitted from the archive. Historical MLS authorization (ADR-024 8-C5b, #959). A stateless Rust/WASM verifier replays the public transcript with the project policy, checks the independently supplied owner, and returns the exact target roles, roster, GroupContext and tree. Historical directory views apply each device's cutoff independently and exclude certifications from future epochs. Archive joins and rejoins use the same policy replay. Unverified decoded rosters are only inputs to certification verification, never authorization results. The app establishes owner intent independently at creation and from verified recipient-bound invitations. An owner-signed manifest binds immutable roots, generation bases and periodic owner checkpoints; each account transfers its accepted state with an account-key MAC, certified-device signature and CAS. Durable local pins reject rollback, withdrawal and same-sequence forks. Historical head, revision, log and replaced-envelope checks require the exact author device in the replayed epoch roster. D4 readers need no MLS leaf. Replay also reaches the independently accepted terminal context and tree, refusing a policy-valid competing fork that retains a demoted editor's role. Unknown-time revocations count only within that exact authenticated prefix; live admission remains fenced and document-author signed cutoffs remain independent. V2 identity-authorized resets hash-bind the prior roots and terminal state, retain old generations, and permit cold solo recovery after the last writer is revoked. Wrong-identity approvals mutate neither group nor local authority pins. Native regressions cover exact historical rosters, checkpoint equivalence, wrong owner/project, signed unauthorized role changes and foreign removals, per-device cutoff boundaries, and the authenticated-prefix boundary. Real Worker/SQLite regressions in `test/e2ee-authority-*.test.ts` cover owner intent, MAC/signature/CAS pins, D4 history, forks, revoked checkpoint fallback and cold identity recovery. Replay is bounded to 8,192 records and 64 MiB. The Linux MLS workflow runs the native tests before reproducing committed WASM; candidate-wide build and CI verification are required before merge. The incompatible pre-0044 experimental archive transition is explicitly gated: 0044 aborts before deployment while any MLS group remains, including reset generations and unfinished staging. Transactional migration regressions preserve prior data and schema. Self-hosting documents retaining the prior release and an owner-consented verified client-side transition; there is no automatic encrypted-to-encrypted backfill. Authority, archive, network and session outages propagate as retryable/original errors through log and leaf verification; only actual verification refusals become integrity failures. Cold shared replacements with independently established owner intent can verify the archive's certified owner manifest and full policy replay even when their only account-authority signer was revoked. The retired record supplies no roots or terminal pin; after joining, the certified replacement CAS-publishes its own accepted state. A real shared-project regression refuses absent/wrong owner intent, reproduces the earlier dead end, then verifies rejoin, record replacement and a subsequent cold D4 read. Production activation remains off pending written outside-review clearance; macOS verification remains deferred. Migration stages canonical rebuild data instead of requiring legacy original import archives. Embedded STEP and mesh payloads, import commands and undo/redo fragments become the existing checksum-addressed source form in the staging copy. Referenced sources can recover from a verified local copy; unavailable or mismatched required data still prevents cutover and preserves plaintext. Source work is reused by kind/checksum/length across imports and save states, and independent initial reads overlap. Regression coverage includes fresh device readback, historical-only imports, source recovery/refusal, one download and encryption for duplicate content, pause/resume and unchanged exact-kernel mesh/topology after source conversion (`test/e2ee-project-migration-engine.test.ts`, `test/project-source-preparation.test.ts`). No key protocol, schema version, deployment flag or production migration is changed. Automatic migration retries config discovery on browser reconnect, removes its listener and aborts pending discovery on account disposal, and checks all four server gates before importing the optional key and migration code (#945). Enabled-gate chunk failures retain a reload instruction through profile saves; closed gates and older servers leave Settings closed. Verified by controller cancellation regressions and the ten Encryption settings browser journeys. Production activation remains off pending outside review; macOS qualification remains deferred. Phase 7, slice 7-2 fix: a refresh of Settings › Encryption › "Encrypt existing projects" no longer overwrites a row the page's own run reported after that refresh began. `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/projectMigrations.ts` numbers every update to a project's row; a refresh notes the number when it begins and, when it answers, keeps the current row of any project updated since (listed or not) instead of what it found then, and an answer from a refresh that began before one already applied is dropped whole. Before, the panel's refresh on mount, whose status still said "staging", could answer after a run had stopped a project past its cutover and turn it back into "Paused", hiding that it was cut over with its purge unfinished. The finishing and cut-over discovery of #922, #924 and #927 and its caps are unchanged. Evidence: `apps/web/src/components/ExistingProjectEncryption.test.tsx` ("a refresh answered after a run reported on the project": a status held by a refresh begun before the run, answered after its cut-over stop; and the newest refresh, begun while the run was under way, answered after it; both fail without the fix), and the test "stays listed as finishing, and its retry resumes the purge" made deterministic (it clicked the main button before the mount's refresh had listed anything, while it was disabled, so the run never started; the panel's buttons are now clicked once enabled). No flag, migration or server change. The final candidate also merges current main's desktop host and renderer changes, so Linux verifies their combined source with the expiry fix. Full required checks and native compilation/tests must pass again on that integrated head before the protected stack merge. Linux run on cbfa5c0a completed green: native host formatting, strict Clippy and all 30 Rust tests passed, including both expiry regressions. Its prerequisite step finished in under a minute. The integrated head retains the exact manifest, lockfile and checksum files from main's already-approved Remus update #863, avoiding an unintended stale-pin rollback; full checks rerun on that head. macOS platform/distribution verification remains deferred. Native CI recovery: the preceding run completed formatting, strict Clippy and all 30 host tests, then timed out after prerequisites had consumed 27 minutes. Prerequisite installation now has a ten-minute step limit, bounded request retries/timeouts, noninteractive package configuration and no recommended packages. The overall native job allows 45 minutes. Security-workflow and desktop-release privacy tests pass; a green native run remains required before merging. Proposed follow-up to ADR-024 slice 8-C3: native blob streams have a five-second reaper independent of renderer commands. Unread streams expire after sixty seconds (within the next tick), dropping their bounded queues and aborting their pumps. The task retains only a weak reference across sleeps so native state teardown releases the table. Tokio's monotonic clock is used for both admission and expiry. Timer regressions cover an abandoned full queue, renewal by reads, a pending reader waking when its pump is aborted, and task termination after state release. Formatting and an independent read-only review pass; native compilation and execution are pending. The new `desktop-native.yml` PR check compiles and tests portable host logic on Linux with Rust 1.96.0; it does not exercise WKWebView, Keychain or macOS packaging. This evidence does not mark native expiry verified or the E2EE plan complete. ADR-024 Phase 8-C2 owner-device removal now refreshes share-authority statements immediately after renewing the account delegation, using fresh project clients bound to the post-removal account view. It republishes snapshots under the remaining device before refreshing authority, including snapshots the removed device signed. A failed publication still removes the revoked signer from authority and reports the failure. Ordinary sharing-dialog upkeep republishes missing snapshots, so opening it retries a transient publication failure. Owned projects continue independently when another project is unavailable; unchanged server statements remain due for ordinary upkeep after a failed refresh. Integration coverage verifies a link whose statement the removed device signed becomes usable again without a document save or sharing-dialog open. Production encryption remains off pending outside review; this does not complete the remaining checkpoint, historical authorization or legacy-reader work. Paired D1/R2 recovery tooling inventories committed object references, checks stored and logical checksums, authenticates encrypted snapshot readback and refuses production/source restore targets. Disposable restore checks actual provider bindings, table contents, object bytes and HTTP/custom metadata. Operator setup, key custody, retention and application qualification boundaries are documented in `scripts/recovery/README.md`; regression coverage is in `test/recovery-snapshot.test.ts` and the Python recovery suite. No rollout flags or production deployment configuration changes. ADR-024 Phase 8-4 adds the deployment-wide E2EE_PLAINTEXT_FROZEN gate, default off in both deployment configurations. The Worker refuses legacy document, sharing, measurement, workspace-session and upload writes before body parsing; persistence and existing room sockets independently fence plaintext mutation. The server-marked encrypted-blob transport remains usable for single-part and multipart uploads, including migration staging. Reads, erasure, access revocation and shelf organization remain available. Reverse migration closes and the sweep abandons its staging. Preflight requires global keys and default-on; self-host documentation covers activation and recovery order. Focused Worker, storage, socket, encrypted-upload and configuration regressions verify the gate. Production activation remains off pending outside review and migration/legacy-reader qualification. This slice does not retire the plaintext pipeline or complete L11. Phase 0 prerequisite: a privacy policy page, the precondition ADR-024 sets for any public end-to-end encryption claim. `apps/web/public/privacy.html` is a static page (no script, nothing from another origin, no sign-in, nothing added to the app bundle) served at `/privacy` by Cloudflare's default asset HTML handling, and linked from Settings › Privacy & data and the email sign-in form (`privacyPolicyHref`: the same-origin path in browsers, `https://zcad.app/privacy` in the desktop app). Every statement was checked against the code: accounts (email, derived account ID, hashed 10-minute codes, hashed 30-day session cookie `__Host-openzcad_session`, Turnstile with the client IP, keyed-hash rate limits), standard projects readable by the server versus encrypted projects (opt-in per account during the beta, possibly not yet available; what is encrypted; ADR-024's plaintext metadata, including signer account and device per version, device lists and contacts), keys and recovery (non-extractable browser keys, macOS Keychain on desktop, no server-held recovery key, lost keys mean lost cloud data, local copies remain), standard and encrypted share links (fragment keys never reach the server), collaboration rooms, the assistant (the exact request inventory from `consentCopy.ts`, the configured provider named as today's, per-project consent on encrypted projects), Glimpse analytics levels, Cloudflare email, processors, retention, deletion routes and the desktop app. Operator Esau Engineering LLC, contact help@zcad.app and Michigan governing law come from the maintainer; the effective date is the one placeholder left, and `apps/web/src/lib/privacyPolicyPlaceholders.test.ts` ("FILL IN BEFORE MERGE") fails while any bracketed placeholder remains, so the page cannot ship unfinished. Wording chosen where the code could not answer is listed in `docs/legal/privacy-policy.md`. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/privacyPolicyPage.test.ts` (renders signed out, all sections, no overclaiming, figures tied to the constants and `wrangler.jsonc` that set them, both asset configs serve `/privacy` without the Worker), `apps/web/src/lib/privacyPolicy.test.ts`, the new cases in `apps/web/src/components/SettingsPage.test.tsx`, and `test/e2e/privacy-policy.spec.ts`. The desktop app follows these links through the native `open_external_url` command (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/external_links.rs`), because WKWebView silently ignores `target="_blank"` without a new-window handler: `ExternalAnchor` stays a plain new-tab anchor in browsers and, on desktop, asks the shell to open the page, which hands `/usr/bin/open` only an exact URL from a three-entry https allowlist (the privacy policy and the two assistant provider data-policy pages). Evidence: the Rust allowlist unit tests, `apps/web/src/components/ExternalAnchor.test.tsx` (including a check that every desktop-routed link is on the Rust allowlist) and `apps/web/src/lib/desktopBridge.test.ts`. ADR-024 cross-client comparison (8-C5a): the encrypted store computes the full 40-digit project state code from the verified saved version hash, document version and signing member's identity fingerprint. Account reads, new projects, saves, incoming room heads and acknowledged own room writes retain that code. Historical reads do not replace the current code. The crypto-free status store transfers it to the top-bar project information and sharing dialog, with outside-channel comparison guidance; locked keys, integrity refusals and key-session teardown hide it. An older asynchronous completion cannot restore it after teardown. Plaintext projects show none. Navigation invalidates pending reports for the project that closed, including late integrity errors. Reopening offline cannot expose a code resurrected by an older request; a fresh successful verification restores it normally. Room reports also require the retained project when they start. Disposed rooms skip queued decoding before and after codec loading, covering frames whose callbacks only begin after navigation and a codec download that ends after navigation. Explicit account loads may still prepare the next project. Comparison digests complete before advancing accepted-head pins. Local head publication is monotonic, so a delayed older room read or an earlier successful account save cannot replace a newer accepted document/code. Deterministic real-Worker races pause the older digest until a newer room head is installed; both reproduce the prior rollback and retain the newer state after the fix. Real Worker/SQLite coverage compares the same head across owner, editor, incoming room and cold D4 reads, including an editor-signed head and a later historical read. Rendered status/dialog tests cover the full code and lock, failure and teardown transitions; the browser encrypted-project journey checks all eight five-digit groups in the top bar and the identical code in sharing. This remains a manual comparison mechanism, without automatic gossip or a transparency log. Production activation and plaintext-pipeline deletion remain held for written outside-review clearance; macOS verification remains deferred. Retained-save backup repair: encrypted named saves stamp their captured checkpoint at the version actually sealed, preserving older checkpoints and caller-owned documents. Existing authenticated saves with the older one-step envelope version skew export their captured logical snapshot without changing the source or current head. Project, checkpoint and revision identity checks remain enforced; unrelated or larger mismatches still fail. Coverage includes real Worker/MLS saves and historical reads, malformed-identity controls, and a complete bulk ZIP containing the legacy retained snapshot (`test/e2ee-project-documents.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/projectBackupDeviceCheckpoints.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/dataExport.test.ts`). Phase 8, slice 8-6a, self-hosting documentation and config for end-to-end encryption. `docs/SELF_HOSTING.md` gains an "End-to-end encryption" section checked against the code and this ADR: the plaintext metadata the server still sees (the ADR's list) and that backups hold ciphertext plus that metadata, with restores of older backups refused by browsers as rollbacks; requirements (D1 migrations 0029–0043 with 0035 an intentional gap, an HTTPS origin because WebCrypto needs a secure context, the passkey relying party being the exact hostname so a domain move breaks passkey unlock and drops each browser's origin-scoped device keys, leaving the recovery key, and the shipped `_headers` CSP that allows the WASM MLS engine and the module Workers); the flag order `E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED` → `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED` → `E2EE_MIGRATION_ENABLED` → the planned `E2EE_DEFAULT_ON` → `E2EE_PLAINTEXT_FROZEN` (named as future, not read by this release), the optional beta-only `E2EE_REVERSE_ENABLED`, the `E2EE_CANARY_EMAILS` secret, checking `/api/e2ee/config`, and what turning flags off does; recovery and data loss (no escrow, `.openzcad` export plus deletion as the exit, D1 Time Travel and the room's point-in-time recovery keeping migrated plaintext up to 30 days); and a rollout runbook for instances with plaintext projects (keys, projects, owners encrypt from Settings › Encryption › Encrypt existing projects, read-only D1 queries to watch the hourly sweep, default-on and freeze left to later Phase 8 steps after the outside review). `wrangler.selfhost.example.jsonc` lists the three flags closed (`"false"`) with comments giving the order, the reverse path and the planned flags as comments only. `scripts/validate-deployment-config.mjs` (`pnpm selfhost:check`) now refuses an `E2EE_` var name the Worker does not read (naming the closest known flag, so a misspelling cannot stay silently off; the planned `E2EE_DEFAULT_ON` and `E2EE_PLAINTEXT_FROZEN` are accepted only as off), an E2EE flag value the Worker would misread (anything but the strings it parses, including an unquoted JSON boolean, which the Worker reads as off), `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED` without `E2EE_KEYS_ENABLED` (allowed only for the official target, where the canary secret opens it), `E2EE_MIGRATION_ENABLED` without `E2EE_PROJECTS_ENABLED`, `E2EE_REVERSE_ENABLED` without `E2EE_MIGRATION_ENABLED`, and `E2EE_CANARY_EMAILS` in `vars` (`test/deployment-config-validator.test.ts`, which also checks the shipped example and the official `wrangler.jsonc`). No flag was enabled and no migration was added or applied. Review follow-up: the rollback runbook requires every account to clear its authenticated Encrypt new projects opt-in while KEYS and PROJECTS remain enabled, includes a read-only count to verify none remain, and recovers an early shutdown by reopening those routes before retrying. The introductory warning distinguishes operator inability to recover ciphertext from remaining members' ability to export shared projects. ADR-024 Phase 8, browser share follow-up: a rejected account encryption check in the encrypted-link copy dialog displays the error and offers retry, retaining the device-only choice and refusing an unknown account destination. Late results cannot update a closed/replaced choice or cross an account, share session or key-session change. Component and real encrypted-share browser regressions cover failure, retry to verified encryption, the disabled account action and the available device copy without an unhandled rejection. Encrypted share-link creation now requires the owner's sharing confirmation. The sharing dialog refuses to present a newly created URL while its authority or model publication remains pending, and disables copying inactive links. The same readiness check withholds unfinished links recreated after migration. Regression coverage checks confirmation before creation, pending publication, and copying states. Publication failures retain their retry reason and are distinguished from missing confirmation; real Worker tests verify retry without another confirmation. The real owner/visitor browser journeys exercise confirmation followed by an anonymously verified, decrypted model. Read-only preview diagnosis confirmed an unrevoked link and snapshot with no sharing authority or owner delegation. Preview deployment remains separate. Phase 8, slice 8-C1 (no migration): share-link server hardening. Each R2 snapshot object is keyed by its link and write; replacement reads the previous object key inside its own batch and deletes only that object. Forgetting retains the snapshot row until R2 deletion succeeds and retries leftovers. Snapshot and delegation writes carry the signer's device-list fence. Link registration needs a certified owner's device signature (`signShareLinkRegistration`, OZN), fenced on the device list and live-link limit. Anonymous blob reads retain a snapshot-sized per-address/link allowance, with a spent marker checked before snapshot access and the address's independent fallback budget. Snapshot authorization uses only the header's blob ids, cached per isolate or fetched through ranged prefix reads capped at 60 per address per minute across links. Evidence: `test/e2ee-share-links.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-share-link-visitor.test.ts` and `packages/crypto/src/share-links.test.ts` cover cross-link object reuse, replacement races, failed-delete recovery, device-list changes, registration signatures, a 400-blob load without snapshot body reads and refusal before further R2 reads once both budgets are spent. Review follow-up: anonymous downloads of one project blob are limited to four per address per minute across every share link to that project, independently of the snapshot-sized load allowance and its address-budget fallback. The guard uses an opaque project/blob hash and runs before snapshot or content R2 reads. `test/e2ee-share-link-visitor.test.ts` covers a 4,096-blob allowance, repeated refusals with no storage reads, sibling links, distinct blobs, address isolation, minute reset, fallback and no extra counters after address exhaustion; the 400-blob load remains covered. Further resource-cap review: the blob route's authorization lookup selects only small metadata, omitting both the inline snapshot and the authority author list. An inline body is selected lazily after the guards, fenced to the metadata's project, link and snapshot hash; cache hits select no body. The independent 60-per-address snapshot-read budget covers inline D1 bodies as well as R2 prefixes. Visitor regressions count executed D1 body selections across cache hits, repeated refusals, address exhaustion and cold misses, and force a concurrent replacement to return an opaque 404 without poisoning the cache. Snapshot responses preserve their full authority and envelope metadata. Project, account-project, full-account and retention purge also sweep the exact project snapshot prefix in the configured project bucket, including unreferenced replacement objects. The existing durable cleanup claim blocks restoration and encrypted writes while cleanup runs. Listing or deletion failure and the bounded ten-page work limit retain database rows and the claim for retry; deletion restarts each page without a cursor so removing earlier keys cannot skip later ones. Regressions cover all four deletion entry points, separate and fallback buckets, 1,005-object pagination, unrelated-prefix preservation, storage failures, restoration/publication during the final listing, and bucketless inline-only snapshots. As with other project objects, operators must retain the original storage binding until deletion completes. Counter-cardinality follow-up: only blob ids named by the snapshot allocate a repetition counter or use its load allowance. Unknown ids consume the fixed address budget. A read-only check rejects exhausted repetition buckets before cold inline or R2 membership reads, while the atomic post-membership increment still admits at most four content downloads under concurrency, before charging the link allowance. A 4,096-blob regression created 300 attacker-selected counter rows before this change and now creates none; twenty concurrent named reads serve exactly four bodies and charge exactly four load requests. Cold reads of named and unnamed ids retain the independent sixty-read cap; the spent-link fallback refuses before snapshot access. Valid links retain their independent load allowance after an address spends its fallback. Inline unknown ids, cold-repeat refusal, minute/address isolation and the legitimate 400-blob load remain covered. Phase 8 share-link state is authenticated across skipped document versions. New document prefixes commit to the canonical signed link set; readers check owner-authorized transitions in the signed log and match the decrypted body before accepting a head. Account and room reads use a bounded owner baseline or an accepted pin, including overlapping log pages. The Worker bounds log pages by a conservative byte budget as well as the record limit, reserving frame metadata space for the head even with large blob lists. The Worker retains the necessary ancestry beyond its usual 2,000-head window until an owner refresh. Client verification also splits overlapping windows by the crypto verifier's 64 MiB prefix/hash budget and 4,096-record limit. A signed history exceeding the byte budget reproduces the former refusal and now opens successfully through multiple windows. Legacy byte vectors and historical reads remain supported; directly verified legacy parents can accept unchanged editor successors. Missing proof and format downgrades fail closed. Old builds need an update for new V2 documents. Real-Worker regressions cover fresh/reloaded/skipped readers, room heads, intermediate changes, missing baselines, retention, legacy transitions and concurrent owner saves. Crypto tests cover canonical commitments, immutable headers, malformed prefixes and mismatched bodies. Focused tests, types, lint and the complete production build pass. This is engineering evidence, not outside-review clearance; production activation remains off and macOS verification is deferred. Phase 8-C2 browser follow-ups use an account/project-scoped IndexedDB journal with transactional updates and cross-tab locks. Interrupted registrations retry the same grant, id and project-key-wrapped share key; partial reissues retain both completed replacements and unfinished sources across reloads. Revoked grants cannot be recreated by a saved job, verified source modes govern reissue, and identity retirement preserves current-identity links. Removal notices persist the affected link IDs while the sharing dialog is closed, exclude post-removal grants, and acknowledge only the epoch shown. Empty sets leave no notice for future links. Unit, real-Worker integration and UI regressions cover these failure paths. Notifications deliver their affected IDs after journal completion without waiting for remote upkeep; an unavailable journal retains a transient prompt and warning in an open dialog. Older background responses cannot erase a newer notice, including while the initial list is pending or a later list has no persisted notice. Discarding device keys and confirmed account erasure clear all of that account's journal records; keeping the device preserves them. Cleanup failure keeps the device record for retry. Owner-device statement refresh and fresh-browser share-set ancestry remain separate work; this slice does not complete L11 or activate production encryption. The privacy-policy draft names `help@zcad.app` for support and privacy questions, including data handling, encryption and account deletion, and explains that support cannot view or recover end-to-end encrypted project content. Cloudflare Email Routing is enabled for this address; an Agentmail delivery test was received in the operator's main inbox on 2026-10-25. The contact update preserves the draft's publication review and unresolved operator/legal placeholders. Sharing confirmation and recovery actions remain visible without hover or keyboard focus. Hover-only styling is limited to actions inside sharing list rows, preserving their keyboard and touch visibility. The encrypted sharing browser journey checks the confirmation button's computed opacity before clicking it and covers confirmation, link creation, anonymous verification, revocation and continued autosave at desktop and compact widths. |  |

## Completion and maintenance rules

1. Refresh main, the pinned kernel and open PR ownership before selecting a
   master ID. Use a new isolated branch; one bounded slice and one ready PR.
   A stacked PR names its dependency. Do not silently switch items when blocked:
   record the missing evidence/input against the selected row.
2. Before coding, record the slice, observable acceptance cases, dependencies
   and any schema/API/rollout decision. Preserve exactness/provenance, units,
   verified topology references, offline persistence and one-transaction undo.
   AI and manual edits use the same validated command path. Never weaken a
   refusal, oracle or tolerance to mark progress.
3. Run the root [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) verification gates and the row's browser,
   geometry or performance checks. Exact-head hosted checks are required before
   a merge decision; queued/skipped jobs are not passing. Deployment and manual
   Apple Silicon execution retain their separate authorization requirements.
4. Update this file in the implementation PR: status, shipped subset, evidence
   (tests plus PR/commit), remaining acceptance and changed dependencies. Add a
   linked child ID for a split, never a second untracked plan. Keep detailed
   acceptance specifications linked; do not copy their status tables back here
   as multiple competing versions.
5. Close only the scope proved. Move completed rows into the delivered baseline
   with their stable IDs, or retain a short completion row so links survive.
   Record live acceptance separately from merged source. Revalidate old kernel
   claims upstream and old UI findings on the rendered current app before work.
